The witness looks at the victim; the victim's own view is where they were looking.
    - **Readings.**
        - *"Nearest ... with line of sight"* has no range in the GDD. It's night, and a still from 200 m back is black, so a crewmate over `witnessRange` (60 m) isn't a witness. Nearest by eye-to-chest distance, then by id.
        - *A PUNISH with nobody held* (a car alight, the Sleepers under the engine, the marsh before it has anyone) is of the enemy: the nearest crewmate who can see it, or no bookmark if nobody can (there's no victim whose eyes to use). A marsh that loses its man and finds him again is one punish: one bookmark per enemy per victim.
        - *Which line a still belongs to:* a GRAB's or PUNISH's sits beside the victim's death line if they died within `lineWindowSeconds` (30 s) of it; a GRAB nobody died of gets a line of its own in its place in the night, "Grabbed by the Dragger on the roof of car 3 at km 4. Got away." A victimless PUNISH gets a line the same way ("Punished by the Car Fire ..."). A derailment's stills all sit beside the derailment's line, and the outro's beside the stranding's.
        - *The cap* (`autoCap` 12) keeps by `priority` (derail, stranded, punish, grab: D.13's order, with the outro's single frame ranked with the derailment as the other end of the night), then a still beside a death line before one that isn't, then the earliest. It's applied when the report is written, not as bookmarks are made: a late fatal GRAB shouldn't lose its picture to twelve early ones somebody got away from. To bound what every machine renders and keeps, the host stops taking GRABs and PUNISHes at `recordLimit` (48); the night's end is always taken.
        - *E.5's "each player's peak frame"*: the build's derailment has no per-player shots yet (it's first person, the chase replay, the orbit: note 170), so each crew member's still is their first-person beat, from their eye in the car they rode, `wreck.json bookmarkSeconds` (2 s) in, carried and rolled with the wreck (`DerailSequence.Riding`, which the first beat now uses too). The crew it took, that is: anyone alive or killed in it, not the dead already watching. (Now the stand-in until the film is in: note 203.)
        - *Manual bookmarks* aren't capped in the GDD. `manualPerPlayer` (3) and `manualPerRun` (8) keep the dead's row to one line of the report. One needs someone to follow: a dead player with nobody alive to watch has nothing to bookmark.
    - **The button is intent.** `PlayerActions.Bookmark` (the second action byte's last free bit; `P` by default, "BOOKMARK (DEAD)" in the controls). The host takes it on the press (a held or resent intent is one bookmark), only from the dead, only while the run's under way, of whoever `EarsOf` says they watch (their intent's `Watch`). The dead card shows "[P] BOOKMARK THIS (2 LEFT)", then "BOOKMARKED AT 18:24" for 3 s.
    - **Replication.** Each bookmark goes to every client as it's made (`MessageType.Bookmark`, reliable, its JSON), so each machine takes the still from its own world at the moment, and the report (`RunReport.Bookmarks`, `ReportLine.Marks`) carries the ones shown, so a joiner's run-end screen has the lines even without the pictures. `Double3Json` writes a camera as `[x, y, z]` (a `Double3` serialised as is recurses through `Normalized`). **Protocol 10.**
    - **The stills** (`BookmarkStills`, Game): a GRAB's, PUNISH's or manual one is due on the frame it arrives; a derailment's `bookmarkSeconds` after this machine heard of it; the outro's once the outro's over, from `Views.Stranded` at its last second. The app draws the due frame offscreen through the same `renderer.Render` the `--capture` path uses, with everyone in view but whoever's eyes it is (this machine's own figure included, which the app otherwise never draws), crops it to 16:9 and keeps it at 320x180. It's a stall of one frame per bookmark, which is rare. Interest management means a client sees enemies only near its own player (or whom it watches), so a still of something far from you can miss the creature; it's 520 m (`enemies.json interestRadius`), far past the witness range, so it rarely does.
    - **On the report.** The overlay has no textures, so `Overlay.Image` draws a picture as cells of flat colour, one quad a run of equal colour in a row (16 levels a channel). Each still sits at the right of its line, 32x18 on the 480x270 canvas at half-pixel cells; the dead's own have a row of their own under the lines, 48x27, each with its time and whom it followed. A bookmark this machine has no still for (it joined after) is a dark plate. A full screen of thumbnails is around 20k quads.
    - **Not yet:** the short clips D.15's question 5 wonders about. E.5's per-player peak still and keeping the stills past the run-end screen are note 203's.
    - `BookmarkTests` covers the witness (nearest with a clear line, not the nearer one behind the car), the victim's own view, a Dragger's GRAB and PUNISH (one each, beside the death), a GRAB got away from, the derailment's crew and the outro, the cap and its priority, punish de-duplication and the record limit, a dead player's press over loopback (one per press, of whom they watch, to every client; none from the living; the per-player cap) and the wire. `SpecTableTests.AutoBookmarksMatchGddD13` pins D.13's rows. `BookmarkStillsTests` covers when each still is due and from what camera, the crop, and the report's thumbnails. `dt screenshot --hud --route frontier:7 --report [derailed]` renders the staged report with its stills drawn from their cameras (`--stills dir` writes each on its own).
177. **The derailment film: everyone's own death, the cause card, the skip vote (GDD v1.4 App. E.2-E.5, E.8, E.9, E.11; WP4).** E.5's shot plan after T121's first person and replay. The playtest asked for "experience it first hand, then replay the moment from the third person train view", and E.5 asks for "everyone gets a shot".
    - **The sequence:** first person (4 s) → the chase-view replay (9 s) → the film's cut (`WreckFilm.Cut`): one shot per player, the settle, the cause card → the run's end. **Reading:** E.5's Freeze and Establishing beats are what the first person and the replay already show, so the cut starts at the first player's shot. With no film yet (a client still shooting it when the replay ends), the old orbit stands in.
    - **E.2/E.8, the stream:** the host doesn't stream keyframes. On the derail tick it takes the film's start (`World.Film`, a `FilmStart`): the wreck's bodies and couplings as `Wreck.Begin` made them, the seed, the cause card's words, where on the line it came off, and every living crewmate's world position, velocity, facing, the car they're inside and their name card ("on the throttle", "at the firebox", "on the gun", else `IncidentLog.Place`).
      - It is sent reliably as Brotli-compressed JSON chunks (`MessageType.Film`, like the report; doubles round-trip exactly).
      - Every client shoots the film from it (`World.ShootFilm`, off the frame loop in `NetPlaySession`). The pre-sim is deterministic (DMath, seeded) and looks the ground up from the start's own line hint, so every machine records the same frames and plans the same shots. `FilmTests` pins this frame for frame over the wire.
      - This replaces E.8's ≤250 KB keyframe budget with a ~2 KB start. It's the same trade the netcode makes everywhere: send intent and seeds, simulate alike.
    - **The physics and director:** `WreckFilm` (from the parked film-wip work).
      - The ragdolls are D.9's 11 joints, flung at E.3's ×1.3 with a 2–4 m/s kick and up to 4 rad/s of tumble, capped at 30 m/s. A slow tip-over still throws people sideways, and a bystander gets nothing and a beat to watch.
      - Colliders are the ground, the cars as boxes (hollow for whoever was inside), and each other.
      - Peaks are the highest apex, the longest airtime or the hardest landing.
      - Shots go in rising order of peak score. Each player's camera is the best of E.4 O9's 24 shell candidates that passes O1/O3/O4/O5 over the whole shot, with O10's overhead fallback. Shots ease from 0.25× to 0.1× at the peak.
      - All numbers are in `wreck.json` "film" (E.10's defaults).
    - **On screen:**
      - The film's car poses replace the frames (`DerailSequence.FilmFrames`) and the crew are drawn as ragdolls (`FilmBodies`).
      - O2's fadeable occluders are a cutaway: any car whose shell crosses the camera-to-subject line is not drawn for that shot (`FilmCutAway`, `GreyboxScene.CutAway`). That's how someone tumbling inside a car is seen. **Reading:** the whole car goes rather than a dither fade; the renderer has no per-object fade yet.
      - O12's light rig: a cool key from the camera's side and a warm rim behind the subject in the lamp's colour, or one light over the crew in a wide (`FilmLights`).
      - The name card is a lower third in the clerk's typewriter face: DAVE, then ON THE THROTTLE.
      - The cause card dims the picture and types out the clerk's line: "Consist derailed at km 14, 72 km/h. Took the 45 km/h bend at 72 km/h, 27 km/h too fast. Throttle: Dave. Recovery not scheduled." (`IncidentLog.CauseCard`, E.5's own example in our words).
      - The opera (note 174) fades out under the cause card rather than at the sequence's end. Its hit stays on the replay's moment of derailment, not the last apex: the music starts with the replay, and the tracks' hits come about 3.5 s in.
    - **Skipping (E.5, E.9):** holding Jump sends `PlayerActions.Skip` once the vote counts: after the first player's shot until the cause card, or from `stranded.skipAfterSeconds` (3) into the Stranded outro. The host counts heads each tick. A majority of the session, or the host's own player (`HostSession.HostPlayer`), sets `World.FilmSkipped`, replicated with the votes (`FilmVotes`) on the World record. Clients jump to the cause card (never past it) or the outro's end. The prompt shows the votes. Protocol 10.
    - **Not yet:**
      - E.3's water buoyancy, doorway gaps in the hollow cars, and the extras (stowed bodies and loose items) joining the wreck.
      - O7's other-body cover and O8's mid-shot cut. O9's scoring is simplified.
      - Radio static under the clerk.
      - ~~E.5's per-player peak bookmark~~: done, note 203 (from `WreckFilm.Peaks`).
    - `dt screenshot --film s [--crew n --speed v --route r]` renders a frame of the cut, cards and all, as the app plays it, from a hosted night with bots, and prints the shot list. Tests: `FilmTests` (the start, determinism over the wire, everyone's shot in order inside the caps, the slow tip-over, the majority and host skips), `FilmPlaybackTests` (frames, bodies, cutaway, lights) and `WreckSessionTests` (the film beat and the host's skip landing on the cause card).
178. **The fortress on the radio: the dispatcher's manifest and the clerk's tally (GDD §9; WP9).** §9: at the gate "the yard dispatcher reads the crew out over the radio by name, as a manifest, with the same tone used for the coal". At the terminus "a yard clerk tallies the run over the radio as the cars come through: cargo by the car, bodies by the body, each fee read out flat".
    - **The words** (`Sim.Run.Radio`):
      - The manifest is "Yard to consist. Manifest follows.", then "Crew: Dave." for each player aboard, then the coal, the powder and shot, the cars, and "Gates open. Yard out.".
      - The tally is the cars delivered and the cargo's worth, then cars lost, then each death as "Sam. Body recovered. Fee 350. Refund 263." or "Ana. Body not recovered. Fee 350.", then the mail, salvage, costs, and "Net: 1158. Next.".
      - Both come from the night's own numbers (the report D.12 already sends), so nothing new goes on the wire.
      - **Reading:** "cargo by the car" is one line for the cars delivered and their worth. The report doesn't itemise cars, and §9's point is the flatness, which the deaths read in the same voice carry.
    - **When:**
      - Each client reads the manifest as its mirrored run first leaves the yard, naming the crew aboard then.
      - It reads the tally once a delivered night's report is in. The end screen waits for the tally (`ClerkTally`), as it waits for E.9's outro.
      - A line goes out every `run.json radio.lineSeconds` (1.6 s), typed out as it's read, with the last four on a card at the top of the screen ("RADIO: THE YARD").
    - **The voice:** there's no speech in the stack, so `radio-clerk` is a man's voice reading flat through a cheap set. It's a low buzz through a spoken vowel's formants, broken into syllables at an even, bored pace, over static, band-limited and crushed like `voice-radio`. It's tier 2, like any voice on the set, so the bed ducks under it.
    - **Not yet:** E.9's clerk line over the Stranded pull-back stays as it was (a caption). The dispatcher doesn't wait for the train to be underway past the towers; it reads as the run leaves the yard.
    - `dt screenshot --hud --radio manifest|tally [--radio-at s]` renders the card. Tests: `RadioReadingTests` (the words and the timing) and `RadioCardTests` (the tally holds the end screen, then gives way).
179. **The dead's tools: the queue they can see, a Call Out the crew can hear, the Live Mic (GDD v1.4 App. D.6, D.7, D.10, D.14; WP10).** The Call Out was counted on the host but never reached a client. The queue was the host's alone. Live Mic didn't exist.
    - **The queue (D.6):** the respawn queue rides its own Holdout record (id 0xFFFF): pairs of player id and lobbied, in order. Clients mirror it (`Holdouts.MirrorQueue`). The dead card shows it whole: "QUEUE: 1 SAM   2 YOU   3 ANA (JOINING)" (`Hud.QueueLine`). The living see nothing; roll call stays verbal.
    - **Call Out (D.7):**
      - Each Holdout counts its Call Outs (`Holdout.Calls`, on its record). A client plays each one once as the count goes up, a shout or a bout of banging, from the Holdout, at 60 m with normal falloff, through its walls (`holdout-shout`, `holdout-bang`).
      - Both sounds are adults: D.7 keeps them apart from the Soot Children's child voice. They're tier 3, never an enemy's telegraph, and can't mask one.
      - It never touches the meter. `HoldoutTests` already pins that, and the count is presentation.
    - **Live Mic (D.7):**
      - Only the player waiting in a Holdout can toggle it, with [SPACE] (Jump, which the dead don't otherwise use). It is off by default and off whenever the Holdout isn't lit (freed or released). It's on the record.
      - On the host, a dead speaker with it on is forwarded on the proximity path to every living listener within the proximity cutoff of the Holdout (`VoiceRouting.HearsLiveMic`), and on the dead channel as ever.
      - The client plays a dead speaker's proximity frames from their Holdout (`VoiceChat`: the hard-cut keeps their air voice only while the Live Mic is on).
      - It's never said aloud in the world: `World.Voices` (what the Soot Children mimic) only hears the living, and the meter only counts living voices, so the Gaunt and the Choir never hear it (D.14 "dead silence").
      - **Reading:** no new VoicePath bit. A dead speaker on the proximity path can only be a Live Mic, so the client positions it by the replicated Holdout.
    - **Not yet:** the lobbied (joining) players' own card doesn't show the queue (their wait panel is the link's). Each occupant has one shout and one bang rather than a voice set of several.
    - Protocol 11. Tests: `HoldoutTests` (the toggle is the occupant's alone and goes off when freed; a client mirrors the calls, the Live Mic and the queue), `VoiceRoutingTests` (heard only by the living near the Holdout) and `DeadPhaseTests` (the queue line; each Call Out heard once from its Holdout).
180. **The dead's creature vote, and commendations (GDD v1.4 App. D.11, D.12, D.13; WP11).**
    - **The ballot (D.11):**
      - A dead crewmate (not a joiner still waiting to board) is offered a ballot of `director.vote.options` (3) creatures. They're drawn by weighted roll from what the director could send right then: allowed by tier and edition, with its spawn rule wanting it.
      - The spawn table never holds the Stoker or the Choir (condition- and loudness-triggered).
      - It's drawn once per player per run, seeded by the director's seed and the player's id, so a later death offers the same ballot. An unused vote carries over.
      - They cast it with 1–3 (the hotbar keys: the dead carry nothing). It's locked on submit.
      - It goes to that dead player alone (`MessageType.Ballot`). The living never see it until the run-end screen.
    - **The effect (D.11, D.13):**
      - Each vote multiplies its creature's weight by `perVote` (1.2), to at most `cap` (1.5) (`Director.VoteWeight`).
      - "Within the creature's want tag": after every other weighting, each want's options are scaled back to the want's own total (`Director.WeighVotes`). The vote moves weight between creatures of a want and never between wants, so the Kill / Split / Trust / Cargo shares hold exactly.
      - It changes nothing else. Budget, gates, caps, pacing, cooldowns, once-per-run limits and the Soot Children's roll all run before or around it. `VoteTests` pins the want totals.
    - **The payoff:** when the director charges a creature someone voted for, every dead player is sent a cue (`MessageType.VoteCue`): "THE DEAD CALLED THE CAR HUGGER: PRIYA, SAM" on their card for 6 s, with a cracked hand bell on the dead channel (`vote-cue`, tier 2, flat). The living's clients are never sent it.
    - **The reveal (D.12):** the report says "The dead voted for the Car Hugger: Priya, Sam." (`IncidentKind.Voted`).
    - **Commendations (D.12):**
      - On the run-end screen (after any film, outro or tally), everyone in the session can give one: living, dead, or still waiting to board.
      - The arrows pick who and which of the starter set (`Run.Commendations.StarterSet`, in the HUD's badge order). Space gives it (`MessageType.Commend`).
      - The host refuses one while the run's on, to yourself, to someone not in the session, or a second from the same player. It sends everyone the night's list (`MessageType.Commendations`), and the screen shows them with their badges.
      - On leaving the screen, what you were given goes in your player profile (`PlayerProfile`, `profile.json` in app data or beside `--saves`), not the character. It's social only.
    - **Not yet:** a profile screen to show the tally; bots don't commend. The vote's screen, voting with a headset and the bots' votes are note 202.
    - Protocol 13. Tests: `VoteTests` (the ballot, the lock, the weight and the want totals, the wire with the cue to the dead alone and the report's reveal, the commendation rules) and `PlayerProfileTests`.
181. **Bodies as loot, completed, and the survivor's identity (GDD v1.4 App. C.4, D.8, D.9, Line Plan §12.6; WP12).**
    - **Carrying (C.4, D.9):** a body, a toy, a find or the child is carried like freight: no faster than `carryHeavy` (2.8 m/s), no ladders (`PlayerFlags.Heavy`). Before, only freight was. The exception is D.9's solo remainer: with nobody else alive, a crewmate carrying a body may still take a ladder, at `soloBodyClimb` (0.4 m/s, a quarter of normal), as `PlayerFlags.SoloCarry` (the flags byte's last free bit). A toy doesn't count.
    - **Recovery (Line Plan §12.6, D.2, D.9, §23.2):**
      - A body or a repair kit at rest on the ground outside the walkable corridor is put back on the formation's edge, `train.json recovery.edgeM` (3.5) out from the track, on the side it went off, and left at rest there (`World.Recover`).
      - "Outside" means further than `corridorM` (40, where the line generator keeps drop sides walkable) from the nearest point of the track, or more than `dropM` (15) below the rails.
      - **Reading:** the ground function has no water and no ravine floor below a bridge, so "below the rails" is how a fall off a bridge or into a gorge shows. Never an unrecoverable body, and never an unrecoverable kit: §23.2's "kit lying on the line is never lost" holds even off a bridge.
    - **Identity (D.8):**
      - Tonight's look is unchanged: freed, the occupant comes out as the Holdout's survivor (note 167's art). That look now lasts.
      - The night's report carries everyone's look by name (`RunReport.Identities`), and the campaign keeps it (`CampaignState.Identities`, merged at settlement, the latest freeing winning).
      - The next night, the host matches looks to players as their names arrive (`World.LooksByName` → `World.Looks`) and sends them (`MessageType.Looks`). Everyone's figure is drawn from `Identity.Of`: tonight's freeing, else the carried look.
      - **Reading:** "the player's ID" across nights is their name, since tonight's player ids are only tonight's.
    - **Already in place:** the Gaunt and every other loot-ranker value a body at 5, above a find or freight and below a living child (note 164's reading of "valued at its refund"), and the Followers rank its car.
    - **Not yet:** the survivor pool beyond the two Holdout kinds (one look per kind).
    - Protocol 14. Tests: `BodyCarryTests` (each kind of hand loot heavy and off the ladders; the solo remainer's exception and its pace; recovery off to the side, far off, and down below the rails; a body in the corridor left where it fell; the identity through settlement into the next night).
182. **Cargo, contracts and the child (GDD v1.4 §9, §18, §19, App. A.6, B.6, B.9, C.4; WP13).**
    - **Where the contract's cargo meets the facility's (reading):** the contract decides what the train *departs carrying*. Every loaded car leaves the fortress with the contract's freight (`Consist.Carrying`, `SessionSetup.Cargo`, `HarnessOptions.Cargo`) instead of goods. The facilities still load their own on top, and a car still goes by the last cargo loaded into it (T68). Each car pays by what's in it at the terminus (`run.json economy.cargoRates`, a share of the tier's per-car value). So the contract's per-car figure is its cargo's rate at the tier (`Campaign.PerCar(run, tier, cargo)`). Goods pay 1, so spec F.1's table (a train of goods) and `NetIncomeByConsistMatchesSpecF1` still hold. The B.9 modifiers already went by what's aboard (`Director.Aboard`), so a contract's cargo changes the night from the gate.
      - Topping a comet car up with food makes it a food car. Mixing loads is a decision the crew make.
      - §18's "top payout" (the chemical works), "best payout" (the military depot) and "high value" (the wreck yard) are now rates too (chemicals 1.4, ammunition 1.5, salvage 1.2).
    - **The board** (`Campaign.Offers`, `campaign.json contracts`): the ordinary contracts are unchanged (the consist's tier, and one from below). Each draws its freight from `contracts.cargo` by the night and its slot. Then, with `contracts.comet`, a comet contract at the consist's own tier: B.9's "high-risk contract ... the best freight payout" (rate 1.8, above every other). Its B.9 effect was already in place: `cargoWeights.comet "*": 1.4` and `cometRelaxesGates`.
      - `Contract` gains `Cargo`. A save from before reads as goods.
      - The fortress screen shows each contract's freight and what it does: "TONIGHT: LOCAL 7913, COMET-DERIVED MATERIAL, 810 A CAR". `dt campaign show` lists it, and `dt campaign play` departs with it.
      - The dispatcher's manifest reads the freight ("Freight: medicine.").
    - **New cargo kinds** (`CargoKind` Medicine, Timber, Coal, on the end; `Cargoes` names them):
      - **Medicine** (§19 "fragile, high value"; `train.json fragile`): any contact closing faster than 0.3 m/s spoils a medicine car by (closing − 0.3)² × the couplings' damage × cargo share × 3, where everything else feels only knocks over 1 m/s (`TrainOnLine.Jolt`). Braking harder than 1.2 m/s² spoils it 0.02 a second for every m/s² over (`BrakeShock`): a full brake on a short train costs about a fifth.
      - **Coal and timber** (§19 "burns", B.9 "fire cascades escalate faster"; `enemies.json carFire.fuelGrowth` 1.4, `fuelSpread` 2): a fire in their car grows faster and runs its spread clock twice as fast.
      - **Not modelled:** timber's "heavy" (every loaded car weighs the same).
    - **Gunpowder explodes** (§19; `carFire.explodeAt`, `explodeRadius`, `explodeKillRadius`, `explodeDamage`): a powder car with cargo left in it that reaches full blaze goes up, once (`CarFire.Explode`). The car's integrity and cargo go to 0. Everyone within 5 m of the fire takes 150 (killed), and less with distance out to 14 m, through walls. The cars either side catch. It's an impact with no shooter on the train's body (`World.Blast`), so every client already draws and hears the cannonball's explosion there. Death cause `Exploded` ("Killed when a powder car went up"; attributed like a burn, to whoever lit the lamp).
    - **A cannon beside chemicals** (B.9 "lethal to the crew"; `combat.json fumes`): a shot from a gun on a loaded chemicals car, or on the car next to one, gasses the gunner and everyone within 8 m of that car, for 150 (`World.Fumes`). This runs host-side after the whole crew has acted. Death cause `Poisoned`, attributed to the gunner (`Attribution.Gasser`).
      - **Reading:** "near" is a coupling, not a distance. The guard van's gun sits 14 m from the car ahead's nearest end, so any distance that caught it would also catch a car two down from the engine's gun.
    - **The child** (§19, B.9 "highest payout", A.6 REAL "cannot be harmed"):
      - **Pay:** each rescued child home (in a car still on the engine, or in someone's arms) pays `economy.childPay` (3) times the tier's per-car value. That's more than any one car-load of anything, comet included. It's in the gross, on the report (`RunReport.ChildrenHome`, `ChildPay`), and in the clerk's tally ("Child survivor: 1. Paid 2100.").
      - **Unharmed:** what takes or eats loot ranks things by `Bodies.Prey`, which is `Value` with the child worth nothing. So the Gaunt passes over the child for the next best thing, or the car's freight. The Followers don't count the child towards the car to nest in. Nothing damages a body. A fire or a blast in its car leaves the child where it is. A child dropped off the formation is recovered to its edge like a body (note 181's `World.Recover`).
      - **Reading:** a car taken whole (the Car Hugger, the Passenger) takes the child with it. That's a lost car, like one with a body in it, not harm done to the child.
    - **A host's first-ever child call is real (B.6, A.6):** the profile (`PlayerProfile.Data.ChildCalled`, note 180's file) says whether a night this player hosted has had a call.
      - Until it has, `SessionSetup.FirstChildReal` sets the host world's `NextChildReal`. The Game passes the bool in, and the Sim reads no file.
      - The spawn rule sets `World.ChildCalled`. After the night, the app marks the profile.
      - **Reading:** "first-ever" is the host's, across campaigns and quick nights, which is why it lives in the profile and not a save.
    - **Departure purchases (§9):**
      - **What was already there:** cars, upgrades and spare repair kits. Coal, rounds and repairs are already the fortress's standard issue, billed on what the night uses (spec F.1's running costs).
      - **What's added:**
        - `campaign.json stores`, bought for the coming night only and spent with it (`CampaignState.Stores`, `Campaign.BuyStores`, a STORES screen; `dt campaign buy powder|lamp|extinguisher`). A crate of powder and shot is 4 more rounds for every gun (120 scrip, up to 3). A spare lamp is 60 (up to 2) and a spare extinguisher 90 (up to 2), both stocked in the guard van; the extinguishers have no bracket to recharge on. They reach every machine through `SessionSetup` (`Powder`, `SpareLamps`, `SpareExtinguishers`), like the spare kits.
        - Taking a car off (`campaign.json sellCar`, `Campaign.SellCar`, `dt campaign buy sell`) brings back half what that car cost, never below three cars. A shorter train drops a tier when it falls below one (F.4), and the row says so.
      - **Reading:** "repair the train" needs no purchase, because damage doesn't carry between nights and the tally bills the repairs. "Repair supplies and tools" are the spare kits.
      - **Not modelled:** extra rounds are billed as used, like the standard ones (the crate's price is for carrying them).
    - Protocol 15. Tests:
      - `CargoTests`: the child's pay, and a child cut off isn't home; each car paid by its cargo; the Gaunt and the child; the comet aboard, its weights and gates; medicine's knock and brake; coal and timber; the chemicals' gas; a host's first call.
      - `CarFireTests`: the powder car's blast, its radius and the child unharmed in it; goods never explode.
      - `CampaignTests`: freight on every contract, the comet best every night, an old save's contract reading as goods; the stores, aboard and spent; selling a car.
      - `PlayerProfileTests`: the first-call flag, kept off the wire.
    - Renders: `dt screenshot --menu fortress|stores` (the board and the stores); `--view inside --cargo medicine|timber|coal`.
183. **Hazards: deep cold, wind, tunnels, fouled guns, broken radios, lamps out (GDD §22, §23; WP14).**
    - **Deep cold (§22):** `ITrackConditions.ColdStep` is linegen §14's cold: the night's `TempStep` plus the exposure stretch's own step (climbed high, or exposed track). Each step makes a player's cold come on `player.json cold.perColdStep` (0.25) faster, and takes `boiler.json coldEfficiencyPerStep` (0.06) off what a shovelful of coal makes, down to `coldEfficiencyFloor` (0.7). Hand-authored routes have no conditions and stay at step 0.
    - **Wind (§22):** `ITrackConditions.Wind` is the night's wind times the stretch's exposure. A gun round feeds the meter ×(1 + `combat.json guns.windLoudness` × wind), since a shot carries further across open, windy country.
    - **Tunnels (§22, "compressed proximity voice, no exterior reference"):** while the listener is under one, proximity voice goes through `voice.json`'s "tunnel" parameter: lows and highs cut, a hard boost in the mids, a little louder. It's the listener's client's call, not the sim's.
    - **Gun jams (§23, "someone repairs it by hand, under fire"):** each shot fouls the bore with `guns.foulChance` (0.04), ×`foulWetFactor` (2.5) on wet rail. Holding Use at the gun, standing still, for `clearSeconds` (4) clears it; letting go starts over. Which shot fouls is a hash of the tick and the gun (`Guns.Fouls`), the same on every machine without a shared die.
    - **Radios break (§23):** at a knock or a grab, each worn radio may break, with chance `kit.radioBreakPerDamage` (0.006) per point of health lost, plus `radioBreakOnGrab` (0.25) when the wearer is newly grabbed. The tick and the wearer decide it, by the same hash. A broken radio is still worn (the slot's taken) but off the air; the HUD says RADIO BROKEN. Host-only, mirrored on the body record.
    - **Lights fail (§23):**
      - A Climber getting into a car puts that car's lamp out.
      - A Climber coming into the cab smashes the forward lamp for `climber.lampOutSeconds` (45).
      - A derailment puts every lamp out, the forward lamp included.
      - **Reading:** the lamp goes out when it gets in, not as it mounts. Otherwise it would pick the car it had just darkened (App. A.4 ENTER: "the first car unlit or with nobody in it").
    - **Not yet:** wind on footing or sway; the cold step on the HUD; repairing a broken radio. All three since: note 201.
    - Protocol 16. Tests: `HazardTests` (cold rate, boiler efficiency, the foul hash, clearing a jam, radios breaking on a knock and on a grab, a derailment's lamps), and the lamp in `ClimberTests`. `EnemyTests`' spraying gunner gets 30 s more for the Choir, since a fouled bore can hold it off past 90 s.
184. **Consist variety: the crew car, armour, a second guard car, and the consist's upgrades (GDD §10, §26, spec F.3; WP16).** §10: "Experienced crews run engine, armour, cannons, utility cars and many freight cars." F.3 listed the consist's upgrades, bought and saved with an empty `effect`.
    - **Composition (`train.json composition`):** the counts are what's been bought; campaign.json's consist upgrades add to them (`utilityCars`, `guardCars`, `armouredCars`) or fit something (`handrails`), and `Consist.Uniform` builds the train from them. The upgrade list already travels in the Welcome, so every machine builds the same train with nothing new on the wire.
      - Converted cars come out of the cargo cars, never leaving fewer than `minCargoCars` (1). Extra guard cars are made first (a gun before a stove), then crew cars.
      - A crew car goes right behind the engine: the first is the kit's car, so the crew lockers (note 173) are its stores. An extra guard car goes in the middle of the cargo that's left, its gun covering what the engine's and the van's can't reach. Armour goes on from the rear forward, guard van first, since the rear is where the Car Hugger feeds.
      - A converted car carries no freight (`CargoKind.None`), so the night pays on the cargo cars alone: F.3's "costs a cargo slot". Its mass stays a cargo car's, as the guard van's always has.
    - **The crew car (`VehicleKind.Utility`, §10, §26 "cramped, lamp-lit, human-scale"):**
      - Walled like the guard van: no side doors, no freight. Berths down the right (`PartKind.Bunk`), the stove at the rear on the left (`PartKind.Stove`, `CarShape.Stove`), clear of the end doorway, and the aisle between.
      - The stove is its own heat (`PlayerMotor.BesideStove`), steam or none. Shut in, the whole car's warm; with a door open, only within `stoveReach` (2.5 m) of the stove, like standing at the cab's firebox.
      - Lamp-lit as every car is, drawn with the art pass's fit-out (`TrainKit.UtilityFit`: bunks, the stove and its smoking pipe, table, lit windows), which until now only a still frame could show.
      - **Reading:** F.3 doesn't list it. It's sold as `crewCar` (major, 1.0 of the next car: the cheapest major, since it costs a cargo slot and carries no gun), the spec's own price bands kept.
    - **Armoured car conversion (§26 "reinforced plating, heavier mass"):** one car, the van, `Vehicle.Armoured`. It weighs `armourTonnes` (10) more, so the same engine pulls it slower (spec B.5's forces are the loaded consist's). `Vehicle.Batter` lets `armourDamage` (0.5) of a blow to its shell through: the Car Hugger's feeding and a knock at the couplers. Drawn in the armoured livery under riveted plate, a skirt over the trucks and angle iron at the eaves (`TrainKit.ArmourPlate`).
    - **Second guard car (F.3 "a third gun, but it costs a cargo slot"):** `guardCars` 2. It's a guard van with cars behind it, so it has no rear platform; its gun faces back, slides on its rail and is restocked like the others.
    - **The small ones:**
      - *Roof handrails* (F.3 "Dragger resistance"): with a hand on the rail, a Dragger's grab range is `rails.draggerGrab` (0.6) of what it was, and it hangs on `draggerHang` (1.5) times as long before it has you, which gives friends longer to haul you back. A bend has to be taken `throwOver` (1.5) times further over its limit to throw you off the roof. Drawn on every car's roof (`TrainKit.RoofHandrails`), with gaps at the ladder heads.
      - *Car insulation* (F.3 "(cold)"): in a car without heat (a door open, or the steam gone) the cold comes on at `insulation` × `player.json cold.indoorsRate`: halved.
      - *Coupling reinforcement:* the closing speed a coupling takes before the ends are damaged goes ×1.5, so a coupling-up at the most that couples (1.5 m/s) does none. The Passenger takes ×1.5 as long to unhook the caboose (6 s).
      - *Reinforced couplings* (F.3 "uncouple under load"): cutting a working train takes no longer than cutting a slack one (`uncoupleUnderLoadSeconds` ×0.5: 6 s to 3 s).
      - **Reading:** "uncouple under load" read as being able to cut under load as readily as slack, since cutting under load was already possible, just slower. "Coupling reinforcement" read as the couplings taking harder knocks. The wreck's `couplingBreak` stays as it is: a derailment's film is not a coupling the crew can save.
    - `dt screenshot --upgrades crewCar,secondGuardCar,armouredCar,roofHandrails` builds the train as the campaign would. `--view side --car n` stands on the ballast level with a car. `dt art show guard-armoured`, `car-crew`, `crew-fit`, `armour-plate`, `roof-handrails`; all are within their budgets.
    - **Not yet:** the handrails' longer hang and the bend's harder throw have no test of their own. (Lamp armour, gun cooling, repair kit charges, radio range and the powered switch thrower were bought and unmodelled here; note 196 did them.)
    - Protocol unchanged (nothing new on the wire; a joiner whose train.json or campaign.json differs is refused by the content hash). Tests: `ConsistVarietyTests` (the composition and its limits, the third gun, armour's mass and plate, the Car Hugger on plate, the crew car's stove heat and recovery, the crew car as the kit's car, insulation, the handrails against a Dragger, each consist upgrade's tuning, a reinforced coupling's knock). `CampaignTests` now finds the handrails fitted, and `InteriorTests` checks the crew car's doorways.
185. **Facility set pieces: the grain elevator's spout, the slaughterhouse's herd, the chemical works' hose, the depot's powder (GDD §18, §19; spec D.2; WP15).**
    - **Built on what was there:** every facility already had its spur, its stop layout, its art, its cargo (note 182) and spec D.2's crates, winch and crane. Three more D.2 modules join `ModuleKind` (`Spout`, `Ramp`, `Hose`, on the end), laid out by `Site` from the route like the rest, and run on the host in `Run.StepSetPieces` (`Run/SetPieces.cs`). They work whether or not the engine counts as "stopped" (a train walked a car's length under the spout is moving). `facilities.json` `kinds`: the grain elevator is its spout; the slaughterhouse the ramp and crates; the chemical works the hose and crates; the depot keeps crates and winch, its crates being its powder.
    - **Grain elevator (§18 "one spout, one car at a time. Endless repositioning and switch calls"):** a bin on legs astride the spur, its spout as far back from the buffer stop as the standing track allows (`spout.back` 80 m), so the car nearest the engine comes under it with the engine well short of the buffer stop and the cars behind it as the engine runs on. Grain comes down only while someone holds the lever on the ground (`pourPerSecond` 0.05 a second from a `bin` of 3 car-loads), into the cargo car whose middle is within `tolerance` (1.5 m) of the spout: nothing there, it's on the ballast; a full car there overflows, and the overflow strains the car (D.2's chute: "overfill damages car and spills"). It's machinery: loud while it pours.
      - **Bots:** once the stop's other loading is done the driver takes a `Spouting` leg. It puts each car with room under the spout in turn, nearest the buffer stop first so the train only ever backs, the way it backs out anyway (`StopPlan.SpoutTarget`). It holds while grain's coming down and while anyone but the shunter is still climbing aboard. The shunter stands at the lever and holds it while a car with room stands under the spout, letting go the tick it's full.
    - **Slaughterhouse (§18 "animals are loud, and something already lives here"; D.2 livestock ramp):** a pen of `ramp.head` (4–6, by the seed) beyond a ramp up to a car's doorway. With `herders` (2) or more in the pen holding Use and a car with room at the ramp's top, one goes up every `secondsPerHead` (6 s), a fifth of a car-load of livestock each. Every herder past two quickens it by a quarter. From the first one aboard until the last, the herd is stirred: the Choir's floor is `ramp.choirFloor` (D.2 "raises Choir floor while active"), and driving them is machinery-loud. "Something already lives here" is enemies.json `director.residents`: while stopped at a slaughterhouse the Gaunt comes on ×4 and Followers ×1.5. **Bots:** the winch pair drive the herd, then carry crates.
    - **Chemical works (§18 "Leaks. Do not fire indoors"; D.2 fluid gantry):** hold Use at the stand for `connectSeconds` (2.5 s) to put the hose on the cargo car with room by it, and again to take it off. On, it fills the car (`flowPerSecond` 0.04). Its pressure climbs `pressureRise` (0.05) a second unless someone stands at the stand, which bleeds it at 0.15. At 1 it leaks: everyone within `leakRadius` (6 m) takes `leakDamagePerSecond` (12, a point at a time), death cause `Leak`, attributed to whoever put the hose on; and the car's load spoils at `spoilPerSecond`. Moving the car off with the hose on tears it: it sprays for `tornSeconds` (20 s) and the load spoils by `tornSpoil` (0.25) at once. **Bots:** Winch0 puts it on, stands by it while the car fills, takes it off once it's full; the driver doesn't leave with it on.
    - **"Do not fire indoors":** a cannon fired from a car within `hose.indoorsM` (60 m) of the works' stand gasses as one fired beside a loaded chemicals car does (note 182, `World.Fumes`): the gunner and everyone within 8 m of the gun's car, death cause `Poisoned`, attributed to the gunner.
    - **Military depot (§18 "best payout, worst cargo"; §19 "explodes"):** its crates are powder kegs (cargo ammunition). A keg brought up short by more than `kegs.blowSpeed` (6 m/s) goes up: that's a throw, or a fall from a roof. Set down by hand it falls a hand's height, about 3.7 m/s, and nothing happens. The change is measured on its world velocity tick to tick, so frames don't matter, and never on the tick it leaves someone's hands. Everyone within `killRadius` (3 m) takes 150, less out to 9 m, death cause `Keg` (attributed to whoever last carried it). Cars within reach lose 0.3 integrity, every keg within `chain` (3 m) goes up the tick after, and every client sees and hears it as a blast (`World.Blast`). A powder car at full blaze already went up (note 182). **Bots:** the crate hands already set crates down inside the car, and never throw them.
    - **The harm lands next tick:** a blast or a leak found in `Run.Step` (after the world's damage has been applied for the tick) is handed out from the next tick's crew step (`Run.Harm`), as the crane's crush already was (`Crushes`).
    - **Headless:** `dt facility drill <kind>` has a bot crew work a facility of that kind (a hand-tuned frontier night's first spur facility made that kind, or `--route tier:seed`'s own) from 600 m short of its spur until the train leaves, all through intent (`Bots/FacilityWork`), and reports legs, what each hand did, each car's load and cargo, and the set piece's state. All four leave with every car down the spur full of the facility's cargo, the crew alive and the switch back. `dt screenshot --route r --site --facility <kind> [--leak]` shows the set piece working.
    - **Readings:**
      - "Endless repositioning and switch calls" is the spout's one car at a time with the train walked under it. The cars are already in the spur and the spout reaches every one of them, so no extra switch moves are invented.
      - "Do not fire indoors" is a cannon fired anywhere in among the works (60 m of its stand takes in every car down the spur). There is no indoors for a train's guns, and personal weapons don't fire.
      - D.2's livestock ramp "raises Choir floor while active" is from the first head aboard until the last (or while being driven). A herd still standing quiet in its pen doesn't count.
      - The grain elevator offers its spout alone, not D.1's 2–4 modules: with crates too, the crates filled the cars before the spout got a turn.
    - **Not yet:**
      - ~~WP15b: the switchyard's cars scattered across sidings and the wreck yard's derailed train to salvage.~~ Done: note 187.
      - Art-pass models for the spout's bin, the pen's animals and the hose stand: they're greybox boxes, drawn from the sim's state.
      - A herd that breaks loose.
      - Audio for the pour, the herd and the leak, beyond the machinery's loudness.
    - Protocol 17 (the run record's set-piece fields; death causes `Keg` and `Leak`). Tests: `FacilityTests` (the spout's one car and its overflow, the spout reaching every spur car, the herd's two herders and Choir floor, the residents, the hose's fill, leak and tear, a cannon fired indoors, the powder's knock and chain, a client mirroring the set pieces); `StopCrewTests` (a bot crew works each of the four through intent).

186. **The verification harness's gaps: combination fairness, the cascade audit, degraded comms, the per-tree GRAB check (GDD §34, App. A.9, B.10, D.14, E.11; WP17).** §34 asked for six checks; the harness had the crew-size and train-length sweeps (`dt balance`, T55) and stability (the soak). The other four are here, each a `dt` command printing JSON and exiting 1 on a finding.
    - **Combination fairness (§34 "every pair and triple in the roster against every hazard set. Flag unwinnable or trivially solved"; `dt balance --pairs|--triples`, `balance.json combinations`):**
      - Each combination of GDD §21's 17 is *insisted on* (`World.Insist`, `HarnessOptions.Insist`): only those kinds, each sent whenever its spawn rule can place it, and again `insistEvery` (10 s) after one's gone. The director's budget, pacing and weights are skipped; the spawn's own placement still has to find somewhere. The director's roster is those kinds alone, so the line brings no Sleepers or marsh and the Choir comes only insisted on (gathered all but its last 5 s, the meter held up until the swarm's there, then left to the crew to hush).
      - A short bot night (4 bots, 6 cars, 90 s, clean link) on `frontier:7`, starting where every kind that needs the line can be placed: just short of a marsh or bridge for the Car Hugger, of a dead line for the Switchman (`Combinations.Stage`).
      - **Hazard sets** (`combinations.hazardSets`, `HazardSet`, laid over the line by `HazardConditions` for host and clients alike): clear; wet (adhesion 0.75, the generator's wettest); cold (cold step +2, wind 1); dark (the forward lamp smashed and every car's lamp out, §23 "lights fail"). §22: they take tools away and never change an enemy's rules.
      - **Verdicts** (`Combinations.Judge`): *unwinnable*, lost every seed (derailed, stranded, wiped out, or half the crew dead); *trivial*, everything came on and nothing landed in any seed (no grab, punish or death, under 2 % of the cargo gone); *fair*. Not judged: *unplaced* (a spawn that found nowhere) and *dormant* (put there, never came on).
      - **Reading:** "every hazard set" by default is each combination against one set, the sets in turn, so every combination runs and every set is met in minutes. `--every-hazard` is the whole cross (the nightly's). `--triples` runs a seeded `tripleSample` (136) of the 680, `--sample 0` all of them.
      - **Reading:** what comes at a stop (the Whistler, Ribbits, the Gaunt, Followers, Soot Children, the Passenger, the Grumbler) is sent at the crew as they board from the ballast at the start. `--at-stops` starts those combinations `approachM` (350 m) short of the first facility's spur instead (one with a crane for the Grumbler) for `stopSeconds` (150 s), so the crew stop and get down to work. A stop's night costs several times a line's, so that's the nightly's.
    - **Cascade audit (§34 "no failure chain is unrecoverable"; `dt audit cascades`, `CascadeAudit`, `balance.json cascades`):** §23's table, each chain scripted into a night (`HarnessOptions.Script`, before the host steps, ended early by `Until`), and the crew left to answer it: six bots over the loopback, through intent like any player.
      - The chains: a rupture with the kit in its locker in car 1 (mended, then back in the working band); the same with the kit on car 4's floor (§23 "left in car four"); the fire let die; car 3 alight; the guard van's gun fouled; the Stoker in the firebox; and a car cut loose at a stand, with the train run 40 m on and set back onto it by a scripted driver (no bot goes back for a car out on the line yet).
      - Each has a window well over the GDD's own times. `--trace` writes what each bot's doing, once a second, after the failure.
    - **What it found, fixed (bots, all three on the mend):**
      - The driver followed the fireman out to the kit once they'd left the cab, and the two of them at the locker undid each other's presses (`ConductorBot.Mend`: not with anyone else on the engine or in car 1).
      - `KitRun` tapped Use "every other tick" by the client world's tick, which steps by two after a snapshot's re-simulation, so it was Use held down. And it tapped again before the snapshot said the kit was in hand, putting it straight back on the shelf. It now taps by the bot's own tick, once every 16.
      - `Heed.Backs`'s idle glance every 4 s turned the fireman away from the firebox, and the 25 s mend started over. A bot holding something isn't idle (as `World.CrewAct` counts it).
      - The rupture is now mended in about 40 s and back in the band in about 85 s at six cars.
    - **What it found, fixed: the kit in car four (`KitCarry`, `Bots/KitCarry.cs`).** `KitRun` only knew the way to car 1, and nobody else went, so the rupture with the kit on car 4's floor was never mended: GDD §12's "it's lying in car four and someone has to go and get it", which §23's table says must never strand. Now a crewmate out on the train brings it, through intent like any player.
      - **Which kit** (`KitCarry.Lying`): one on a coupled car's floor or in its locker, behind car 1, with every car from there to the engine one to walk through. One in the engine or car 1 is still the cab's (`KitRun`); one in anyone's hands is theirs. (Not `Body.Claimed`, which is the host's: a kit in a coupled car is the crew's anyway.) A crewmate who dies with it in hand drops it where they fall (`Bodies.Handle`), so that's a kit on a floor too, or on a roof or the ballast, where nobody goes for it yet.
      - **Who goes** (`KitCarry.Mine`), worked out alike by every walker and the gunner from what each sees, nobody calling it: anyone already down on the floors and plates (the lowest player id), else whoever's on the roofs fewest cars from it (the kit's car and the one ahead count as none), the lowest id of them; one already on its way down keeps on until someone's down (two may go; the first down has it). Never the engine's crew; a gunner in its seat only if there's nobody else. **Reading:** cars, not metres. Each bot sees itself where it is and the others a snapshot late: two walkers side by side on car 4's roof each saw the other nearer by a hair, and for 25 s neither went (or both did, by turns). And without the keeping on, the one fewest cars off changed as the others walked from car to car, and a walker turned back from the end of a roof each time, for the whole window (the audit at 8 s in).
      - **The way:** down off the roofs as a walker goes in to get warm (`WarmUp`, sent into the car ahead of the kit's, from its roof or the kit car's, so it lands on the plate in front of the kit), then along the floors and plates (`KitCarry.Toward`: through a car to its end door, onto the plate, into the next), holding Use at each shut door till it's open. With the kit in hand it goes car by car, run along the floors, and through car 1 and up the tender's gangway to the cab by `KitRun`'s own way, where it holds the kit at the firebox itself (§12: the engineer is whoever has it; `CrewActions.AtTheRupture` asks only for the kit). Mended, it puts the kit down in the cab (the cab's to fetch next time) and goes back out by car 1 (`KitRun.BackToTheTrain`), and up its rear ladder.
      - **Carry rules:** the kit isn't freight (`World`'s heavy flag), so ladders aren't barred, but nothing's carried along the roofs: a gap jump puts it in the air. And Use with it in hand puts it down (`Bodies.Handle`), while a door wants Use: on a plate a metre wide it could go off onto the ballast. So the doors of the next hop (the car's front door, the next car's rear, and car 1's front when the next is car 1, so the kit's never put down in the cab's car) are opened first, empty-handed, the kit left on the floor 2.2 m behind the front end wall, clear of the door's reach, on the load's side (by the left wall, it went down beside the car's extinguisher, and the hands took that up instead, put it down, took it up, all night); then it's taken up and carried through. Taps by the bot's own tick, every `KitRun.TapEvery` (the double-press above).
      - **A walker warming in a car shut every door the bringer opened** ("someone came or went and left a door open: shut it again"), and the kit never left car 4. While the boiler's ruptured, a car's end doors are left open (`WarmUp.LeaveOpen`): nobody warms up in a car with a door open (spec B.2), so a walker in there waits on the cold for the minute or two that takes, well inside the 600 s onset.
      - The kit from car 4 is mended in about 76 s and back in the band in about 118 s at six cars (the window: 120 s and 240 s); 73-83 s and 116-126 s over failures 8-60 s in and seeds 1-4. `dt audit cascades` recovers all seven chains and exits 0. The audit's trace says where on a floor the kit lies.
    - **Degraded comms (§34 "agents on lossy, laggy, restricted voice — simulating people shouting over each other"; `dt harness --comms poor|awful`, `balance.json comms`):** `CrewCalls` (the bots' shared board of who's where with which part) can go over a `CrewVoice`. Each call or repeat is lost with chance `loss`, heard `latencySeconds` (± `jitterSeconds`) late, and a new call is talked over with chance `talkOver` for each other crewmate who started one in the last `talkOverSeconds`. Older news arriving after newer is dropped as stale.
      - It's seeded from the night's seed and drawn in the bots' fixed order, so it's deterministic. The report's `voice` says what got through.
      - **Reading:** claims (a door to shut, a Holdout to breach) stay instant: they settle who goes where, as much by sight as by word, and they answer on the spot. A lost call is lost to the speaker too: the board is everyone's ears.
    - **The per-tree GRAB check (A.9 "every GRAB is interruptible by the crew actually present", B.10 "at every crew size"; `dt audit grabs`, `GrabAudit`, `balance.json grabs`):**
      - For each of the 12 kinds that grab, at crews 2–8, the victim is put where the creature takes them: alone, idle, at its gap, in front of its mouth, the Gaunt woken and kept silent at, the Grumbler hit by them. The friends stand `standOffM` (4 m) off, or a metre past the creature's own "alone" radius where it has one, so it still takes the victim. They carry the starting kit.
      - When it grabs, the friends answer as the bots do (`Heed.Rescue`). For the Climber in its car, they first come down off the roof behind and in at the rear door (`WarmUp.Into`): anyone inside a 14 m car is inside its count.
      - The check passes if the grab breaks with the victim alive inside its window. The control (`friendsHelp: false`) shows the rig doesn't pass it by itself.
      - **Reading:** crew sizes 2–8 are B.10's "seven crew sizes". A crew of one has the solo struggle (T89), and the Passenger is built to be unbreakable alone (A.8).
    - **What the first sweep flagged (pairs, default run):** 24 fair, 21 trivial, 4 unwinnable, the rest dormant or unplaced. The four unwinnable were all the Car Hugger in the dark: the roof walkers went down the guard van's ladder to its mouth one at a time and were eaten, nobody hauling them out. That's left for the bots (and the director) to read, not fixed here. Most of the trivial ones have the Stoker in them (clubbed out of the firebox in a second), or Cinder Hounds the gunner drives off.
    - **Nightly:** `soak.yml` runs the pairs against every hazard set, the pairs at stops, the triples' sample, both audits, and a degraded-comms night. The audits and sweeps report without failing the job (`continue-on-error`): they're a sieve for the director. Timeout 240 min (note 204).
    - **Not yet:**
      - A kit lying on a roof or out on the ballast (a crewmate who died up there with it) isn't gone for; nor one in a car with no way through (a car without a room between it and the engine). And a human down on the floors with a lower id than any bot is the one the bots leave it to.
      - D.14's and E.11's rows are already pinned elsewhere (`StrandedTests`, `HoldoutTests`, `VoteTests`, `FilmTests`) and aren't re-run here.
      - Bots never went near a sleeping Gaunt, stood at a roof's edge, or were caught outnumbered on the ground, so the Gaunt, the Draggers and Ribbits lay dormant. On an insisted night the last walker is now the look-out and goes and looks (note 212); with no walker in the crew (a crew of two) they still do. The Grumbler is unplaced without `--at-stops` (no crane out on the line). The sweep now runs over a grid of routes and crew sizes with seeds a cell (note 204); the default grid is still one route, one crew and one seed, so by hand an unwinnable verdict is one lost night unless `--wide` or `--seeds` is given.
      - Comms degrade the stop crew's calls only. The bots' combat heuristics read the world directly.
    - Protocol unchanged (all host- or harness-side; `balance.json` is content, so its hash already covers it). Tests: `KitCarryTests` (which kit is a crewmate's to bring, and exactly one goes for it), `AuditTests` (every grab broken at crews 2 and 8, and the watching friends' control; all seven cascade chains recovered; the pairs and triples and their sample; the verdicts; a hazard set over a line; an insisted night bringing only its kinds; staging for the Car Hugger and at a stop; the voice's lag, loss, talk-over, stale news and determinism, and a harness night's voice report).
187. **The switchyard's standing cars and the wreck yard's derailed train (GDD §18; spec D.1, D.2; level-design I.4; WP15b).**
    - **Built on what was there:** the rakes and couplings of `TrainOnLine` (any number of rakes, each on a path; rakes that meet at a crawl couple), the yards' tracks (note 95, level-design P5: every track of a generated yard is a spur off the main line with its own switch), the crates' stowing and the crate hands, the hand lamps in the guard van, and the set pieces' harm-next-tick (note 185). Two more D.2-style modules join `ModuleKind` on the end, `Rakes` and `Wreck`; `facilities.json` `kinds`: the switchyard is `["rakes", "crates"]`, the wreck yard `["wreck", "winch"]` (they keep their crates and winch). The new code is `Run/Yards.cs`.
    - **Switchyard (§18 "mixed. Cars scattered across six sidings. A live track puzzle solved by shouting"):**
      - **Standing rakes at the start of the night** (level-design I.4's deferred "derelict cars"): `TrainOnLine.Stand` adds a rake of cars to a train already built, growing its vehicle, pose and frame arrays; the cars take the ids after every vehicle there is (`OwnVehicles` is where the train's own end). `Run.StandCars`, called from `World.EnableRun` on the host and every client alike, puts `rakes.cars` (1–2) on every track of the yard but its own (on its own if that's all it has), `rakes.back` short of the buffer stop, handbrakes on, each car part-loaded (`rakes.load`) with one of `rakes.cargoes` ("mixed": each pays at its own cargo rate). They're `Vehicle.YardCar` and unlit (nobody's lit their lamps). Only the route goes into it (route.json's 12 m points, never the session's switch tuning), so every machine stands the same cars with the same ids.
      - **Not the train until coupled:** `TrainOnLine.Standing(rake)` is a rake of yard cars alone, on a spur, without the engine. `TrainRakes` counts the train's own rakes (the engine's and its cuts); the bots' "is the train together" tests, the report's lost cars (`Run.Tally`, the clerk's "cars lost" lines) and the winch's car-finding all leave standing rakes out. Coupled up, the cars are the train's: they're in the engine's consist, delivered and paid like any car; cut off again on the main line they're lost like any car.
      - **They come away ahead of the engine:** every yard track's points face up the line (I.4: no trailing points, no run-round), so the engine runs onto the standing cars front first and they're in front of it from then on: the night runs on propelling them. Nothing about the consist assumed the engine was first except `StopPlan` (below); the rest that reads a rake's first vehicle wants its front one, which is what it gets.
      - **Never more than fits:** in all, no more than the shortest of those sidings takes with the engine and `rakes.spare` (1) car of the train's own behind it, since the crew go into each siding with every car already picked up still in front.
      - **Bots:** a switchyard's own track is a stop as before (its crates; and any cars standing on it, coupled up as the engine runs in); each other siding with cars standing is a stop of its own, a *pick-up* (`StopPlan.PickUp`, keyed by its siding so crew mark each done separately). On a pick-up the shunter cuts behind the first car behind the engine and throws that siding's switch; the driver runs in onto the standing cars at a crawl so they couple (`SpurIn` aims at their rear, as `BackOut` aims at a cut), backs out onto the cut, clears the points, and the switch is set back; nobody else gets down. `StopPlan.Ahead` counts the cars ahead of the engine into what goes in, and skips a stop whose spur can't take the engine. Walkers don't climb a standing car to get aboard (the shunter did, and stood on the wrong car's roof all night).
    - **Wreck yard (§18 "salvage, high value. Pull cargo off derailed trains. Unstable, unlit, already occupied"):**
      - **The wreck:** the last train came off at the end of the yard's own line. `wreck.heaps` (3–4) of its cars lie where `wreck.layout` puts them, metres from the buffer stop (positive: through it and beyond) and out on the site's side; each holds `wreck.salvage` (2–3) pieces, crates of cargo `salvage` carried out by hand and stowed as crates are.
      - **Unlit:** there are no lamps (the powerhouse's lamp isn't drawn at a wreck yard either). A heap's salvage is only found, and comes out onto the ground beside it, once a hand lamp is within `lampReach` (6 m) of it, carried or set down, or it's in the engine's headlamp (within `beamLength` 40 m ahead, from 2 m either side widening `beamSpread` 0.2 m a metre). The layout puts two heaps in the beam of an engine run up to the buffer stop and the rest out wide or beside the cars: those need someone to take a lamp to them.
      - **Unstable:** every piece picked up costs its heap `strainPerPiece` (0.4); at none it groans for `warnSeconds` (3 s: the tell, dust shaken off it, and the HUD's "IT'S GOING") and then shifts: everyone within `crushRadius` (3.5 m) takes `damage` (45), death cause `Wreckage`, attributed to whoever pulled the last piece (`Run.WreckBy`); it settles at `settle` and shifts at most `shifts` (2) times. The pieces come out at the heap's near edge, so someone carrying one off towards the cars is clear of it in a couple of steps; someone lingering by it isn't.
      - **Already occupied:** enemies.json `director.residents.wreckYard`: Draggers ×3 and the Gaunt ×2 while stopped there.
      - **Bots:** the crate hands (and the winch pair, once the sleds are in) carry out whatever's been found, as crates (`StopPlan.Loose` takes a piece lying within 60 m of a heap; `CratesToLoad` counts them), and step clear of a groaning heap. They carry no lamps, so they salvage what the headlamp finds.
    - **Replication:** the standing cars are rakes and vehicles like any (records keyed by id), and each heap is a record of its own (`RecordKind.Heap`: salvage unfound, found, stability, groan, shifts). Protocol 18.
    - **Headless:** `dt facility drill switchyard|wreckyard` (and `--route tier:seed`) report the cars picked up (id, load, cargo), those still standing at that facility, the engine's rake front to back, the heaps, and every stop the driver made. Both default drills leave with the crew alive and the switch back: the switchyard's crates loaded and its siding's cars brought away ahead of the engine; the wreck yard's sleds in and the headlamp's heaps salvaged. `dt screenshot --route r --site --facility switchyard` shows the engine run up a siding to its standing cars; `--facility wreckYard` the heaps from beside the engine at the buffer stop, all but the last found, the first groaning (`--settled`: quiet).
    - **Readings:**
      - "Cars scattered across six sidings" is the yard's other tracks (its generated layout's 1–5 of them, spec D.1), a rake on each, rather than six whatever the yard has.
      - "Assemble them into the train" with every point facing up the line is cars propelled ahead of the engine for the rest of the night. A run-round or trailing points would let them go behind it; that's I.4's engine change, still deferred.
      - "Mixed" cargo is each car its own, from a list (goods, food, timber, machine parts, medicine, coal), part-loaded, rather than the facility's one cargo; the switchyard's crates stay goods.
      - "Unlit" is a rule about finding, not seeing: the sim decides what a lamp has found, so a bot and a player agree on what's there. The crew's lamps are the hand lamps and the engine's headlamp; the cars' own lamps don't reach out of them.
      - "Unstable" is the heap shifting from what's pulled out of it, with a tell first, never at random.
      - A yard track that isn't one of a generated plan's edges is walked at its own limits (none): `PlanRouteWay.Locate` names it `yard<n>` rather than throwing (it threw as soon as an engine went down one).
    - **Not yet:**
      - Run-rounds and trailing points, so picked-up cars could go behind the engine (I.4).
      - Bots fetching a hand lamp from the guard van for the dark heaps.
      - A resumed night (spec E's autosave) doesn't keep picked-up cars: the checkpoint rebuilds the train from its own cars, and the yard's stand where they stood.
      - Art-pass models for the heaps (greybox boxes drawn from the sim's state; the art pass's wreck-yard scenery is separate), and audio for the groan (the tell is visual and on the HUD for now).
      - frontier:11's and deadLines:2's switchyards, drilled, lose a hand to the cold before their first stop's done: as they did before this package.
    - Protocol 18 (standing rakes from the start; the heap record; death cause `Wreckage`). Tests: `FacilityTests` (the standing cars, their ids and limit, a client standing the same, nobody counting them lost; coupled up they're ahead of the engine and paid; the wreck dark until a lamp's on it; pulling pieces makes a heap groan, then shift on whoever's by; a client mirroring the heaps; the residents); `StopCrewTests` (a bot crew fetches the switchyard's standing cars a siding at a time, and salvages what the headlamp finds at the wreck yard). `StopCrewTests`' crate-only and winch-only stops give a switchyard or wreck yard just those modules (a route's own, T44).
188. **Bot crews that get there with the cargo: the car fires, the Fire Flies, and the harness's own night (T81/T114 leftovers).** `dt harness --route frontier:7 --bots 8 --enemies --seconds 1800` ended `run.end = "none"` at 14–16 km with 0.3–0.7 of 4.5 cargo-loads left: 11–12 car fires, 9–11 of them alight (`CarFire:punish`), no stop worked. The traces (`--trace`, now with what each walker's doing about the trouble and the cold) said why.
    - **Not slow: short.** frontier:7 is 24.3 km with a 43.5 min dawn, and the harness started a route's night 400 m along, so the crawl to the gate (1,180 m, the yard all at 2.5 m/s) was 312 s of the 1,800: 1,488 s of night, where the train takes about 2,330 s at the cruise and the boards. Given the time, the same crew got there before too (2,328 s), with nothing left in the cars. `dt harness` now starts a route's night where the game does (`RunTuning.DepartFrom`: just short of the gate), and one started further on (`--start` past the gate, to look at one stretch) puts the crew at their posts (T102's walk aboard is the yard's: started out on the line, the fireman stood on the ballast all night and the fire went out).
    - **No stop worked, rightly:** frontier:7's dawn leaves 2.4 min over the ideal run (`dt linegen generate`'s `dawnSlackMin`), so every facility is one there's no time for (GDD §18: every stop is optional; `StopDriver`'s reckoning). Only a Switchman's points were set back.
    - **Where the fires came from:** the director sends no car fires; they were the Fire Flies' (App. A.5), five swarms and every one ignited, then spread. Each failed a different way:
      - **The guard van had no way in.** `WarmUp.Plan` went in by a car's own rear plate (a car behind it) or the car ahead's, so the van, last, was only ever entered from car 9's plate, into car 9. Its fire was walked over on the roof until it spread forward a car at a time. The last car's own way in is now its rear platform (the van's rear door opens onto it); stepping off the roof onto it is done at `PlatformStep` (0.4) of a roof walk (walked off at the roof's pace you carry 1.4 m in the fall, past its 1.2).
      - **Never outrun.** GDD §21: "lamps off when they swarm. Or drive away"; App. A.5's BREAK OFF: "the train pulls away at speed". The cruise (spec B.3, 14 m/s) is a hair under the flies' pull-away speed (enemies.json `fireFlies.pullAwaySpeed`, 15), so a train at its cruise never shook them. With a swarm on a lit lamp and the line allowing it, the driver's cruise goes `OutrunBy` (1.5 m/s) over the pull-away speed until they've gone (`ConductorBot.OpenCruise`); the fireman fires and vents by the same (or he'd vent it away), and the stop driver doesn't reckon the run home on it (`StopDriver.ReckonTop`). **Reading:** driving away gives up some of the lamp's stopping margin (spec B.3's 14 m/s is the speed at which the train stops for what the lamp shows) for the seconds it takes; it's the contradiction §21 sets, and the bots take it where the line allows the speed.
      - **Everyone to the same trouble.** Each walker took the nearest fire first, so five stood in one car while a swarm three cars off set another alight. Trouble that crewmates are already in to work (two for a fire, one otherwise: `RoofWalkerBot.Covered`) is left to them while there's trouble nobody's in to.
      - **The doors first.** In for trouble, a walker shut every door before working it; it's worked as soon as it's inside now (in its first seconds, before it's taken hold, a fire burns nobody). Only once inside: from the plate, the work walked it at the door it was outside, and kept its give-up clock at nothing.
      - **The extinguisher out of reach.** `Tend` stood just aft of it to take it: in car 1 that's inside the crew lockers (note 173), and three walkers walked at them for 25 s with the car alight round them. Put down spent in the aisle, the next try stood in from it, inside the cargo's stack. And the hands only take Use with nothing else of the car's in reach (`Bodies.Handle`): facing a crew locker or an end door, the press was theirs. `RoofWalkerBot.TakeFrom` finds a spot round it, nearest the walker, clear of the car's fittings and shut doors, facing so the hands reach it and no locker or door does; one it can't get to (out on the steps, up on the stack) is left for a minute. Use is a tap by the bot's own tick (`KitRun.TapEvery`), as the kit's is (note 186): a press held from a tick the hands didn't take was never taken.
      - **The lamp left lit.** The flies go once the car's alight and leave its lamp lit for the next swarm: frontier:3's crew put car 2's fire out again and again. Whoever's working a fire taps its car's lamp out too.
    - **Smaller things the traces showed:** a door shut in a leaver's face is opened again rather than walked into (`WarmUp` `Out`), and a walker warming doesn't shut a door a crewmate is going through (`WarmUp.Passing`, crewmates by it and on the move: counting anyone stood by it, the warmers stood by the door they'd shut kept it open for each other, and a whole crew sat in the cold for 400 s). Neither was reproduced alone in a test; they're guards. The trace says what a walker in the warm is waiting on (the cold, a tunnel, the job it's in for).
    - **The stop driver and a rolling cut.** At deepTerritory:2's Kessel Colliery the Passenger let off the brakes on the cut left on the main line, and it rolled away down the grade. Backing out onto it, `StopDriver.Toward` braked for where it was, not where it was going: the engine settled a couple of metres behind at the cars' own speed for 400 m and never touched them. It closes on a target over the target's own speed now (`away`), so it couples.
    - **deepTerritory:2 at 24.5 km (note 131's leftover):** not seen again. From the gate it worked its first stop (Kessel Colliery's mine head) and was at 12.7 km at 1,469 s; started at 12.5 km with the crew at their posts it worked the depot and the coaling stage and ran through 24.5–24.8 km (a greased climb at 24.8: the driver out sanding, through at 2–5 m/s) to 25.5 km by 1,600 s. The one stretch that did stall there was started at 23 km before the posting fix: the fireman on the ballast, the fire out on the greased climb, the train rolling back. A whole deepTerritory:2 night (71 min to the dawn, over half an hour of the sandbox) wasn't run.
    - **Measured** (`dt harness --route R --bots 8 --enemies`, the night to its end; before is main's code started at the gate the same way):

      | route | before | after |
      |---|---|---|
      | frontier:7 | delivered at 2,328 s, 0 of 4.5 loads, 16 fires (10 alight), 4 deaths, net −1,590 | delivered at 2,186 s, 4.5 loads, 6 fires (1 alight), 1 death, net +3,515 |
      | frontier:3 | delivered at 2,823 s, 4.48 loads, 21 fires (2 alight), 3 deaths, net +2,869 | delivered at 2,824 s, 4.48 loads, 4 fires (1 alight), 1 death, net +3,268 |
      | frontier:11 | derailed on the Sleepers at km 23.8 (1,921 s), 0 loads left aboard, 14 fires (10 alight) | derailed on the same Sleepers (1,931 s), 4.49 loads aboard, 3 fires (2 alight) |

      One night each, and they vary: frontier:3 before the lamp fix delivered 1.84 loads (15 fires, 11 alight), frontier:7 4.49 (2,326 s). frontier:11's derailment is the driver's, before and after alike: braked to 9 m/s for one group of Sleepers, it opened up again, and the next was in the lamp too late from 12.5 m/s (`WatchTheRoad` brakes for Sleepers it sees within 200 m, and only those).
      The 1,800 s harness night of the report, from 400 m: 13.7 km, 0.30 loads, 12 fires (11 alight); with the gate start and the first of these fixes, 22.6 km and 4.49 loads at 1,800 s.
    - **Not yet:**
      - A standing train can't outrun the flies, and at a stop the hands are on the ground at their parts: deepTerritory:2's stops (lamps lit, the flies' depth weight ×2) still lose cargo to fires, and walkers burned below `TooHurt` (35) fight nothing more that night.
      - One extinguisher a car, and a bot uses only the car's own: a fire past 0.85 is left to burn out, and a big one needs "several extinguishers at once" carried in from the next cars.
      - The gunner leaves the gun for every mail bag (its legs' `Into`), and the walkers all go in for every bag: frontier's crews spend much of the night shut in cars waiting at side doors.
      - frontier:11's Sleepers at km 23.8 (above), and the driver opening up between groups of them.
      - frontier:3's second Switchman set the points for a dead line it reached at 14.7 m/s; the driver couldn't stop short, braked down the falling dead line for 2.6 km (the steam against the brake, the brake fading on the grade) and took 477 s to get back. A "the brake isn't holding, vent" call to the fireman made no difference on a test line, so it isn't in.
    - Protocol unchanged; no tuning changed. Tests: `CarFireTests` (into the guard van off its platform; the extinguisher beside the crew lockers, and one put down in the aisle; the lamp out after a fire; the driver drives away from Fire Flies where the line allows, and back to its cruise), `WarmUpTests` (a walker warming leaves the door to those going out), `RollBackTests` (backing onto cars rolling away down a grade, the driver catches them and couples).
190. **Attribution complete: C.9's rows that aren't deaths, and the right contributing action for the Switchman's and the Stoker's derailments (GDD v1.4 App. C.9, D.12, E.5, E.7; §23.1 "Owned"; WP23).** The log wrote a record for every death, GRAB, rescue, rupture, stranding and derailment, but nothing for the other rows of C.9's table, and every derailment named the throttle: a Switchman derail didn't say whether the forward gun was crewed, and a Stoker's runaway read as a bend taken too fast by whoever drove.
    - **New records** (`IncidentKind` Struck, Fire, Nest, Aboard, Runaway, Points, Punished; `IncidentLog.Event`), each written host side on the tick it happens and read out by `IncidentLog.Lines` as "what where. contributing action." like the rest:
      - **Track Doll struck** (`TrackDoll`, its PUNISH; and struck before she could be seen, which breaks her): "Struck the Track Doll at km 4. Throttle: Dave, 38 km/h."
      - **Track debris** short of a derailment (`Sleepers`, the heavy-damage PUNISH): "Ran onto the Sleepers at km 9. Throttle: Dave, 29 km/h." Hit fast enough to derail, the derailment's record says it.
      - **Fire Flies fire** (`FireFlies`, at ignition, when it starts a fire): "Fire Flies set car 3 alight at km 9. Lamp lit by Priya." / "Nobody lit that lamp."
      - **Followers nest** (`Follower`, its PUNISH; the carrier kept host side as it drops off): "Followers nested in car 2 at km 11. Carried aboard by Sam."
      - **Grumbler aboard** (`Grumbler`, the tick its casting lands loaded in a car): "Grumbler craned aboard car 2 at Kessel Foundry. Crane: Dave." (the crane's last operator, who let the lift go).
      - **Stoker runaway** (`Stoker`, the tick it starts to FEED): "Stoker got into the firebox down the stack at km 12. Firebox last tended: Priya, unattended 52 s." ("through the open door" for one that came in that way).
      - **The Switchman's points** short of a derailment (its PUNISH: the train down the dead line, or the points split under a crawling engine): "Switchman threw the train down the dead line at km 7. Forward cannon not crewed."
      - **Any other PUNISH that holds nobody** (A.9 "every PUNISH writes a record"): `Enemy.Punished`, virtual, defaults to "Punished by the X at …. Nearest crew: Y, 12 m." (C.9's last row). The car fire (its cause is the Fire Flies' record; what it does after is deaths and cars lost) and the marsh (its target's death is the record) write none. One that holds someone is their death's record, as before.
    - **Derailment blame:** `World.Derail(why, actor, action)` keeps the derail's contributing action (`DerailActor`, `DerailAction` with `{actor}`), and the cause card (`IncidentLog.CauseCard`), the report's derailment line (`Run.Finish`) and every death in it (at the speed it came off) read it. `Derail(why)` is the throttle, as before ("Nobody on the throttle." for nobody, where the report said "Throttle: nobody.").
      - **Switchman:** `IncidentLog.ForwardCannon`: a gun facing forward on a vehicle on the engine, and the lowest-id living crewmate at it (`Guns.MannedGun`) on the derail tick. "Consist derailed at km 7, 32 km/h. The Switchman threw the points under it at 32 km/h (over 25 km/h they throw a train off). Forward cannon crewed by Priya. Recovery not scheduled." / "Forward cannon not crewed." / "No forward cannon on the consist."
