      - **Stoker:** `World.Overspeed(why)`, called for every derailment that's a speed taken too fast (a generated line's bend, a lineside board's bend, the Sleepers): with a Stoker feeding, the cause is its runaway and the action the firebox (`IncidentLog.Firebox`: `Attribution.Tender`, who last fired, vented, opened the firebox door or clubbed a Stoker in it, and the seconds since). "Consist derailed at km 14, 68 km/h. The Stoker ran away with it: took the 45 km/h bend at 68 km/h, 23 km/h too fast. Firebox last tended: Priya, unattended 40 s. Recovery not scheduled." The rupture's row stays `Fireman` (fired or vented).
      - Washouts and weak bridges stay the throttle's (C.9 has no row of their own; it's the track's danger the driver had the boards for).
    - **Bookmarks:** a PUNISH bookmark that held nobody goes beside the record its enemy wrote that tick (`Run.MarkBookmarks`), rather than on a line of its own.
    - **Readings:** "Who ran the crane for that lift" is the crane's last operator when the casting lands loaded (the lift's release). "Whether the forward cannon was crewed" is on the derail tick, not over the Switchman's whole telegraph. "Stoker runaway" is the FEED starting, the first moment the runaway is a fact; "how long unattended" is from the last tending to that tick (or to the derail tick). A derailment for speed with a Stoker feeding is the Stoker's whatever the throttle was set to, since FEED opens the regulator past what's set (App. A.5).
    - **Not yet:** the HUD's report draws the new lines in the dim ink of a GRAB nobody died of; their bookmarks (the Track Doll's, the nest's) are only taken where someone can see the thing (as before). The Gaunt walking off with a corpse (A.6) had no row because it wasn't built; #149 built it (`Body.TakenBy`, note 195), and it still has no report row.
    - Protocol 20 (19 is the sound package's): the report carries the new incident kinds. No tuning changed. Tests: `IncidentEventTests` (each new record and its actor and action; clubbing the Stoker is tending; a bend too fast with a Stoker feeding is its runaway on the cause card, without one the throttle; a victimless PUNISH's bookmark beside its line), `DerailCauseTests` (a Switchman derailment names the forward gunner, or that nobody was on it, and writes no second record; the split points' record), `AttributionTests` (the Stoker's runaway derailment in the report and its deaths; a non-death line's text).
191. **Recorded sounds (the audio checklist's pass, merged with main in note 233).** The mixer could only synthesise; the audio pass brings hundreds of recorded one-shots, several takes each, and loops. A layer can be `"source": "sample"`:
    - **Takes** are Ogg Opus (mono, 48 kHz, `ffmpeg -c:a libopus`) under `content/audio/samples`, the sounds folder's sibling (`SoundBank.Samples`). `"sample"` names a folder (its `*.opus` are the takes; each instance picks one by its seed) or one file. `OggOpus` is a small page reader over Concentus that honours pre-skip and the last granule position, so a take is exactly what was encoded and a loop's seam is where it was cut. Takes decode on first play (about 50x real time) and are kept as 16 bit; a long one decodes on a worker (note 192), and `SoundBank.Preload` decodes a sound's takes up front.
    - **On the layer:** `rate` (value or curve; 2 is an octave up in half the time; the pitch envelope and vibrato scale it), `pitchJitter` (semitones) and `gainJitter` (dB), each picked once per instance from its seed. Filters, tremolo, envelope, delay and gain curves work as on any layer. A loop loops its take; a one-shot plays it once.
    - **When a one-shot ends.** Synth layers have no end of their own, so `duration` (now optional, 1 s unset) is still their length. A sample layer ends with its take, at its rate, after its delay. An all-sample one-shot ends with its takes and ignores `duration` (a 0.2 s step doesn't hold a voice through 0.8 s of silence; a 3 s crash isn't cut at 1 s); a mixed one at the later of its takes' end and a given `duration` (`SoundInstance.Finished`). A virtual voice's takes keep time, so it ends too.
    - **A missing sample is silence, not a crash:** the bank's `LastError` names it as the sound loads (so `EverySoundFileLoads` catches a typo), or a take that won't decode when first played. A name can't reach outside the samples root.
    - **One sample source, two recordings.** A sample layer that names a take plays it; one that names none plays its instance's own clip (`SoundInstance.Clip`, from `ClipSeconds` on: note 174's opera), and a sound playing a clip is over when the clip is.
    - `dt audio render --sound <name> [--param k=v]` plays one sound alone to a WAV and spectrogram, with its length, takes and level. `SampleTests` pin the decode (length, pitch, phase), packets across pages, the end rule, rate, a seamless loop, and takes and jitter by seed.
192. **The mix and voice lines at L1: long takes off the mixing thread, the work's drone on its tier, spaces, tunnel voice.**
    - **Decoding without a stall.** A take whose file is over `SampleLibrary.InlineBytes` (10 KB, about a second at 64 kbit/s) decodes on a worker, two at a time. Its layer waits in silence, then plays the take from its first sample, its own clock that much behind the sound's (`LayerState._late`). Which take plays is the seed's alone (`SoundInstance.Takes` names it before it's decoded), so only *when* depends on the machine. A short take, or one a worker already has, is decoded or waited for at once. Offline renders set `InlineBytes` to `long.MaxValue`, so they're the same every run. `SoundBank.Prefetch` starts a sound's takes on a worker; GameAudio prefetches the music at startup. `SampleTests` step the worker by hand.
    - **Music's tier (T7, decided 1 Oct).** `Mixer.Tiers` is 7. In mix.json every other tier ducks it: −12 dB under a tell, −9 under voice, −6 and −3 under train state and actions, −2 under the bed and the world (which are nearly always there). It has no rule of its own, so it ducks nothing, voice least of all. `tierDb` is each tier's fader (music −6; its takes are levelled to −30 LUFS). GameAudio plays `ui-music.drone`, if installed, flat, from when the run leaves the yard while the work goes on: it stops as the train comes off the rails or the night's over (GDD v1.4 E.1, opera means the night is over; E.9's outro has no music). The opera's bus (tier 0, note 174) is apart from it: nothing ducks the opera, everything ducks the drone.
    - **Spaces (spec A.6).** `GameAudio.SpaceOf` decides from the listener's ear. Inside a car's walls is "car", even in a tunnel. In a tunnel's bore (within 6 m of the main line) is "tunnel", the open-backed cab included. Then "cab", then "facility" along a facility's yard, else "outside", which is dry. Each space's response is synthesised from spaces.json (`ImpulseResponse.Synthesize`): early reflections where the walls are, then noise decaying in Decay (its highs in Decay × HighDecay), a lowcut, unit energy at WetDb, and the last 30% eased out. `Convolver` is uniformly partitioned overlap-save: 512-point FFTs shared by both ears, and a vectorised multiply-add over the partitions. Every positioned voice goes in at its tier's send, post-fader; flat sounds (UI, music, the radio) stay dry. A new space's reverb starts empty while the last one rings out. **Cost**, Release on AVX2: the tunnel's 1.8 s (338 partitions) is 18 ms per second of audio, 0.2 ms of a 90 fps frame; the car's is 0.08 ms. Responses are built at startup (the tunnel's takes 12 ms). `dt audio render [--sound s] --space tunnel`.
    - **Tunnel voice (GDD §22).** On top of note 183's band-pass on voice under a tunnel (voice.json's "tunnel"), the space's compressor and reverb. "Compressed proximity voice" is read as the sound of it, not its range: the 26 m cutoff stays. The tunnel's `voice` compressor works on a positioned tier-2 voice's level at the ear (−26 dB threshold, 3:1, +8 dB makeup). 4 m against 16.5 m goes from 8.2 dB apart to 4.0, and the far voice comes up 5 dB. Its `muted` list takes the outside (wind, bed-wind, world-night, world-wind, rain outside, thunder) to nothing over 0.3 s and keeps the tunnel's own sounds. The tunnel's reverb takes voice at a send of 0.9. `dt voice bench --space tunnel`.
    - **The tell audit** now meters the tells' own share of the reverb apart (`Mixer.TellReverbStem`, convolved separately only while a tap is on). A tell's room is the tell's; the bed's room still counts against it. With that split, the cab's reverb costs the cab's tells under half a dB. Tells go in at lower sends than voice (0.25 to 0.35) so their rhythm and direction stay crisp (spec A.4 rules 2 and 4).
193. **The train's, the world's and the places' cues in the game (GameAudio.Train, GameAudio.Outside).** Every checklist cue on the train, the line, the stops, the radio and the Holdouts is played from replicated state (out/audio/hooks-map.md), on its edge or held while its state lasts.
    - **The bed's recordings stand in for its synths, never on top of them.** `GameAudio.Synth` plays a synthesised bed sound (boiler-roar, chuff, wheel-rail, brake, wind, safety-valve, boiler-strain, vent-hiss, boiler-burst) only while none of the cues that replace it is installed, and stops it if one is installed while it plays; the slack's run-in and run-out each fall back to the synth's clunk. The exhaust beats four times a turn of 1.7 m wheels (chuff.json's figure), the rods once a turn, a joint under each axle every 12 m rail. While a recorded roll plays and the joint isn't installed, `rail-joint.json` (the synth roll's own click) keeps the click-clack.
    - **Levels.** As recorded, the wheels and the wind sat 20 dB over the synths they replace and buried every tell in `AudioTests`' benches. Their levels are `tools/audio/install.py`'s `CUE_GAIN_DB` (matched to the synths in the chaos and tells benches), and the wind's and the wheels' takes are lowpassed (`LAYER_EXTRAS`, 1.5 and 2.5 kHz) as the synths were, so the tells' bands stay clear.
    - **A derailment is its wreck (the director's note on state-derail).** Read off note 162's physics, the poses every machine has (note 233): each car's wheels off the rail as it starts to slide sideways, a hit where its velocity jumps (on the ground when the jump's more up and down than along, into another car otherwise), going over past 55°, steel on the rails while it slides on them and grinding through the ballast once it's off, a coupling torn as its cars pull apart past the slack, and settling as each comes to rest. Each is its own cue where it happens (state-derail's climb, impact, collide, tip, rail-scrape, grind, tear, settle); where one isn't installed, the synth crash and grind stand in (wreck-crash, wreck-grind).
    - **A Call Out** is note 179's `Holdout.Calls`, played as the count goes up: a call or a shout in the occupant's voice set, or a bout of banging. The eight sets are dealt by the night's seed and given out by player id, so no two players on a run share one; holdout-shout and holdout-bang are the synth stand-ins under them.
    - **Chosen here, not modelled:** which animals a livestock car carries (by its id), the night's far things, thunder and gusts (at random), and a weak bridge's groan (from cars over its limit, and speed over its crossing speed). Still unhookable for want of state: graded signal on your own set (it's live or dead: static plays while you key it where it's dead; a crewmate breaking up is VoiceChat's).
194. **The opera, for real: CC0 recordings of the public-domain works, cut to their climaxes (GDD v1.4 App. E.6; WP25).** The user's call: replace note 174's synthesised tracks with public-domain compositions in well-known recordings that are CC0 (real orchestras and singers, Musopen's among them), each hit on the piece's own big moment. E.6's rule stands: the file's own licence must be CC0 1.0; a "public domain" mark on an old recording isn't enough.
    - **The intake runs in CI.** The cloud sessions' proxy refuses Commons, upload.wikimedia.org, Musopen, the Internet Archive and Freesound; GitHub's runners don't. `.github/workflows/music-intake.yml` starts on a push that changes `tools/audio/music_intake.json` (the request: which candidates, how many) to an intake branch, or by hand (`workflow_dispatch`, once it's on the default branch); never on a pull request. It installs ffmpeg, builds `dt`, checks the list (`--dry-run`) and the cut path (`--offline-test`), runs the intake, runs `MusicManifestTests`, `FrontEndTests` and `Ballast.Audio.Tests`, uploads the evidence (and the sources as downloaded) as artifacts, and commits `content/audio/music` back to the branch that asked (by hand: to `music-intake`, replaced each time). Its push touches only `content/audio/music`, which the paths filter leaves out.
    - **The candidates** (`tools/audio/fetch_music.py`, `CANDIDATES`): 26 public-domain works over E.6's four moods (E.6's own 14 first, then the Lacrimosa, the Funeral March, the Barcarolle, the Mountain King, the 1812, Mozart's Dies irae, Bald Mountain, Danse macabre, Beethoven's Fifth, the Carmen prelude, the Hallelujah, the Barber overture), each with Commons titles to try first, searches (and Musopen's uploads of the composer) when none of them passes, patterns a found title must contain, and the hit as a range in the file (seconds from the start or end, or fractions of the length: recordings differ) with a note of the moment ("Ridi, Pagliaccio"'s top note, the Dies irae's first bass-drum strikes, the can-can's whip-crack climb, the Valkyries' brass entry, "Vincerò!"). A CI probe found the Musopen overture to *Orpheus in the Underworld* (it ends with the can-can) and Offenbach's Barcarolle CC0; the Ride of the Valkyries and the 1920s *William Tell* on Commons are "Public domain" only, and refused.
    - **The licence test** reads each file page's wikitext through the API: it needs a `{{cc-zero}}` (or `{{CC0}}`) template on the page itself; the extmetadata (`License`, `LicenseShortName`, `LicenseUrl`) is recorded beside it. PD-old, PD-US, PD-USGov or a public-domain mark alone is a refusal. **Reading:** a rights holder's own `{{PD-self}}` release is a dedication but not CC0 1.0, which is all E.6 and the manifest test accept; the report lists such files as near misses, with the template, for a person to decide (a CC0 statement from the performer would let one in). Renderings of MIDI or notation software (title, description or wikitext saying so) are refused: E.6 wants performers. Commons rate-limits (a probe met HTTP 429 after about eight searches), so requests go at least 2.5 s apart with a descriptive User-Agent, 429 and 503 are retried honouring Retry-After, and a search fetches wikitext only for titles that match.
    - **Evidence** (E.6 "Manifest"): per track, in `intake/_sources/music/<id>/` (gitignored, in the artifact): the download, its SHA-256 (and its SHA-1 checked against Commons'), the extmetadata, the wikitext, and the page as rendered (the archived licence page). Committed: `content/audio/music/evidence/<id>.json` (the metadata, the CC0 templates found, the page's licence section, the revision, the source's SHA-256, the window and the analysis), and the manifest's `evidence` gains `sourceSha256`, `page` and `record`; `source` is the Commons file page.
    - **The hit and the window.** Within the hint's range the intake picks the 10 ms block with the biggest rise in the bright (over 3 kHz) level against the 300 ms before it, plus half of how near the next second is to the loudest second in the range (a crash into the biggest bar beats a consonant in a quiet one). It cuts up to 24 s before and 21 s after (at least 4.5 and 19.5: the replay's 3 s lead and the 18 s from the hit to the sequence's end, with a second's slack): more lead than the build uses, for E.5's hit on the final apex, which can be most of the film in. Two-pass `loudnorm` to −16 LUFS (true peak −1.5 dB, linear where it can be), a 50 ms fade in and 1 s out, mono Opus at 48 kHz, 64 kb/s. Then `dt audio music` (`src/DarkTerritory.Cli/MusicCommands.cs`) moves each hit onto the sharpest onset within a second (`AudioBench.SharpestOnset`, the manifest test's own measure) until it stays put, no more than 1 s from the analysis, measures BS.1770 loudness the game's way and sets the gain, takes the SHA-256, and writes the manifest and `CREDITS.md`. A track whose hit won't settle, that's too short, or whose loudness doesn't measure is left out with the reason.
    - **The pool** (`MusicFiles.Pool`, used by both `dt audio music` and `dt audio opera`): with 4 or more CC0 recordings (E.6's demo bar) they replace the synthesised fallback, and a mood none of them covers keeps its synthesised track (the speed weighting wants every mood: `TheDemoPoolHasEveryMood`); with fewer, the fallback stays and the recordings join it. A rerun keeps earlier recordings it didn't retake; files and evidence the pool no longer names are deleted. `dt audio opera` rewrites only the synthesised tracks the pool still has. At most 16 tracks (`maxTracks`).
    - **Opus, not WAV** (`Ballast.Audio.OggOpus`, `AudioClip.Load`): a 45 s window is about 2 MB as 22 kHz 16-bit WAV and about 360 KB as Opus, so 16 tracks are about 6 MB rather than 30. The decoder is the Ogg framing (pages, lacing, OpusHead's pre-skip and output gain, the last granule for the true length; mapping family 0, mono or stereo averaged) around Concentus, the managed Opus already used for voice, so no new dependency. `OggOpusTests` round-trips a stream written with Concentus's encoder and RFC 7845 framing (length to the sample, pitch, level).
    - **Alignment:** unchanged (note 174): `Opera.Cue` starts a track at `StartFor(replayLeadSeconds)`, so the manifest's `hit` lands on the replay's moment of derailment; whatever moment a later cue lines up (E.5's final apex), it lines up the same `hit`.
    - **Credits screen** (E.6: "the credits screen lists every performer anyway"): CREDITS on the title screen; `Screen.Credits` lists every manifest track, two lines each (work, composer and year; performers, licence, and the Commons page or the script that made it), scrolling, Esc back. The app hands the front end the manifest (`FrontEnd.Music`). The pixel font has no accents, so accented capitals draw as their plain letters ("LA DONNA E MOBILE"), curly quotes as straight. `dt screenshot --menu credits` renders it.
    - **Tests:** `MusicManifestTests` as before (CC0-1.0 with the file's hash, fields, every mood, loudness, the hit the sharpest onset where it says, the sequence covered, the credits), with a recording's source a Commons page rather than a repo path, plus `EveryRecordingCarriesItsLicenceEvidence` (source SHA-256, page, the record's CC0 templates and licence section) and `EveryHitLiesInsideItsTrackWithTheLeadTheSequenceNeeds` (the hit in [inPoint, outPoint], at least the replay's lead past the in-point). `FrontEndTests.TheCreditsListEveryTrackWithItsPerformersLicenceAndSource`; `OggOpusTests`.
    - **The first run, and what it changed** (run 37119988938, commit dd89caf): 8 CC0 recordings, all but one Musopen's (the can-can overture, the Anvil Chorus, the Toreador Song, the Carmen prelude, the Funeral March, and a National Library of Sweden phonograph cylinder of "La donna è mobile"), but two mislabelled and the performers wrong:
      - *Mislabels:* the loose title patterns let Åse's Death in as the Mountain King ("peer gynt") and the Egmont overture in as the Fifth ("beethoven"). A candidate now has `must` patterns that all have to be in the title (the work's own words) and `never` patterns that refuse it (another movement's name); `--dry-run` checks them against titles seen in CI (`MATCH_CASES`). Both pieces are candidates in their own right now, labelled honestly: Åse's Death a Lament (the strings' fortissimo peak, 33-62% in), the Egmont overture Doom (the final Allegro con brio's fanfare, 95-25 s from the end).
      - *Performers:* the Commons "Artist" field is often the composer ("Performed by Frédéric Chopin") or a source blurb (the Swedish library's). Now: the page's performer field, then author or artist, then "performed by ..." in the description, never anything with the composer's name, a source or a URL; a Musopen upload naming nobody is "Musopen (performers uncredited)"; else "Unknown performers (uploaded by <user>)".
      - *Finding them:* searches met the rate limit and missed. The intake now scans categories first (`list=categorymembers`, files and subcategories, paced, at most 260 requests): `Category:Musopen` and Musopen's audio categories, the opera audio categories, and each candidate's own (`categories`), then fetches wikitext only for the titles a candidate matches; a search is the last resort. Every matching file that isn't CC0 is reported as a near miss with why (`report.json` `nearMisses`), so what isn't available as CC0 is on record.
      - *Hits:* three of the eight landed where the music falls away or in a quiet bar (the Egmont's was in the Fifth's range). The analysis now also scores how much louder the second after the hit is than the one before, and each cut is checked (`hitCheck`: the 2 s after against the 2 s before, and the loudness rank of the second after; "weak" for a person to listen to first). The manifest's note gives the hit's time in the original (m:ss), the range it was found in and the moment it's meant as. The Anvil Chorus, Carmen prelude and Funeral March ranges were set from the pieces' structure.
      - A full run replaces the recorded set (`dt audio music --replace`), so a mislabel from before goes; `dt audio opera` runs after it to fill the demo's 4 and every mood from the fallback if the recordings don't.
    - **The second run** (run 37122568018, commit 36af685; 104 categories, 1,145 audio files scanned): 7 CC0 recordings (the Anvil Chorus, "La donna è mobile", the Toreador Song, the Funeral March, Åse's Death, the Egmont overture, and the Carmen prelude under the Habanera's name) and the synthesised Infernal Galop and William Tell. The famous arias were mostly "Public domain" only, refused. Fixed for the third:
      - *Pinned sources:* a candidate can pin files (`pinned`: a title and, optionally, a hit range inside it) past its patterns, still licence-checked. The strict patterns had lost the Musopen *Orpheus in the Underworld* overture (its title names neither the galop nor the can-can); it's pinned for the Infernal Galop with the hit on the can-can's final climax at its end. The Carmen prelude is pinned to its own candidate.
      - *Categories don't match:* run 2 handed the Carmen prelude to the Habanera because both listed "Audio files of Carmen" and being in a candidate's category was enough. Only the title patterns (or a pin) match now; categories only say where to scan.
      - *Performer parse:* an empty field ("|author =" then a new line) was read as the next field's name ("|title ="). Values now stop at the line and never start with "|"; `--dry-run` checks the parse on pages laid out like the runs' (`PERFORMER_CASES`) and on every committed evidence record's wikitext, which records now keep (up to 20 KB).
      - *The open pool* (`OPEN`): 39 more well-known dramatic public-domain works across the moods (Bach's Toccata in D minor, the New World finale and Largo, Mozart's Confutatis and Rex tremendae, the Grieg concerto, Tchaikovsky's Fourth and Pathétique, the Moonlight's Presto, Beethoven's Seventh and Fifth finales and the Ode to Joy, Brahms's Fifth Hungarian Dance, Light Cavalry, the Sorcerer's Apprentice, Nimrod, Swan Lake, Pomp and Circumstance, the Radetzky March, the Great Gate of Kiev, Zadok the Priest and more), each with its climax as the hit, matched only against the scanned files (no title guesses or searches). Every candidate's best CC0 file is found first, a file to one candidate; then the picks go a mood at a time in turn, E.6's table first, then the named extras, then the open pool in order of recognisability, to `maxTracks` (16).
      - The job log now has the kept tracks with each hit's time and check, the refusals, and the near misses.
    - **The third run** (run 37123738964) took in the Pathétique sonata and symphony, the Blue Danube, the Tchaikovsky concerto and the Carmen prelude again, then failed `EachTrackIsMeasuredAndNormalisedToMinusSixteenLufs`: the sonata's gain to −16 LUFS put its peaks over the top, so nothing was pushed. loudnorm's dynamic mode had left the piano well under the target. Now the intake measures each encoded cut (ffmpeg `ebur128`), and one more than 0.3 dB short is encoded again with the rest of the gain through the limiter (`limiterGainDb` in the evidence); every cut goes through a limiter last, lowered until the decoded peak is under −1.5 dBFS (Opus overshoots a hard transient: the offline test's noise crash decoded at +2 dBFS from a −2 dBFS limit; `limitDb`, `decodedPeakDb` in the evidence). `dt audio music` caps the gain so the decoded peak stays at or under −1 dBFS (`MusicFiles.Gain`), recording what that leaves it short of −16 LUFS as `shortfallDb`; a track more than 3 dB short is left out. The test holds the same: −16 LUFS, or the recorded shortfall (at most 3 dB) with the peak what stops it, and never a peak over the top. (Run 2's "Habanera" was the Carmen prelude through the shared category, never a Habanera; none has turned up as CC0.) The job log ends with a short table (`--summary`: id, hit, jump, rank, weak, performers; the refusals, near misses and what became of each pinned file) right before the tests, and a failed test prints why.
    - **Not yet:** the intake hasn't run from here (no route to Commons), so the committed tracks are still note 174's five synthesised ones until the workflow's push lands; the hits it finds want an ear (the analysis is a heuristic near a hint, and a search-found recording may time its climax differently); the candidates' first-try titles are guesses at Commons' names, with the searches behind them. Mono only, as before.
195. **Protocol 21 for #149's body record (an audit fix).** #149 gave the body record an eleventh field, `TakenBy` (who holds a body or loot: the Gaunt walking it out of the car, App. A.6), and left the protocol at 20. A host and a joiner of the two builds would both say 20 and then misread each other's bodies (`WorldRecords` reads `f[10]` unguarded) instead of the joiner being refused at the lobby. Now 21. Numbers 191-194 are left to the branches that hold them (194 is the WP25 opera's); 19 stays reserved for the sound package.
196. **The last of F.3's upgrades: lamp armour, gun cooling, repair kit charges, radio range, the powered switch thrower (spec F.3, A.5; GDD §17, §23).** Five upgrades were bought and saved with an empty `effect` and did nothing: a purchase that does nothing is a bug. Each now goes through `Campaign.Apply` as note 184's did, a tuning value in campaign.json (each citing F.3), so every machine gets the same night from the upgrade list the Welcome already carries.
    - **Lamp armour** (`lampOut` 0.5): the Climbers still smash the forward lamp coming over the tender into the cab (§23 "lights fail", note 183, the only thing that smashes it), but it can be lit again after 22.5 s, not 45. The time was a code default; it's now `enemies.json climbers.lampOutSeconds`.
    - **Gun cooling** (`foul` 0.5): `combat.json guns.foulChance` halved, 2% a shot (5% in the wet). The foul is a hash of the tick and the gun under the chance, so a cooled gun fouls on a subset of the shots a hot one would.
    - **Repair kit charges** (`repair` 0.6): **Reading:** a kit is never used up. Mending the boiler (`CrewActions`, `repairSeconds`) and opening a Holdout's lock (D.7) both leave it in hand; nothing in the sim counts uses, so a charge has nothing to count, and adding charges would mean making the kit consumable, which F.3 doesn't ask and which would make losing kits (Stranded, GDD v1.4) come sooner. Read as a better-stocked kit that works faster: a ruptured boiler is mended in 15 s, not 25. The lock's slower quiet opening is left as D.7 has it (that trade is the point of it).
    - **Radio range** (`radioReach` +150 m, the one additive effect): spec A.5's radio already covers the whole run and "dies in tunnels and mine spurs". **Reading:** the upgrade carries it a distance into them before it dies. `train.json kit.radioReach` (0) is how far in from a tunnel's mouth (`Route.DeepInTunnel`) or from a mine spur's points (`Run.Underground`, along the spur's path) a radio still works; the host's voice routing (`HostSession.ForwardVoice`) passes it to both tests, so it stays host-side and nothing changes on the wire. At 0 both are exactly what they were.
    - **Powered switch thrower** (`switchThrower`, fitted to the cab like the handrails: `train.json composition.switchThrower` and `thrower`): F.3 "removes the ground excursion at junctions". A lever in the cab (`InteractableKind.Points`, the driver's side behind the tool rack, drawn as a red-handled lever when fitted; `dt screenshot --upgrades poweredSwitchThrower --view points`). Held there with Use for route.json's `throwSeconds`, it throws the nearest points within `thrower.reach` (200 m) ahead of the engine's front on the main line (`SwitchStands.PointsAhead`), through the same host path as a stand (`SwitchStands.CrewAct`, `World.SwitchThrows`), so it's intent (Use) and bots could use it the same way. Only with the train at or under `thrower.maxSpeed` (5 m/s, 18 km/h), and the points still won't move with a wheel on them. The HUD says what it'll do: the points' distance and which way, or to slow down, or that none are in reach. Unfitted, the lever's there in the shape but throws nothing and has no prompt.
      - **Reading:** "removes the ground excursion" is nobody stepping down, not taking junctions at speed: a crew still slows for one, as it would to put someone on the ground. The speed limit is a tuning value, not a safety need (the points' own lock already refuses a throw under a wheel).
    - **Not yet:** the driver bot doesn't use the thrower: the stop crew's switch legs (`StopCrew` SetBack/Held/Clear) still send the shunter out. The HUD's thrower prompt has no Game test (`PrototypeSession` takes no upgrades); the art pass has no modelled lever (the greybox draws it). The radio's reach has no HUD cue for where it dies.
    - Protocol unchanged (21): the upgrades travel in the Welcome as before, the new tunings are content (hash-checked), and the lever is a new interactable kind in the engine's shape, which isn't sent. Tuning: `enemies.json climbers.lampOutSeconds` (45, the old code default), `train.json kit.radioReach` (0), `composition.switchThrower` (false) and `thrower`; no spec-pinned number changed. Tests: `UpgradeTests` (each upgrade's tuning, and every upgrade sold does something; the lamp lit again sooner; a cooled gun fouls about half as often; the kit mends faster and stays in hand; the radio heard 60 m inside a tunnel with it and not without, and dead past its reach; down a mine spur the same; the thrower throws the points ahead from the cab fitted, and not unfitted, out of reach, too fast, or over the points). `CampaignTests` no longer expects lamp armour to do nothing.
197. **A swing at nothing is seen too (note 146's "not yet"; audit order P1).** T121's HitConfirm gave a crewmate's blow a reader when it landed: every client plays their swing clip round it (`SceneArt.Crewmate`'s `swung`). A swing that hit nothing was never on the wire, since the host decides blows from intent (App. C.2), so to everyone else the crewmate stood still while they swung at a Dragger's limb that had gone, or at the dark.
    - **The host logs every swing.** `World.Swing` adds a `SwingEvent` (its id, the tick it started, who) each time a swing starts, landed or not, so one per melee recovery (`enemies.json melee.swingSeconds`) while Swing is held. They're kept as long as hits are (`combat.json hits.keepSeconds`), so a dropped snapshot doesn't lose one, and replicated as `RecordKind.Swing` (tick, who). Clients mirror them with the hits (`World.MirrorHits`).
    - **Drawn as a landed blow is.** The blow is decided on the tick the swing starts, so a landed swing's HitConfirm and its SwingEvent share a tick, and either times the clip alike. `GreyboxScene.Swung` takes the newest of the two.
    - **Prediction is untouched.** The event is host state on the world record, not the player's; the swinger's own first-person swing still plays from their own input (note 146).
    - Protocol 22 (a new record kind). Tests: `HitConfirmTests.ASwingAtNothingIsStillSeenByEveryClient` (one swing per recovery, no hit, mirrored to a client).
198. **The set pieces heard (notes 185 and 187's "not yet"; audit order P1).** The grain spout, the herd, the hose and the wreck yard's heaps worked and were drawn, but made no sound of their own: a heap's groan, the 3 s warning before it shifts onto whoever's beside it, was only dust and a HUD line.
    - **Four loops** (`content/audio/sounds`), each where its set piece is, for as long as the run's state says so (`GameAudio.SetPieces`): `spout-pour` at the spout's mouth while grain comes down (a dry rushing roar, the chute drumming under it; tier 2); `herd` at the pen while the herd's stirred up (`Site.Stirred`: lowing in irregular breaths, hooves on the boards; tier 2, spec D.2's "constant noise"); `hose-leak` at the fluid gantry's stand while it's leaking (a hard high hiss that spits; tier 2); `heap-groan` over a heap while it groans (`WreckHeap.Groan`: a deep creak sliding down, metal shrieking over it; **tier 1**, since it's the tell for a crush). (Since the sound package's merge, note 233, the spout, the herd and the hose are its recorded loops, and only the heap's groan is this note's.)
    - **Reading:** the heap's groan is a tell, so tier 1, but it isn't in `AudioBench.TellBands`' chaos audit: it only ever sounds at a standstill, at a facility, where the bed is the idling engine, not the moving train the audit stresses. `AudioTests.TheSetPiecesAreHeard` holds each 20 dB over that idle beside a stopped train.
    - The run's set pieces and heaps are already replicated (protocol 17, 18), so every client hears the same; nothing new on the wire.
    - **Headless:** `dt audio render --scenario sound:<name> --speed 0` auditions any sound file alone, 6 m off the listener, over a standing train (WAV and spectrogram).
199. **A dropped joiner is told, and doesn't see itself (audit order P2's rehearsal).** Before the two-machine test, the 4 Oct rehearsal ran it here: two real app windows (xvfb, software Vulkan), one `--host`ing, one `--join`ing over UDP. At the software renderer's 0 fps the joiner's link timed out (8 s of silence, `DatagramOptions.TimeoutSeconds`), and what it showed then was wrong in two ways, either of which a slow machine stalling for 8 s, the host quitting, or the wifi dropping would hit.
    - **It drew itself.** On a drop `ClientSession` clears `PlayerId`, and `RemoteIds` filtered on it, so the joiner's own player came back as a remote: listed again ("CREW 2" under YOU) and drawn round its own eyes. It keeps who the host said it was (`_was`) and filters on that.
    - **It never said so.** `NetPlaySession.Lost` was set only while the client still read as connected, which the drop had just ended, so the HUD sat on "connecting…" with "HOST" in the corner. `ClientSession.Dropped` (a disconnect after the welcome) sets it: "CONNECTION LOST", and "NO LINK" in red where the ping was.
    - **The joiner's lobby lines.** A joiner was shown the host's "EVERYONE IN? DRIVE OUT OF THE YARD" (and, with no ping, "A PRIVATE NIGHT"); it reads "THE HOST DRIVES OUT WHEN EVERYONE'S IN".
    - **The ping** is just "100 MS" at the big size: "PING 100 MS" ran into the route strip at 1280 wide.
    - Rerun, the joiner held its link ("2 aboard, ping 100 ms") and its screen read right. Tests: `NetcodeTests.ADroppedClientKnowsItAndDoesntSeeItselfAsCrew`.
200. **`dt playthrough`, and the train's own frames (the 5 October audit).** The art checklist rates each piece in a staged view. Nothing showed what a crew sees over a real night.
    - **`dt playthrough --route frontier:7 [--minutes 20] [--every 90]`:**
      - plays a solo night with the director's enemies, the train driven by the line's own authority (`LineGen.Ride`, as the app's `--ride`);
      - photographs every encounter as it happens (each enemy's Alert, Telegraph, Grab and Punish), from beside it, or down the aisle if it's in a car, with the game's own scene;
      - takes a chase shot every `--every` seconds;
      - writes frames and `index.json` (what, when, where) to out/playthrough.
    - **What it found: the chase view and the derailment were filming a switchyard.**
      - A switchyard's standing cars (note 187) are frames past the train's own, so `Views.Chase`'s "last car" was one of them, 17 km down the line on frontier:7.
      - The Tab chase view and the derailment's replay (`DerailSequence.ReplayCamera`) looked at an empty siding.
      - Worse, `Wreck.Begin` made a wreck body of every frame: a derailment threw the standing cars off their siding with the train, and the orbit round the wreck's middle (`Views.Wreck`) framed nothing.
    - **Fixed:**
      - `Wreck.Begin` skips standing rakes, in its bodies and its links.
      - `Views.Train(frames, StandingCar)` gives the train's own frames, and the chase view and the replay use it.
    - **Verified:**
      - `FacilityTests.ADerailmentLeavesTheSwitchyardsStandingCarsWhereTheyStand`;
      - `ChaseCameraTests` (frontier:7: the chase view and the replay within 250 m of the engine);
      - both fail without the fix;
      - a playthrough's chase shots show the train.
    - **What else it found: a car fire's TELEGRAPH couldn't be seen** (App. C.5 smoke before flame; the fairness rule says a tell must be perceivable). The smoulder's smoke was grey at 0.24, as bright as lamp-lit planking, and the car looked untouched.
      - It's `Effects.SmoulderGrey` now, at HDR level (the effects pass isn't lit, and the lit walls run over 1), denser and bigger, pooling lower under the roof.
      - An ember glow breathes in the load where it's caught, with one small light.
      - `dt screenshot --threats --smoulder --view fire` stages it. `EffectsTests.ACarFireSmouldersThenBurns` pins that the smoke outshines the walls and that the embers are short of flames.
    - **The playthrough's own camera** mirrors the app's scene state each frame (the Stoker's perch timing, the firebox door, the wreck). It aims where the scene draws a thing (the Fire Flies on the nearer lantern, the Stoker on the stack's rim) and keeps the fire up, since there's only the driver, so a night isn't lost to the boiler on the first grade.
    - **A solo night stops at the first facility**, and the director's night only reaches its later roster further on: crew-of-4 solo nights met five kinds and stood still from 7 km.
      - `--bots n` has the harness's bot crew work the night instead (clean loopback; stops and all). The host's world is photographed, with each crewmate drawn doing what they're doing (`CrewActs`).
      - `--insist kind,kind` sends those kinds, as the combination audit does (note 186).
      - Beside the line, the camera stands on the ground. In a cutting, 7 m off the line had put it inside the bank: a blank grey wall, with the slope's rocks and bushes floating.
    - **What the bot nights found: the lineside dead trees read as cages.**
      - `dead_tree_card`'s limbs ran off the card's edges, cut square there, and curled back over the crown, each a constant-width stroke.
      - They now taper a segment at a time, keep reaching up and out, end in twigs inside the card, and the trunk tapers smoothly (tools/art/texgen/mat_foliage.py).
201. **Hazards, the rest: wind on a roof's footing, the cold step on the HUD, mending a broken radio (GDD §22, §23; WP14, note 183's "not yet").**
    - **Wind on footing (§22; spec B.2 "roof run: wind and balance penalty"):** up on a roof, `PlayerMotor.WindPush` adds a sideways push across the car to the motor's velocity: `ITrackConditions.Wind` where you are (the night's, ×1.5 on exposed track) × `player.json wind.drift` (0.3 m/s), from `wind.still` (0.4) of that with the train stopped up to all of it at `train.json maxSpeed`, in gusts. ×`wind.walking` (0.5) walking or standing; a roof run takes it all. The roof handrails take it to `composition.rails.wind` (0.4) of that; a gun's seat, a grabbed player and anyone off a roof get none. At the worst (exposed, flat out, running) about 0.45 m/s: walking against it holds you, but stood at the edge in a gust it walks you off, and the fall is the landing rules' (never a kill of its own).
    - **Reading: the gusts.** The weather has a wind but no direction, so the push comes in gusts from either side: a hash of each `wind.gustMetres` (120 m) of line where you are (`PlayerMotor.Gust`, −1 to +1, eased from one to the next). No trig and no dice, and the conditions are built from the night's seed on every machine, so a predicting client pushes exactly as the host does.
    - **Reading: no crouch, no camera roll.** The sim has no crouch to resist it with (a headset player crouches for real, unseen), so walking rather than running is the balance, and the rails are the upgrade. The drift is what's seen; a camera roll would be felt in a headset's stomach.
    - **The cold step on the HUD (§22):** under the radio line, top right, while the line where you are has a cold step: "DEEP COLD" (1), "BITTER COLD" (2), "KILLING COLD" (3+), then what it does ("OUTSIDE, IT COMES ON 1.5X FASTER", from `cold.perColdStep`). Amber out in it, dim in the warm. A client knows it as the host does (`PlayerMotor.ColdStep`, the route's conditions built from the seed). `dt screenshot --hud --hazards cold` stages a hazard set over the line.
    - **Mending a broken radio (§23 "radio breaks"; §12 "the engineer is whoever has the kit"):** the repair kit in hand, at nothing Use works, and a broken radio on your own belt or lying in reach (a crewmate takes theirs off with [RMB] to hand it over): Use held, standing, for `train.json kit.radioMendSeconds` (8 s, a third of the boiler's 25) mends it. Moving or letting go starts it over. Like a locker's tap and hold, the hands wait for the release, so a tap still puts the kit down. Host-decided in `Bodies.Handle` from intent, and mirrored on the body record as the broken flag is. The HUD prompts "[E] HOLD: MEND YOUR RADIO WITH THE KIT (8S)", then "MENDING ... (n%)" with its bar, and "RADIO BROKEN" now adds ": THE REPAIR KIT MENDS IT".
    - **Reading:** the kit, not the lockers. §12 has the kit mend "a ruptured boiler, and nothing else can"; that says nothing else mends a boiler, not that the kit mends nothing else. A spare radio from the stores is still the quicker fix.
    - `kit.radioBreakPerDamage` and `radioBreakOnGrab` are written into train.json now (they were code defaults).
    - Protocol 23: a broken radio's body record carries how far the mending's got (0 whole, 1 + ticks broken). Tests: `HazardTests` (the push and its sum over a second, a run, the rails and the gun seat, the gusts both ways and smooth, a cold-and-windy harness night predicted exactly, the cold step, mending held, a tap putting the kit down, walking not mending, a loose radio mended), `HudTests` (the cold line, the mend prompt and its progress).
202. **The dead's vote as a screen, from a headset, and the bots' votes (GDD v1.4 App. D.11, D.12; WP11, note 180's "not yet").**
    - **The ballot's screen (D.11):** dead with a ballot (`ClientSession.Ballot`), a plate of its own high on the right, clear of the dead card and the yard's lobby list: "THE DEAD'S VOTE: ONCE A RUN", each creature with its key and, at the right, the want it serves (the vote only moves weight inside a want), then what to do next. The trim's lit while there's a vote to cast. `Hud.BallotRows` says it; `BallotPlate` draws it.
      - **Pick, then cast:** a number key picks its creature (lit on a bar), and the same key again or Enter casts it: "[2] AGAIN OR [ENTER] CAST IT / IT'S LOCKED ONCE CAST". Then "CASTING ..." until the host's ballot comes back cast, then "CAST, AND LOCKED" with the choice in green. The dead card keeps "YOU CALLED THE FIRE FLIES. THE LIVING WON'T KNOW TILL THE END".
      - **Reading: "locked on submit" wants a submit.** Before, a number key cast at once, and one stray key spent the night's only vote. The pick is this machine's alone (`BallotPicker`); only the cast goes to the host.
      - **Same intent path:** the cast is still the option's number as `PlayerIntent.Select` (the dead carry nothing). `NetPlaySession` sends it every tick until the host's ballot says it's locked, so a lost packet can't lose it. While the ballot's open the number keys are the ballot's, not the hotbar's.
    - **From a headset (T36):** the left stick's up and down step through the ballot (its left and right still change whom you watch), and clicking it casts. The trigger and A already mean something to the dead (next crewmate, Live Mic), and the click means nothing. `VrMenuInput` reads the click as `VrMenuPress.Click`, which the menus ignore; `BallotPicker.Headset` applies a press. With `Hud.Headset` the hints say "[STICK UP/DOWN] PICK ONE", "[CLICK STICK] CAST IT".
      - The run-end screen's commendations work the same way in a headset: the stick picks who and which, and its click gives it. A, which leaves the screen, now also keeps your commendations in your profile, as Enter does (before, only Enter did).
    - **Bots vote (D.11: "dead players", and a bot is a crewmate):** `BotCrew.Vote`, the last step of `BotCrew.Think`. A dead bot (not one waiting to board) casts `enemies.json director.vote.botSeconds` (6 s) after the host first offered its ballot, timed by the host's tick (`ClientSession.BallotOfferedTick`, the newest snapshot's when it came), for option `id mod options`: no dice, and a crew of bots spreads its votes. It sends the same `Select` a player's cast does, until it's locked. botSeconds isn't a GDD number: it's the bots' deliberation, so a harness night with deaths votes. The harness's threat report now counts `votes` by creature.
    - `dt screenshot --hud --spectating --ballot pick|cast [--headset]` stages it: the watcher dead, their second option picked, or cast and locked by the host (`--vote` alone: offered, nothing picked).
    - **Not yet:** a profile screen showing the tally; bots don't commend.
    - No protocol change (the ballot, cue and intent are as they were). Tests: `VoteTests.ADeadBotVotesAWhileAfterItsOfferedAndTheSameEveryTime` (a bot's whole tick over loopback: no vote alive, cast after botSeconds and within a second and a half of it, its id's option, the same both nights, it stops once locked); `HudTests.TheBallotIsPickedThenCastAndOnlyCastOnce` (keys, the stick and its click, nothing taken back once sent) and `TheDeadSeeTheirBallotPickItAndSeeItLocked` (over a real host: the rows, keys and wants, the headset hint, the pick, casting, the host's lock).
203. **Bookmarks, the rest: each player's peak in the film, and the night's stills kept on disk (GDD v1.4 App. D.12, D.13, E.5; WP8, note 176's and 177's "not yet").** E.5: "Each player's peak frame is captured as their auto-bookmark (D.12)"; D.12: "A derailment captures one still per crew member from the cinematic, at the peak of their shot."
    - **The peak still.** The host's derailment bookmarks are as they were (one per crew member, their first-person eye: note 176), so nothing new goes on the wire. What changes is the picture each machine takes.
      - `DerailSequence.PeakOf(film, player)` finds the crew member's own shot in the cut and how far into it the film shows their peak (`WreckFilm.Peaks`). The shot's time eases but only runs forward, so it halves towards the moment `FilmShot.At` reaches the peak, clamped to the stretch the shot covers.
      - `BookmarkStills.Due` hands that out as a `DueStill` with its `FilmPeak`: the shot's camera, and the recorded instant.
      - The app draws it the way the film draws that frame (`DerailSequence.Stage`): the film's cars, the crew as ragdolls and nobody else, O2's cutaway and O12's light rig for that camera, none of the live wreck's dust. It's the frame the player sees in the cut at that moment, without the name card.
    - **Reading: the first person stays as the stand-in.** Each client shoots the film off the frame loop (note 177), and a client that never gets the film (it joined after the derail tick) has nothing to take a peak from.
      - So a derailment's still is still taken `bookmarkSeconds` in from the first-person eye.
      - When the film is in, the peak is due at once and replaces the stand-in (`Keep(..., peak: true)`, `IsPeak`). The film is pre-simulated, so its peak doesn't wait for the cut to reach it.
      - A crew member the film has no shot of keeps the stand-in. The cost is at most one extra offscreen frame per crew member, once a night.
    - **Kept past the run end (D.12, D.13).** `BookmarkAlbum` writes each still on the report as a PNG (320x180, as kept), if this machine has it. They go in the user's app data, beside the crash reports and saves: `DarkTerritory/bookmarks/<yyyy-MM-dd HHmm> <line>/`.
      - Each file is named by its number, its run time, its kind and whom it's of: `01 18m24s grab priya.png`, `02 1h02m05s derailment dave.png`, `03 3m20s bookmark dave.png` (the dead's own, named for whom they followed).
      - `night.txt` beside them has the report's lines, each still's file under the line it belongs to, then the dead's own with who took them.
      - The app saves every frame once the report's in. It writes only what's new or changed, so a derailment's stand-in is rewritten when its peak replaces it.
      - A disk that refuses is said once on the console, and the night goes on.
      - The run-end screen says "n STILLS KEPT IN YOUR BOOKMARKS FOLDER" under ENTER: BACK, and the console prints the folder.
    - **Readings.**
        - *Which stills:* the report's: D.13's capped automatic ones and the dead's own. That's what the night showed. A GRAB cut by the cap was never on screen, so it isn't kept.
        - *"Named by night":* the folder is named for when the night began by the player's clock (the sim's run seconds can't say which night) and for the line. A file's time is the run's, as the report prints it, without colons (Windows won't take them).
        - *Presentation only:* the sim never sees a file. The wall clock is only in the folder's name.
    - **Not done:** D.15 question 5's short clips. It's an open question ("may be worth the tech later"), and clips would mean recording frames, not taking a still. There's no setting to turn the album off.
    - `dt screenshot --film s --stills dir` takes each crewmate's peak still from a hosted night's film as the app does, and keeps them the same way. It prints each one's moment against `WreckFilm.Peaks`. `dt screenshot --hud --report [derailed] --stills dir` now keeps the staged report's stills the same way (it used to write `bookmark-n.png`).
    - No protocol change. Tests: `BookmarkStillsTests`.
      - Each player's peak: their shot, its moment and its camera.
      - A derailment's still: the stand-in first, then the peak once the film's in, drawn as the film draws it. One the film has no shot of keeps the stand-in.
      - The album: its folder, the file names, `night.txt`, and rewriting a replaced still.
204. **The combination sweep over a grid of routes and crew sizes (note 186's "not yet"; GDD §34, App. B.10; WP17).** `dt balance --pairs|--triples` ran every combination on frontier:7 with four bots and one seed, so an unwinnable verdict was one lost night on one line with one crew.
    - **The grid** (`balance.json combinations`, `CombinationGrid`): `routes` × `crews`, `seeds` nights a (route, crew) *cell*. Each combination is run in every cell (`Combinations.Nights`: combination by combination, then route, crew and seed, a fixed order). The hazard sets are met as before: one each, in turn down the combinations, or all with `--every-hazard`. Each route has its own staging (`Combinations.Stage` per route and combination).
      - The default grid is the old one (frontier:7, crew 4, one seed), so a by-hand run still takes minutes. `wide` is the nightly's: frontier:7 and deadLines:2, crews 2 and 8 (B.10's smallest and largest), two seeds. That's 8 nights a combination: `--pairs --wide` is 1,088 nights, about 13 CPU-seconds each here.
      - `--wide` picks that grid; `--routes a,b`, `--crews 2,4,8` and `--seeds n` override either one (`--route` and `--crew` still name a single one).
    - **Verdicts, aggregated** (`Combinations.Judge`, `Combinations.Cells`): a row is still one combination under one hazard set, now over every cell.
      - *Unwinnable* means it lost every night in some cell. The detail names the cells and how many of the others it won a night in. A combination lost once in every cell but never every night in one cell isn't unwinnable.
      - *Trivial* means everything came on and nothing landed in any night in any cell.
      - *Unplaced* and *dormant* look at every night of every cell, as they did at every seed.
      - **Reading:** a cell is the unit because B.10 asks for fairness "at every crew size" and a line is what the crew meets. A combination the bots can beat at eight but never at two is the finding, and averaging over the cells would hide it.
    - **Reported per cell:** each row has `cells` (route, crew, nights, lost, unwinnable, grabs, punishes, deaths, the kinds that came on), and each run says its `route` and `crew`. The report's `byCell` gives each cell over the whole sweep: its nights, its losses, and the combinations unwinnable there. The JSON's header says `routes`, `crews` and `seeds` where it said `route` and `crew`.
    - **First run** (Dragger, Climber, Cinder Hounds, Stoker; frontier:7 and deadLines:2 × crews 2, 8; one seed; 24 nights, 80 s on 4 cores): three trivial, two dormant (the Draggers, as note 186 says), and Dragger+Climber unwinnable in one cell, deadLines:2 with a crew of two, where the one death was half the crew. It won a night in the other three cells.
    - **Nightly:** `soak.yml` adds `balance --pairs --wide` beside the default grid's `--every-hazard` run, and prints `byCell`. The job's timeout is 240 min (note 186's 180 min no longer leaves room).
    - **Not done here:** bringing the dormant kinds on (the Gaunt, the Draggers, Ribbits). That's bot behaviour, not the sweep's: done in note 212 (the look-out's errand).
    - Harness-side only; protocol unchanged. Tests: `AuditTests.TheGridRunsEveryCombinationOnEveryRouteWithEveryCrew` (the nights' count and order, the hazards in turn or all of them, the tuning's two grids) and `UnwinnableIsLostEveryNightInSomeCell` (a cell lost every night is unwinnable and named; a loss in every cell that never takes a whole cell isn't; trivial is nothing landed anywhere; `byCell`'s nights, losses and unwinnable list).
205. **The Climber in play: hands for steel, skin that isn't plastic (the 5 October audit, note 200; note 136's model).** In the playthroughs the Climber at a coupling gap read as a pale curved pipe. Up close, in the staged gap (`dt screenshot --threats --climber scrabble --view gapside`), it read as a smooth blue-grey toy with forks for hands.
    - **The hands** were a narrow palm and three thin straight fingers (11 mm), a fork against the sky. Now (tools/blender/climber.py):
      - a broad flat palm;
      - three heavy fingers, each knuckled (a joint bump and a bend) and ending in a black hook;
      - a short thumb-hook off the inner side.
    - **The skin** was one black at a roughness of 0.25 everywhere, so it mirrored the pale sky and read blue. Now (tools/models/recipes/climber.py):
      - roughness 0.45, wet only in blotches;
      - deeper crossing wrinkles and shallow puckered pits in the bake;
      - a dull bruise-brown mottling under the black, so the light breaks on it.
    - It's 4,768 triangles (was 3,652), well inside its budget (`dt art check`). The rig, the clips and the sim are unchanged.
    - **Looked at:** gapside scrabble, before and after; the crouch in the aisle; and the walk on the roof.
206. **A car fire's flames aren't a row of cards (the 5 October audit, note 200).** Burning, the fire read as one flat sheet of flame with a straight bright edge along its foot.
    - **The cause, in two parts:**
      - `fx_flame`'s tongues rose off a ruled line, faded in over only the bottom 7% of the frame;
      - `Effects.CarFire` stood every tongue's foot on the floor, in two straight ranks.
    - Side by side, the feet joined into one edge.
    - **Now:**
      - each tongue's root rises over a ragged, noise-broken band (tools/art/texgen/mat_fx.py);
      - the tongues' feet are where the load burns: some at the floor, some up on the crates and in the gaps between, in and out from the face.
    - `--view fire --threats`: before and after. The furnace and the guns use the same flipbook. Their roots only soften.
207. **The effects' last "not yet"s: the furnace flare, brass dust, a fire you can see spreading (the checklist's demo VFX rows).**
    - **The flare when coal goes on** (§31 "furnace flare"; the boiler's `SinceShovel`, already sent to clients):
      - for `Effects.FlareSeconds` after a shovelful, the bed roars up (every tongue, taller);
      - a gout of flame licks out of the hole and a shower of sparks is thrown into the cab;
      - its light jumps.
      - `GreyboxScene.SinceShovel`, set by the app and the playthrough. `dt screenshot --view fireman --flare 0.2`.
    - **Brass dust over a brass field** (§30):
      - `Effects.Air.Brass`: fine gold motes, hanging and turning slowly, each glinting now and then;
      - wherever the eye is within 35 m of a plan's brass field (`WorldArt.NearBrass`), over the biome's own air.
      - `dt screenshot --route deadlines:4 --at 5100 --view trackside`.
    - **A car fire visibly on its way to the next car** (App. A.5 "grows, jumps couplings"):
      - the sim's blaze (`Extra2`, already sent to clients) over enemies.json carFire.spreadSeconds is the spread;
      - `Effects.CarFire` sends flames creeping along the aisle floor toward both ends of the car as it builds, and rolls the smoke a little further.
      - `dt screenshot --threats --spread 0.9 --view fire` (`Staging.Spread`).
    - **Verified:** `EffectsTests.AShovelfulFlaresTheFireThenItSettles`, `OverABrassFieldTheAirCarriesItsDust` and `AFireAboutToJumpTheCouplingCreepsTowardTheCarsEnds`, and each looked at.
208. **The killed go over and crumble (T121's hit confirm; the checklist's Switchman "shot or clubbed", Soot Children "killed").** A kill took a creature out of the sim at once (Gone, then removed that tick), and its hit lasts a second on the wire, so a killed creature blinked out behind its hit flash.
    - **`GreyboxScene.Deaths`, presentation only:**
      - The scene remembers what it drew last frame.
      - When a killed hit arrives for one of those, it draws it for `Effects.DeathSeconds` (2 s) longer: rolled over onto its side about its own length, away from the blow (`Fallen`: eased in, pushed along it), held at the end of its hit clip or, with none, still (`CreatureArt.Enemy(..., dying)`).
      - From halfway it crumbles (`Effects.Crumble`: ash and soot billowing up, a few embers going out), sinking into the ground or floor, and is gone.
    - **Scope:**
      - Every creature that stands on something.
      - What floats, swarms, burns or is the train's own keeps its own end: the Choir, the Fire Flies, a car fire, the Stoker in its box, the Car Hugger, the Sleepers, the Drift.
      - The first cut tipped them about the blow's axis, which stood a low crawler upright on its tail. They roll about their own length instead (a biped falls sideways, a crawler goes over).
    - **Staging and tests:**
      - `dt screenshot --threats --killed kind:s` stages one, `GreyboxScene.Killed`.
      - `CreatureArtTests.AKilledCreatureGoesOverThenIsGone` (drawn going over and crumbling, not after) and `EffectsTests.AKilledCreatureCrumblesToAshThenIsGone`.
    - **Not yet:** the scene's memory is per scene, so `dt playthrough`'s fresh scene a shot doesn't carry it.
209. **The Cinder Hounds board the rear car (the checklist's "not yet": a distinct boarding leap, not the lunge).**
    - **Before:** a hound's COMMIT aboard (Sim's `CinderHound.Board`: on the rear car's roof, 2.5 m in from its end) began with the lunge, a leap from roof level. On the roof from nowhere.
    - **Now it has its own `board` clip** (tools/blender/cinder_hound.py, 1.1 s, `CreatureArt.HoundBoardSeconds`). The root starts a car's roof-height below and behind where the sim puts it:
      - a bound off the ballast onto the car's end;
      - the body reared up it, the forelegs hooked over the roof's lip, the hind legs scrabbling at the planks out of step;
      - a heave up and over, landing in the pack fight's crouch, which the crouch-lunge loop takes on from.
    - **Not yet: at night it doesn't read.** Climbing the car's end in shadow, only its ember slashes show. The pack on the ground behind is lost from the chase camera too (the Look Review round of 5 October). Their need is "read at range in the rear lamp, and as a pack": more glow, and a light of their own (launch scope; the hounds aren't in the demo).
    - **Staging:** `dt screenshot --threats --board s --view board`. The `board` view is behind the rear car, a little over its roof; `--board` takes the staged Car Hugger off that end.
    - **Tests:** `CreatureArtTests` budgets now list `board` among the hound's clips.
210. **The body under a headset (T82, roadmap M4 "VR body IK"; note 51's "not yet").** T47 gave the crew a headset player's arms, reaching from shoulders that never moved: a crewmate crouched for a crate stood bolt upright with their arms down through the roof, and turned their head round with their hips nailed in place.
    - **What's replicated.** The head's yaw and pitch already were (the sim's look follows the headset, note 30), and the hands (note 51). Its height wasn't: the tracking space hangs from the eye point, so a real crouch moved nothing in the sim. Now `PlayerIntent.Head` (the headset's height over the feet, `Eyes.Height` plus where the head is in the LOCAL space) rides with the hands, in the spare bits of the "other hand" byte plus an I16 of centimetres, and `PlayerState.Head` goes out on the player record (one more field, zero for a keyboard or a bot, so delta encoding makes it free). `TakeHand` takes it with the hands, held between kneeling (0.5 m) and `hand.overhead`; nothing in the sim acts on it. Remote players' heads are interpolated with their hands. Protocol 24.
      - **Reading: with the hands, not on its own.** Every free bit of the intent's three flag bytes is taken; the hand block's byte had seven. So a headset whose controllers have both dropped out stands its body straight until they're back.
      - The app now copies the other hand into the intent too: `VrLocomotion.Intent` built it (T43), but the app took only the reaching hand from it, so on the real path the host never had two hands for a heavy crate. `HandTests` drove `PlayerIntent.Reach` directly and never saw it.
    - **The spine** (`Game/VrBody.cs`, pure; `vr.json body`, our numbers, none being in the GDD or spec, note 40). A head under standing height (`Eyes.Height`, where the room hangs from) leans the torso over from the hips for the first `leanDrop` (0.25 m), to `leanDegrees` (35°); past that the knees bend, the hips going down by the rest (to `crouchMost`, 0.5 m). The head's yaw is the sim's; the hips keep theirs until the head is `yawDeadzoneDegrees` (35°) off them, then are dragged round to the deadzone's edge. The torso takes `twistShare` (0.6) of the turn between them, a third on each spine bone, and the neck the rest, nodding the head to the headset's pitch (to `nodDegrees`).
      - **Reading: a lower head is a lean first.** The height alone can't tell a bow from a crouch. Most of what a headset player does near the floor starts as a lean (reaching for a lever, a crate's handle), and a deep drop can only be a crouch, so the first quarter metre leans.
    - **The legs.** Each foot is planted in the frame of the car they stand in (the train moving under them moves nothing; a change of frame plants them afresh). A foot steps when the hips have moved its place `stepDistance` (0.2 m) off it or turned `stepTurnDegrees` (30°) from it: one foot at a time, the furthest out first (level, the one that didn't go last), over `stepSeconds` (0.28 s) in an arc `stepHeight` (9 cm) high, landing `stepOvershoot` past its place so creeping hips aren't chased. The crew model (`CreatureArt.HeadsetBody`) is turned to the hips and let down by the crouch, bent up the spine, and its legs reached to the ankles over the feet by the same two-bone IK as the arms (the knee forward of the hips and a little out), each foot turned back to how the clip had it, at its own yaw. The arms then reach from where the shoulders have gone.
      - **Only stood still.** Walking and running stay the clips' (above 0.4 m/s), and so does any act (the levers, the shovel, a hold). A headset crewmate's pace is now measured in their own car's frame, so riding a moving train isn't walking.
      - **Not yet:** hands that hold what they carry; sideways leans (only the height is sent, not where the head is over the feet, which the sim doesn't follow either). (The greybox box figure leaning: done in note 223.)
    - **Verified:**
      - `VrBodyTests` (8): every number is in vr.json; a lower head leans, then crouches, to the most a crouch goes; the hips wait inside the deadzone and follow past it, the short way round the half-turn, and a look round inside it never steps; a 90° turn steps two to four times and leaves both feet turned with the hips; creeping hips plant and step left, right, left, a planted foot never moving, the stepping one lifting to the step's height, and they settle square once stopped; the same head gives bit-identical bodies; the model-space feet and the hips' lag; and on the crew model, a crouch brings the figure down 0.3 m with its soles still on the floor, and a stepping foot is off it.
      - `HandTests.TheRestOfTheCrewSeeAHeadsetsHeadHeight`: over loopback another client sees the head's height, the headset's own prediction keeps it, and it's held at kneeling and gone without a hand. `VrLocomotionTests.TheHeadsHeightGoesWithTheHands`.
      - `dt screenshot --view crew --vr-body` (or `crewside`) stages three headset crewmates across car 2's roof (`Staging.Headsets`): one leant over reaching down ahead, head bowed; one crouched right down, knees bent and both hands by the roof; one whose head has turned 100°, the hips come round 65° and the torso twisted between, a foot halfway through its step (the stride lived up to that moment).
211. **A crewmate's gait is paced over the car they stand on, not the ground (presentation; `SceneArt.Crewmate`).** The gait (idle, walk, run, and the walking forms of carry, shoulder, cradle, lantern and gap) was picked from how far a crewmate's world-space feet moved between draws. A keyboard player or bot stood still on a train at 15 m/s therefore played the run clip.
    - `Crewmate` now carries `Car` (the replicated `PlayerState.Parent`, or `PlayerState.World` on the ground) and `Local` (`PlayerState.Position`, their feet in that car's frame). `CrewActs.Crewmate` fills both from the seated placement it already uses, and `GreyboxScene.AsCrewmate` fills them from the Passenger's `Attached` and `Local`. The pace is measured on `Local` while both samples are on the same car, and on the world-space feet on the ground. The draw where they change frames (stepping car to car, or off onto the ballast) keeps the pace they had, because its two samples are in different frames.
    - Ground players are unchanged. The data was already in the snapshot, so the protocol is unchanged (`Messages.Version` stays as it is). It replaces note 210's headset-only measure: one pace for every crewmate, the headset body still read off it (it stands only on Idle).
    - Test: `CrewActsTests.ACrewmateStoodOnAMovingCarIsIdleNotRunning`. Stood still on car 2 at 15 m/s is idle (it was a run before this change). Walking 1.5 m/s along the moving roof is a walk. On the ground, stood still is idle and 1.5 m/s is a walk.
212. **The look-out: the sweep's dormant kinds brought on (notes 186 and 204's "not yet"; GDD §34, App. A.4, A.6; WP17).** The combination sweep reported the Gaunt, the Draggers and Ribbits dormant in every combination: placed and never come on, so never judged. Bots keep to their posts. Nobody walked over to the Gaunt asleep 25 m out on the ballast, stood within a metre of a roof's lip, or went off alone where a Ribbit pack outnumbers them. On frontier:7 the crew go in at the start for a bag at a crane, so nobody was even up on the roofs.
    - **Chosen over staging** (the task's option b): no start on the line makes a bot stand at a lip or walk off from the others. Staging only picks where the night starts; this is what a curious player does.
    - **The errand** (`Bots/LookErrand.cs`, `RoofWalkerBot.Errand`, `HarnessOptions.Look`, `balance.json combinations.look`). On an insisted night only (`HarnessOptions.Insist` and `Look` both set; `dt balance --pairs|--triples` and `dt harness --insist` set them), the crew's last walker is the look-out:
      - **On the ballast** (boarding at the start, or down at a stop): it waits up to `waitSeconds` (12) for an insisted Gaunt or Ribbit pack to be sent. Then it walks to within `gauntM` (3) of the Gaunt (it stirs at 9, wakes at 4) or `ribbitM` (5) of the nearest Ribbit, if one's within `seekM` (80). It leaves the others behind, which is what puts it outnumbered. Then it boards as usual.
      - **Up on the roofs** with a Dragger insisted: until it has met one, it stays out there and doesn't go in for a bag (`KeepOut`), so the Dragger is placed under the car it's on. Then it walks along the roofs to that car and stands `edgeM` (0.6) in from the lip on the Dragger's side, beside it along the car (it reaches from `grabRange`, 1.0 m).
      - Each thing that comes on (telegraphs or further, for anyone) is crossed off by id, for good. A Dragger that rearms after is looked at already. Nothing left to look at, and the walker's back to its post.
    - **Fair:** it's only feet and a facing, intent like any player's, worked out from what its own client sees. The enemies' rules aren't touched. What wakes, wakes by its own rule. The bots answer with their normal counters: `Heed.Voice` talks to the Gaunt, a walker steps back from the lip and swings, `Heed.Rescue` hauls a held crewmate free. The errand comes after the repair kit and a car's trouble, and before a stop's part, and not while the walker is getting warm. It's deterministic: the bot's tick, the enemies in id order, a set of ids only asked whether it holds one.
    - **Before / after** (`dt balance --pairs --only Gaunt,Dragger,Ribbit,Climber`, default grid: frontier:7, crew 4, one seed):
      - Before: 6 dormant; never exercised Dragger 3, Ribbit 3, Gaunt 3.
      - After: 3 fair (the three with the Gaunt), 3 trivial (Dragger+Ribbit, Dragger+Climber, Ribbit+Climber); nothing never exercised.
      - `--at-stops`: all three pairs of the three come on (trivial). `--crews 2,8`: they come on at eight; at two there's no walker to send, and they don't.
    - **What it found:** the sweep now judges these kinds, and it fails on them. With one look-out who steps back from the lip and outruns the tongues, a Dragger or a Ribbit pack lands nothing in 90 s. That's the trivial verdict doing its job; it's left for balance, not hidden.
    - **Not yet:** looking more than once at the same thing; the Grumbler's crane out on the line (still unplaced without `--at-stops`). (A look-out in a crew of two: done in note 222, the gunner.)
    - Harness and bots only; protocol and enemy rules unchanged. Test: `AuditTests.TheLookOutGoesAndLooksAtWhatLiesInWait`: a 30 s insisted Gaunt+Dragger night, four bots. Without the look-out, both are placed and neither comes on; with it, both do.
213. **The Whistler carries its victim (the checklist's "not yet": "the victim carried during the run").**
    - **Before:** carrying someone off (App. A.4 GRAB), it played its run, laid flat on the ground. The one it had was drawn where the sim has them, at its middle, upright in `held_carried`, a pose made for the old upright Whistler. So the victim stood inside a flat coil.
    - **Now it has a `carry` clip** (tools/blender/whistler.py, the run's 0.6 s loop):
      - the back half runs flat and snaking (seg_05 takes back the snake's turn, so the front heads straight on);
      - the front half rears two metres in a column, the head curled forward over them, the siphon out over their head;
      - the forelegs wrap round from behind (solved with `rig.reach`), the hooks under their arms at `CARRY_UNDERARM`, 1.42 m.
    - **Sockets `hook_r` and `hook_l`** at the forelegs' tips say where that is. `CreatureArt.Clutches` records them, by the held player's id, for each Whistler drawn this frame.
    - **`GreyboxScene.Hung` draws the victim there,** by the armpits (`CreatureArt.CarriedUnderarm` over their feet), facing the way it runs. The sim still has them at its middle; this is presentation only.
    - **`held_carried` is redone for it:** lifted 0.1 m to the hooks, the shoulders forced up, the hands clawing back at the forelegs, the legs swept back off the ground and kicking.
    - **Put down at the nest,** out of its forelegs, they're drawn on their back (`held_pinned`), not hung in the air.
    - **Facing:** carrying, it faces square off the line, the way its nest is from the gap (Sim `Whistler`), not off the nearest car's middle; a gap is at a car's end, which put it 45 degrees out.
    - **Staging:** `dt screenshot --threats --whistler carry --view carry` (a second into the run, 7 m out, `Staging.CarryOut`). `--whistler nest` now has its catch too.
    - **Tests:** `WhistlerTests.CarryingSomeoneOffItHoldsThemUpUnderTheArmsInFrontOfItsRearedFront`; `CreatureArtTests` budgets list `carry`.
214. **The Choir disperses (the checklist's "not yet": "a disperse when the crew hushes (it just leaves)").**
    - **Before:** driven off (World: its quiet held, enemies.json `choir.disperseQuietSeconds`, or its one taken), the swarm is dismissed the same tick (`Enemy.Dismiss`: BreakOff, then Gone), and every ghost blinked out.
    - **Now the scene sees it go** (`GreyboxScene.Leaving`, presentation only, the scene's own memory like note 208's deaths). A ghost it drew last frame that's gone without being killed is drawn going for `CreatureArt.ChoirLeaveSeconds` (3 s):
      - turned away from the train, in its swoop (tipped mouth first, tendrils streamed back);
      - swept up 14 m and out 10 m from the train, faster and faster (the square of the time);
      - its cold light going out, the cold mist off it gone at 60%.
    - **`DrawEnemy`'s flinch push now applies with or without a tip** (going is all push). Every existing push came with a tip, so nothing else moves.
    - **Staging:** `dt screenshot --threats --dispersing s --view choir` (the staged ghosts driven off s seconds ago).
    - **Tests:** `CreatureArtTests.TheChoirDrivenOffIsSeenGoingUpAndAwayThenIsGone`.
    - **Not yet:** like the deaths, `dt playthrough`'s fresh scene per shot doesn't carry it.
215. **The Track Doll vanishes, and takes a toy and goes (the checklist's "not yet": "vanish with no walk-off (it just stops being drawn); takes a toy and goes").**
    - **Before:** the sim moves it from one tick to the next. Come at in its car, it hops to another; stopped short of on the rail, or appeased with a toy, it leaves the run. It simply stopped being drawn.
    - **Now the scene sees it vanish** (`GreyboxScene.Vanishing`, presentation only, the scene's own memory as in notes 208 and 214). It copies each doll it drew (the sim moves its own). A doll that's gone, or moved car or more than a metre, is drawn where it was for a flicker (0.3 s, on and off at 15 Hz), held stock still in its last pose. It's never seen to walk.
    - **Where it was,** `Effects.Vanish` leaves a puff of pale porcelain dust and a few white chips of glaze, for `Effects.VanishSeconds`.
    - **Appeased,** a toy body gone the same tick goes with it: in the flicker it's clutched under its chin in both hands (its giggle's), drawn as `SceneArt.Toy` draws that toy (`CreatureArt.DollHolding`).
    - **Staging:** `dt screenshot --threats --vanish s[:toy] --view inside` (the haunting doll moved into car 2 over its cargo and gone s seconds ago; s < 0, still there).
    - **Tests:** `CreatureArtTests.TheTrackDollFlickersOutWhereItWasNotWalkingOff`.
216. **The firebox's bed is a heap of coals (the 5 October audit; the checklist's firebox).** Through the open firehole, what the fireman sees all night was a flat bright band along the grate (an emissive box) under a row of flame cards rooted on one line.
    - **Now `Effects.Coals` draws the bed** as 72 lumps across the grate, mounded in the middle and banked at the back, each turned its own way.
      - The dark ones are coal, lit by the fire's own light.
      - The ones in the heart of the bed glow, more of them the hotter the fire, each breathing on its own slow beat. They keep the Stoker's green.
    - **The flames lick up off the heap:** each tongue's root follows the mound and sits in or out over the bed, shallow enough not to be cut by the back wall.
    - **The glows are toned down:** the coals' glow is a soft low blob, not a sheet, and the roaring white heart is smaller, so the coals still show at full heat.
    - **The scene's flat band** (`GreyboxScene`) is drawn only without the art pass's fire, or with the door shut.
    - **Tests:** `EffectsTests.TheBedIsAHeapOfCoalsNotABand`. Looked at: `dt screenshot --firedoor --view firebox` (at `--coal` 1, the default and 9), and `--view fireman --flare 0.15`.
217. **The spruce near the line is modelled, and the pines fill out (the 5 October audit's "pines from above": narrow stacked columns).**
    - **What the chase camera found:**
      - Along a Maritime-planned forest (PlanArt), every spruce was `NovaKit.Conifer`'s three crossed narrow cards; from above they read as a column of separate dark clumps with a stick on top.
      - The modelled pine (`WorldKit.Pine`) showed sky between its 14 whorls, and its trunk poked out above the leader.
    - **The fix:**
      - `WorldKit.Pine` and the new `WorldKit.Spruce` share one bough builder. The pine now has 18 whorls, and both have a slim core of crossed foliage cards inside the boughs, so the gaps between whorls are foliage. The trunk stops in the leader.
      - The spruce has short boughs in 24 whorls, a narrow spire as black spruce is, a club of dense short boughs at the top, and is tinted bluer like the far cards.
      - PlanArt uses it within `NearSpruce` (40 m, as WorldArt's `NearTrees`) of the line; out in the fog, the cards stay.
    - **Cost:** near-line forest frames on frontier:7 at km 15.9 go from about 260k to 410k triangles (chase and trackside), inside `perf.json`'s 1.5M.
    - **Tests:** `LinesideArtTests.TheNearSpruceIsModelledNarrowAndMassed`. Looked at: `--route frontier:7 --at 15930 --view chase` and `--view trackside`, and `--at 4000 --view chase`.
