    - **While they're gone, the crewmate goes limp.** It's the inert body spec E and GDD v1.4 App. D.2 name in as many words. No bot takes over, because a bot would play someone's character for them, and nothing on the train stands frozen for the enemies.
      - The host holds the place: the id, the name, the token, the state at the drop and the last input applied. It holds it for `rejoin.reserveSeconds` (180), counted in host ticks.
    - **Coming back** (`HostSession.Return`). The token of a held place gets that id back: a fresh Welcome, then what the player missed (names, looks, commendations, the film and the report if they've gone out, as E.8 asks, and their ballot offered again).
      - **Alive at the drop:** they stand up where their body lies now (`PlayerMotor.StandUp`). That's the highest walkable surface under it within a step (a floor, a roof, a coupler plate), or the ground if it's off the train or between cars.
        - They keep the health, the cold and the facing they had. Their kit is whatever is still on the body: a crewmate who lifted it took its tools.
        - The body leaves the world. `Placed` is bumped, so their client adopts the new state as a placement, not a correction.
      - **Dead at the drop:** still dead, of the same cause, on the dead channel with their ballot. The Holdout queue takes them back at the back, as D.6's Disconnect row says, though as a dead entry and not a lobbied one.
      - **Still live on another connection** (the client gave up on its link before the host saw it go): the new link takes over the crewmate where it stands. The old link is closed, the delta baselines reset, and no body is left.
    - **The ambiguity, and its flag.** D.2 says "a player who rejoins enters the queue as a lobbied player". Taken literally, a five-second wifi blip would cost a living player their night.
      - The reading here: inside the reserve, a dropped link that comes back is the same player getting up. D.2's letter still holds past the reserve, where a returning player is a new joiner and is lobbied at once under Holdouts.
      - `rejoin.reclaimBody` (true) is the switch. With it off, the body stays and the player comes back in their slot as a joiner: lobbied with Holdouts, otherwise a drop-in at `BoardAt`.
      - A body that's gone (the Gaunt walking off with it, `TakenBy`) always goes by D.2's letter.
    - **Past the reserve** the slot frees: its token is no good any more and the body stays (D.2). `HostSession.Reserved`, `IsReserved`, `Rejoins` and `ReservesExpired` report it, and `HostSession.Drop(id)` cuts a player's link from the host's end (tests, the screenshot).
    - **On the client.**
      - `ClientSession.Reconnect(transport)` keeps the world and the input sequence (the host carries on from the last input it applied), and drops the snapshot baselines. It also keeps `Token` and counts `Reconnects`.
      - `NetPlaySession`, when it's lost, dials the host again the way it first came (the address, or the lobby's owner). It tries `rejoin.retries` (5) times, `rejoin.retrySeconds` (3) apart. Each try lasts until the player is back aboard or the link fails (the transport's connect timeout). After that, F5 (`Reconnect`) starts another round.
      - Not for a host's own player, and not once the host has left its lobby: spec E's host disconnect ends the session.
    - **The HUD** (`dt screenshot --hud --lost`):
      - "RECONNECTING: TRY 1 OF 5" in amber under the red NO LINK.
      - In the middle of the screen, "RECONNECTING / TRY 1 OF 5: YOUR BODY LIES WHERE YOU STOOD TILL YOU'RE BACK".
      - Out of tries, "CONNECTION LOST: [F5] RECONNECT".
      - The yard's lobby panel is hidden while lost, because who's aboard is stale.
    - **Determinism.** The return happens on the host at the start of a tick, through its receive. The player's own state arrives as a placement. The other clients only ever see the id the host has.
      - `NetcodeTests.OnAPerfectLinkPredictionMatchesTheHostExactly` stays green, and on a perfect link a rejoined bot predicts exactly afterwards.
    - **`dt harness --drop-rejoin bot:at:seconds`.** `bot` is a crew index or a bot's name (`roof-walker` means the first one). Its link is cut `at` seconds in, it redials `seconds` later, and the report gains a `rejoin` block: which id it was and is, how long the redial took, what it came back as, the host's counts, whether every other client sees the host's crew exactly (the bot once), and its corrections from then on.
      - `dt harness --bots 8 --seconds 300 --route frontier:7 --enemies --drop-rejoin roof-walker:120:15` (90 ms ±20, 3% loss): bot 3 dropped at 120 s and redialled at 135 s. It was back as player 3 0.27 s later, on its feet in car 6, and every other client saw it exactly once. Its worst correction afterwards was 3.2 mm (16 small ones). For the night: 47.3 kbit/s down and 24.3 up a client, 214 B snapshots, the worst correction anyone made 0.18 m, the fewest snapshots any client got 8188 of 9000 (the rejoiner, 15 s away), and no deaths.
    - **Tests:**
      - `RejoinTests`: the wire, back to its own crewmate (id, health, kit, beside the body, the body gone from every world, seen once, no corrections after), the dead come back dead, the slot frees past the reserve, a takeover before the host saw the drop, reclaimBody off, and harness nights over the rough and the perfect link.
      - `NetPlayTests.ADroppedJoinerReconnectsToItsOwnCrewmate` (real UDP, the automatic retry, a new socket) and `ABotRejoinsThroughTheLobby` (the fake Steam: a new account, the same slot).
    - **Not yet:**
      - Bookmarks made while a player was away aren't re-sent.
      - ~~A held place doesn't count toward a full lobby, because there's no crew cap yet.~~ It does now: the crew cap, note 254.
      - The token goes in the clear over UDP: enough for co-op, not a defence against someone on the path.
254. **The crew cap, and a full lobby (notes 24 and 253's "not yet"; GDD §1 "Players: 2–8", §15).** Nothing capped the crew: the lobby took 12 Steam members, the LAN list said n/12, and a full game left a joiner waiting.
    - **The cap is `player.json crew.cap` (8).** GDD §1 says 2–8 and §15's largest crew is 8; ARCHITECTURE §8 question 4's "8+" is the net budget's headroom (to 12), not the design.
      - **The reading of "8+": mods.** A mod raises it with a patch (`{"$patch": true, "crew": {"cap": 12}}`, note 53). The content hash means everyone in a game runs the same cap. `CrewCapTests.AModsHigherCapTakesALargerCrew` takes ten. Past 12 is untested.
    - **What counts (`HostSession.Occupied`).** Every place that's someone's:
      - the crew in the world, living and dead (the dead are still crew: the dead channel, the vote, the Holdout queue);
      - those welcomed and waiting to board at a stop;
      - the places held for the dropped (note 253).
    - **Bots count.** A bot holds a crewmate: an id, a body, a part at the stops. So the menu's bots and `--bots` are clamped to cap − 1 when hosting, and the host's own player always has a place. The codebase has no bots giving way to a joining human (T110 only made "no bots" the menu's default), and none is added here: a bot leaving mid-night would take its part (the driver, the gunner) with it. To make room for a friend, host with fewer bots.
    - **Turned away (protocol 27).** Greeting a joiner (note 253's Hello line), the host checks the token first: a held place's token always gets back in, full or not, because its place is already counted. Otherwise, at the cap, the host sends `Refused` (reliable: `RefusalReason.CrewFull`, the crew, the cap) instead of a Welcome. It keeps the link open for `crew.refuseLingerSeconds` (3) so the refusal arrives, then hangs up (`HostSession.Refusals`, `TurnedAway`).
      - The joiner's game (`NetPlaySession.Connect`) stops waiting and the join screen says **CREW FULL (8/8)** (`Refusal.ToString()`), as it says other refusals.
      - A joiner back too late (its place ran out and was taken) is refused the same way. It stops retrying, and its HUD says **CREW FULL (8/8)** in red mid-screen ("YOUR PLACE WAS HELD TILL IT RAN OUT, AND THE CREW'S FULL: [F5] TRY AGAIN") and top right (`LinkInfo.Refused`). F5 tries again.
    - **A slot frees** when a held place runs out (note 253's reserve), or when a player **leaves on purpose**. A joiner's game says `Leave` (protocol 27) as it's closed (`ClientSession.Leave`, from `NetPlaySession.Dispose`). The host then lets the place go at once instead of holding it 180 s. The body stays (spec E), the token is no good, and `HostSession.Leaves` counts it.
      - If the word is lost, the place is held as for any drop.
      - The host takes it either way round: if the hang-up is heard first (the loopback hears it at once), the place already held is let go.
    - **The listings.**
      - The LAN beacon's aboard is now `Occupied`, and its max is the cap. The browser's row shows `n/8`, and at the cap it's greyed `FULL` (`ListedGame.Full`, unchanged).
      - Before, aboard was the HUD's head count less the bots, which counted a Passenger wearing a crewmate's face (App. A.7). That would have given it away to anyone looking at the join list.
      - **The Steam lobby** (`NetPlaySession.Advertise`): `aboard` and the new `max` and `full` values. The member limit is the cap (`IOnlineBackend.SetMemberLimit`, new: Steam's `SetLobbyMemberLimit`). At the cap it's `SetJoinable(false)`, so Steam's search drops it, and an invite or "Join Game" can't get in. A joiner turned away there is told CREW FULL (n/cap) from the lobby's values where the platform shows them, else "the lobby is closed". When a place frees, it opens again.
      - `Lobby.SetJoinable` and `SetMemberLimit` only call the platform on a change. `FakeOnline` keeps the limit and `Doors(id)` shows it to a test.
      - The browser takes the cap from `max` before the member limit.
      - The host's status line says `crew n/8` (FULL at the cap), and the yard's lobby panel says "THE LOBBY: n ABOARD OF 8" and, at the cap, "CREW FULL (8/8): NOBODY ELSE CAN JOIN".
    - **Silent connections take no place.** The greeting line (note 253: connected, not yet said Hello) isn't counted, and since f436200 a quiet greeter is never welcomed until it speaks. So a silent or abandoned connection can't fill the crew or shut the lobby; the link's timeout takes it.
      - The same phantom from the client's side: a UDP client whose host hung up redialled on its own while under `ConnectSeconds` old (`NetPlayTests.ADroppedJoinerReconnectsToItsOwnCrewmate` counted 3 players for 2 at one run here). A client's link to its host, once gone, now stays gone (`DatagramTransport.Drop`), and a late Accept is ignored. Coming back is a new transport, as note 253's redial already makes.
    - **The harness.** With more bots than the cap, the crew is a crew of the cap (its parts, fireman and all). The rest are spare hands with no stop part, turned away at the door. The report gains `crew` (cap, places taken, refusals, and each refused bot with what it was told), and the refused are out of the crew's figures.
      - `dt harness --bots 10 --seconds 120 --route frontier:7 --enemies` (the rough link): `crew` reads cap 8, 8 places taken, 2 refusals, "bot 9 (roof-walker): CREW FULL (8/8)" and bot 10 the same. The eight played on as before: 2.3 km, 45 kbit/s down a client, the worst correction 0.08 m, no deaths.
    - **Determinism.** The refusal happens on the host before anyone's welcomed, and the refused never enter the crew, so `NetcodeTests` is untouched.
    - `dt screenshot --hud --lost --refused` (back too late at a cap of two) and `--hud --crew-full` (the host's lobby panel at a cap of two). `--menu join` now has a full LAN game greyed FULL, and a full Steam lobby that the search doesn't find.
    - **Tests:**
      - `CrewCapTests`: the wire, the ninth joiner refused with the reason, a held place counting and its token still getting in at the cap, the place opening when the reserve runs out (and the one back too late refused), a Leave freeing the place at once, the refused hung up on after the linger, a mod's cap of ten, and the harness turning the ninth bot away.
      - `LobbyBrowserTests.AFullCrewOnTheNetworkIsListedFullAndOpensWhenItsHeldPlaceRunsOut` and `AFullCrewsPlatformLobbyIsShutAndOpensWhenAPlayerLeaves` (the fake Steam: values, member limit, not joinable, not in the search, CREW FULL on joining, and open again once a bot quits).
      - `NetPlayTests.BackTooLateToAFullCrewTheJoinerIsToldCrewFull`.
255. **The crew's clips, from the Look Review's notes (the notes boxes under each clip; twelve on the crew's).** Each fixed in `tools/blender/crew_clips.py` and looked at in `dt art reel`:
    - **The step** (carry_walk, lantern_walk "stiff in the legs"): every walking clip's gait has a soft front knee at the heel strike that takes the weight down, the back foot rolled up onto its toe, the swinging knee lifted well through the pass, and the hips dropping and rising. carry_walk's stride is shortened less (0.85). lantern_walk's free arm swings against the legs with the elbow soft.
    - **Up a ladder** (climb: "follows ladder pacing"; climb_carry: the hand went up through the head): both are new here, one 40-frame cycle per two rungs (`TrainKit.RungPitch`). A hand or foot on a rung sinks down the body as the body rises past it, then goes up for the next rung but one: right hand with left foot, then left with right. `SceneArt` plays them by how far up the ladder the climber is (`ClimbCycleRise`), not by the clock, so the hands stay on the rungs at the sim's 1.6 m/s, the solo remainer's 0.4 m/s or standing still. climb_carry's one hand goes up out at the side, clear of the head and the load.
    - **The shovel** ("looks a bit off"): the fireman's swing, new here. Knees bent, the blade flat into the coal ahead and right with the back hand low, lifted on the legs, then turned and thrown forward through the firehole.
    - **crew_clips wins over the mesh's own clips** (`ModelLoader.WithClips(replace: true)`), so climb and shovel are reworked without re-baking crew.glb. The survivors take the crew's clips the same way.
    - **On the back** (held_dragged "face clipping floor"): its pose and held_pinned's lay face down with the legs through the floor. It's now on the back with the chin up off the boards; dragged, the hands claw at the boards back over the head.
    - **fall** ("not as flaily panic"): the elbows bent, the hands grabbing at the air round the head with the fingers half-closed, the legs running at nothing, the back arched. **firedoor** ("what's the other hand doing?"): braced on the left knee, not up at the face. **hurry** ("what's with the arms?"): the elbows near square and driven back past the hips, the fists close in. **pick** ("head isn't looking down"): the head and neck bowed onto the hands. **reload** ("props?"): they were always drawn in the game (`CreatureArt.ReloadKit`) but not in the reel, which now draws the crew's acts through `CreatureArt.Crewmate` with what's in their hands (the reload's kit, the hand lamp, the bar, the wrench). The ram is now at the chest, and the rammer's butt stops just past the back fist (it ran back through the gunner's head).
    - **drag_fwd** (drag: "what about forwards"): a new clip, the body hauled along behind while walking forwards. Neither drag plays in the game yet: the sim carries bodies over the shoulder (App. C.4), so the drags are for when it lets a body be dragged.
    - `dt art reel --clips a,b` renders only those clips. The `rig.reach` arm and leg solver takes a `pole` (an elbow or knee drawn toward a point), which `arm_to`/`leg_to` pass as `elbow`/`knee`.
256. **Limbs through the body: a clearance check, and the second round of clip notes (Look Review; note 255's clips).** The second round of notes was mostly "arm through body", "arm through knee", "arm through head" and "legs through coat", which a look at a small reel misses.
    - **`Art.Clearance`** samples every clip of a figure at 30 fps. It treats each limb as a capsule round its bone and measures it against the trunk, the coat's skirt, the head and the other limbs. The capsules are sized to the crew's coat as measured off crew.glb: about 0.2 m round the chest and the hips, and a 0.22 m skirt to the knee.
      - `dt art clearance [--only crew] [--clips ..] [--limit m] [--allow clip:pair]` lists the worst overlaps.
      - `CreatureArtTests.TheCrewsLimbsStayOutOfTheirBodies` fails any crew clip deeper than 0.1 m. Up to `Clearance.Touching` (6 cm) reads as touching; a forearm on a knee, and the swing's two hands on one haft, go a little past that.
      - Before this round the deepest overlaps were 0.19-0.23 m: spray's arm through the chest, shovel's through the hips, uncouple's through the knee, whistle's through the head. Now nothing goes deeper than 0.1 m.
    - **The arms keep out of the body as they're placed.** `crew_clips.arm_to` steers the elbow and the forearm off the posed trunk, skirt, head and thighs (`clear_of_body`), so every clip's hands get there round the body rather than through it.
    - **The gaze is solved, not guessed.** On this rig a positive neck or head pitch lifts the face (`look_at`'s probe), so the clips that meant "head down at it" had it looking up (the pick note). `look_at(pose, point)` turns the head and neck onto a point; pick, mend, uncouple, gap, gap_step, the shovel and the crouch use it.
    - **Tools are aimed.** `aim_tool` turns the right wrist so what's in the fist points at the work: smash's bar back over the shoulder and down onto the hasp, pry's bar into the board, the swing through its arc, the wrench's head on the nut. Before, they pointed at the sky or back through the head.
    - **Reworked, per note:**
      - The shovel is held as a fireman holds it: the D-grip by the left hip, the shaft across the body, the lower hand where the arm reaches. Its shaft ran straight ahead, which put the D-grip behind the right hip, and the scoop's hand target was out of reach.
      - uncouple kneels on the left knee with the left hand on the coupler. mend kneels on the right knee, so the wrench arm is clear.
      - whistle's cord is out to the left of the head.
      - hurry has a smaller stride and the fists swing clear of the coat. fall is calmer (a second's drop, the arms up and reaching).
      - crouch_idle is reworked here (with replace, note 255): forearms on the knees.
      - The climbs put the feet and hands on the same rungs, 1.5 m apart, and climb_carry's free hand is in front of the shoulder.
      - The extinguisher's handle and the shoulder carry's hug are off the body's middle.
    - **The reel shows what's in play:** the extinguisher in the extinguisher clips, the crate in the carries, and a ladder whose rungs run down past the climbs at their pace.
    - **Not yet:** the carried body (shoulder, climb_carry) and the child (cradle) aren't drawn in the reel; the game draws them from the sim's ragdolls.
257. **The derailment film, bigger and closer: cars hit bodies, the gunner thrown from the seat, landings thud (the director, 5 Oct 2026; GDD v1.4 App. E.3, E.4, E.10; note 251's "not epic enough").** Note 251's video showed small, upright bodies lying in cut-away cars. The director's calls, all in `tuning/wreck.json` "film" and `Train/WreckFilm.cs`:
    - **A bigger fling** (inside E.10's ranges): ×1.6 (was 1.3), a kick of 3–5 m/s (2–4) and 3.5–7 rad/s of spin (up to 4; E.10's top is 8; `spinMin`, so a body in the air goes over rather than drawing next to no spin). E.3's text and E.10's defaults say so.
    - **The cars hit the bodies.** The ragdolls stepped against the cars where the wreck had them at the end of each 30 Hz frame, so a car jumped up to 0.7 m through a body between steps and the hit was a push-out. Now each ragdoll substep sees the cars between where they were a frame ago and now (`Stage.At`), and every contact carries its surface's own motion (`Ballast.Physics.Contact.Motion`, new, zero for the still world): `PbdBody` takes friction and bounce relative to it, and a moving surface bounces what it meets at the speed they closed at. A car ploughing into a body throws it ahead; one coming down on it presses it into the ground (crushed, note 258). Nothing pushes a body to somewhere safe: it can be pinned or run under. Bodies piling up (Pile) and the rest are as they were. No limb separates (E.1): the joints are D.9's bones.
    - **Doorways are gaps (E.3, note 177's "not yet").** `FilmCar.Doorways` (the car's `DoorList`, end doors and a cargo car's side doors): a body inside is held by the walls except in a doorway, floor to lintel; out through one (its pelvis out of the box), it's outside and the car is a solid to it.
    - **The gunner is thrown out of the seat (T112).** `FilmCar` carries its mounted gun (pivot and laid yaw) and the film makes it solid: the barrel and breech (`GunBarrel`, the cannon model's 0.44 m behind the pivot to 1.5 m ahead) on its pedestal (`GunPedestal`). `FilmPlayer.Seated`: the gunner's ragdoll starts `seatClear` (0.25 m) further back from the gun than the seat, with the seat's own velocity (the car's, not flung on into the breech) and `seatKick` (6 m/s, E.10's top kick) up, head over heels at the full spin about the seat's axis; the car slows under them in the air, so they go up and over it. `dt film --gunner` sits the last of the crew in the first gun's seat as it comes off.
    - **Landings are recorded and heard.** Each frame carries `FilmLanding`s (which body, where, how hard, off a car or the ground): a hit of `thudSpeed` (2 m/s, of velocity taken off the head, chest or pelvis inside a frame by the ground or a car) or more, `thudGap` (0.25 s) apart (a hit at the killing speed always thuds). `GameAudio.Film` plays `crew-carry.body-land.ground` or `.wood` (the checklist's thrown-body landing, already installed) at each, the hardest three in a stretch, its volume by the speed. No new sound, so `dt audio render` is unchanged.
    - **Each shot is the throw into their death.** E.5's peak was the highest apex, longest airtime or hardest landing, and the scorer's was mostly the top of the hop off a car's floor 0.3 s in: the shot ended before anything hit anyone, and they were drawn stood upright. A player's shot is now built round their death (note 258): its stretch of recording is 70% before the hit and 30% after, slowest just before it, at `baseSlow` 0.4× easing to `peakSlow` 0.15× (was 0.25× and 0.1×, inside E.10's ranges; at the old rates a 2 s shot showed 0.35 s of the throw). The peak's score still orders them, biggest last; the bookmark (D.12) and the opera's hit (E.6) land on the deaths. E.5 says so.
    - **Framed closer (E.4 O6).** The director's shell scoring now favours the 4 m ring (−0.12 a metre out, was a pull toward 7 m), a player's shot follows the subject's middle (`WreckFilm.Middle`: their bounds' centre, steadied over ±0.1 s; the camera pans, it doesn't move, O13), and its field of view is fitted (`Fit`) to hold them at `shotFill` (45%) of the frame's height through the middle of the shot, never over 60% or 70% of the width, between `fovMin` and `fovMax` (22–60°). O1–O5 are checked as before over the whole shot. Someone inside a car through the middle of their shot gets a camera rigged to that car (`FilmShot.Rig`, `WreckFilm.CameraAt`), held in its frame and checked at every frame where it is: held in the world, the car carrying them ran out of the shot at 20 m/s (13% of the frame's height at its end).
    - **A cutaway keeps its floor** (note 251's other answer): a car cut away for a shot is drawn as its deck and sills (`GreyboxScene.CutFloor`), so someone inside reads as lying in a car with its shell lifted off.
    - **A fixture's curve.** `FilmTests`, `FilmPlaybackTests` and `WreckTests` built their line with `new TrackSegment(600, 1 / 300.0)`: a radius of 3 mm, not a curvature of 1/300, so the whole train was coiled on one spot and its wreck exploded upward. Now 300 m. The tests passed either way.
    - **Measured** (`dt film --crew 8`, frontier:7 at 72 km/h; note 251's numbers before): every subject central the whole shot (O6's 80%), at 13–49% of the frame's height (5–40%, mostly under 25%), all from the 4 m ring; every body tumbles 94–180° over the film (they were drawn stood upright through most shots), and 2–10 landings each. The 33 s video: the first person (2.97 s, the host's own death), the replay, eight shots (Lindqvist upside down on car 6's roof, legs in the air; Okafor over the end of car 1's floor; Reyes, Halloran and Petrov on their heads into their cars' floors; Achebe flat out; Dunmore stumbling into the cab front; Dave head first between car 1's roof and the tender), the settle and the cause card. With `--gunner` (Dave, the host, in car 1's gun seat): his own eyes turn over high above the train for 5.1 s, and his shot is a spread-eagled dive onto the track beside the cars going over: thrown 3.3 m clear, 160° over, dead on a 14.7 m/s hit at 1.73 s.
    - **Still weak:** the driver in the cab dies 0.2 s in against its front and stumbles more than flies; crewmates stood alike in like cars tumble alike (the same physics from the same start); the opera still runs out at 24.9 s (note 251).
    - **Tests:** `WreckDeathTests.ACarPloughingIntoABodyThrowsIt` (a car at 10 m/s carries a body standing on the line over 5 m on, never inside it, and its hit is the one that kills, a landing off a car), `TheGunnerStartsClearOfTheGunAndIsThrownUpOutOfTheSeat` (no joint ever inside the gun's solid, up off the seat within 0.1 s, the pelvis over 1 m higher at its height), `EveryBodysLandingsAreRecorded`. `BookmarkStillsTests`' peak camera is the tracking one.
258. **Each of the crew dies on their own hit, not on the derail tick (the director's decision of 5 Oct 2026; GDD v1.4 App. E.2 step 1, E.5's Freeze, E.3's Bystanders).** The run ended and everyone died on the derail tick, and the first person rode your car for a fixed 4 s. Now each player dies in the impact that follows, and their first person ends on it.
    - **The death.** The film's recording (`WreckFilm.Record`, the physics without the shots, deterministic from the film's start) gives each crewmate a `FilmDeath`: their first hit of `impactSpeed` (6 m/s taken off the head, chest or pelvis inside a frame by the ground or a car, so a car hitting them counts), or crushed (a car pressing a joint into the ground). Never hit that hard: their hardest hit. A bystander (E.3, nothing hits them): the settle.
    - **On the host.** `World.Derail` records the film on the derail tick (about 110 ms once; its code is compiled off-thread when a host session starts, `WreckFilm.Warm`, or the first derail held the tick a second longer) and schedules each death (`World.DoomedAt`): the derail tick plus `DeathDelay`, the death's moment in the first person (recorded seconds over `firstPersonRate`, capped so a bystander dies as their first person ends). `ApplyDamage` kills each on their tick, as Derailed. It has to be synchronous: finished on another thread, the tick a death lands on would depend on the wall clock. `World.ShootFilm` reuses the recording.
    - **Till then the wreck has them** (`World.Wrecked`): host and predicting client alike skip their motor and keep only the skip vote of their intent, so they ride where they were in their car's frame; any other damage is ignored (the wreck's hit is what's in the log).
    - **The settlement doesn't change** (E.7: fixed on the derail tick). The run still ends Derailed on that tick, and its report is written then, so the deaths are counted and logged then: on the first derailed tick `ApplyDamage` counts each doomed death ahead (`Bodies.CountAhead`) and writes their Death incident; when the body goes down it isn't counted or logged again. `Run.Tally` brings nobody home from a derailment (the living on the derail tick are the wreck's). Fees, refunds, lines and the cause card are as before.
    - **The mic** hard-cuts on each player's own death (`CutTheDead`), not all at once: a scream on the way down is heard.
    - **Every client's first person ends on its own death.** Every client already shoots the same film from the start it's sent (note 177), so it has every death without anything more on the wire. `IPlaySession.SequenceTuning` is the wreck's tuning with the first person this player's own (`WreckFilm.FirstPersonOf`: `DeathDelay` plus `firstPersonAfter`, 0.8 s, within `firstPersonMin`–`firstPersonMax`, 1.5–6 s); the replay, the cut, the opera, the skip and the HUD all run on from it. While the film's still being shot (a few hundred ms) the first person rides the car as before, for `firstPersonSeconds`.
    - **Through your own eyes.** With the film shot, the first person is your ragdoll's (`DerailSequence.FilmEye`): at its head, facing the way its chest does, rolled with its shoulders, steadied over a few frames, the recording played at `firstPersonRate` (0.4), the film's cars and the rest of the crew as its ragdolls (`Dress`), your own body not drawn, no hands or held tool (the app and `dt film`). You're thrown, it tumbles, the ground comes, and it ends a moment after.
    - **Protocol 28.** The film's start carries each car's gun and doorways and whether a crewmate was seated, and the film it shoots is a different one; mixed builds would shoot different films from one start and disagree on deaths.
    - **GDD:** E.2 step 1, E.3 (the fling's numbers, what collides, bystanders), E.5's Freeze beat and E.10's defaults say so, marked as the director's decision.
    - **Tests:** `WreckDeathTests.EachPlayerDiesInTheirOwnFatalHitNotOnTheDerailTickTheSameEverywhere` (each death is a landing by take 3's rule below, with the hits survived before it counted, never the derail tick; the host's tick is the derail tick plus its delay; alive till it, dead from it, every death counted on the derail tick; the same deaths recorded again and from the start over the wire), `WreckSessionTests.TheCrewRideTheWreckToTheirOwnDeathAndTheFirstPersonEndsOnIt` (a hosted night: their death counted and logged on the derail tick, alive and unmoved by input till their own tick, dead then and not counted twice; the first person as long as the film says). `FilmTests` (determinism over the wire), `NetcodeTests` and the rest pass.
    - **Take 3 (the director, 5 Oct 2026: "much better", and three changes).**
      - **Alive till the hit that kills them.** Each ragdoll is an active one until its death (`WreckFilm.Brace`, `MusclePairs`, `Reach`): muscles (distance constraints at `muscle`, 0.25) toward a brace, elbows and hands up and out, knees bent, over D.9's bones, and its hands reaching each substep (`reach`, 0.03, moved with their history so it's a reach, not a shove) toward a point before the chest the way the body's going. It collides with everything it did before and survives its first hits: two (`surviveHits`) make it vulnerable, and the next of `impactSpeed` (6 m/s) kills; a hit of `certainDeathSpeed` (16 m/s) or being crushed kills at once; past `giveUpSeconds` (2.4 s of recording), or after `mostHits` (3) survived, any hit kills; never killed, it died in its last hit (lying still by then). At the fatal hit its muscles go to zero (`Die`): limp. A bystander has no muscles: it stands its beat, then goes limp. `FilmDeath.Survived` counts the hits taken alive.
      - **What a hit is, now there are muscles.** A head, chest or pelvis velocity change counts only on a substep that joint touched something (the muscles yanking the torso were reading as hits, everyone "dying" 0.1 s in), and the body's middle's (exact for what touched it; bones and muscles pull equal and opposite) counts too, so a hit taken on braced hands and feet is one.
      - **4 s a player in the cut** (`shotSeconds` and `shotMin` 4, `shotCap` 32): the stretch is 55% before the death and 45% after, the hits on the way in and the limp aftermath.
      - **The first person runs 3 s longer: past the death.** The two readings were starting earlier (a ride-in before the derail) or running on after it; the eyes going limp with the body reads as "they die in the impact, and that's the first person we see", and the run-in is already the replay's. `firstPersonAfter` 3.8 (was 0.8), `firstPersonMin`–`firstPersonMax` 4.5–9 s (1.5–6 plus 3); `firstPersonRate` 0.5 (was 0.4) so a death at the give-up time (2.4 s) still lands inside the window, and the host's kill (`DeathDelay`) stays where the first person shows it. With deaths later, the window is capped: one past 2.6 s recorded would be killed by the host at 5.2 s and seen a little later.
      - **The engine's floor is its cab's.** The driver stood on the film's engine at rail height (it has no `Interior`), and take 3's cutaway floor hid him under it; `World.Derail` and `GreyboxScene.CutFloor` now take the cab's floor (`Shape.Cab`).
      - **Length at crew 8:** about 52 s from the first person to the cause card (was 33 s). GDD E.2, E.3 ("Alive till the fatal hit"), E.5 and E.10 say so.
      - **Measured** (`dt film --crew 8`, 51.5 s): hits survived before the fatal one: Dunmore 1, Okafor 3, Reyes 2, Halloran 1, Petrov 2, Achebe 2, Lindqvist 2, Dave 2; everyone central, 7-54% of the frame's height, tumbling 94-164°. On screen: Lindqvist crouched braced on car 6's roof, hands down, then thrown off it; Reyes flailing, arms up, in the air over his car; Dave braced and slammed into the tender; Dunmore out of the cab with an arm up. With `--gunner` (53.4 s): Dave thrown high off the gun car and dead on the track (survived 1, 13.4 m/s); there Okafor, Reyes and Halloran take only one hit, the one that kills (0 survived), and Petrov and Achebe 1.
      - **Still weak:** the first person's limp 3.8 s is mostly dark (a face in the ballast or under a car); crewmates in like cars still move alike; where the wreck gives a body one hit there's nothing to survive; the opera runs out at 24.9 s of a 52 s sequence.
259. **The lone driver breaks the dead out of a Holdout (T115's leftover; GDD App. D.5 "a living crew member begins the breach").** `Heed.Holdouts` only sent a bot with a `StopHand` (a walker or the gunner), and `BotCrew.Think` gave the job to nobody else, so with the driver the last bot alive (a night of one person playing and one bot, or a crew of two whose gunner was dragged) `ConductorBot.ForAHoldout` stood the train at the lit Holdout for its 180 s and drove on without them.
    - **Who goes.** Each bot says whether it's a breacher (`CrewCalls.CanBreach`: a walker or the gunner, alive) and that it's a bot (`CrewCalls.Bot`, so `BotCrew.Think` hands the driver `ConductorBot.Players`, the people playing). Standing alongside a lit Holdout within `Heed.HoldoutRange` with nobody claiming it and nobody at its door, the driver (`ConductorBot.BreachAlone`) goes itself when no breacher is alive, or after `LeaveItToTheCrew` (45 s) unclaimed (one warming in a car can't get down, T115).
    - **Someone playing is preferred at the controls.** One alive in the cab has the brake: the driver goes straight out and vents nothing. One aboard elsewhere gets `ForAPlayerToTheCab` (20 s) to come to it. Otherwise (or with the fireman minding, T107) the driver goes anyway.
    - **Leaving the train to hold**, the switch-back and Switchman pattern (the train stands on its brake, `CabControls.Clears`): brake held and throttle notched off the whole way out; the firebox filled first to `LeavingFire` (0.8), once (a latch: every shovelful opens the door, and waiting for it to shut the fire burnt back under the mark and round again, 145 s at one Holdout); the door shut (App. A.5, only waited on with enemies, since nothing shuts it without them); the gauge vented to `StandingPressure` (60, what it fires to at a stand) at the cab's vent. Then `StopHand.Breach` (down on the Holdout's side, along to the door, Use held) and `StopHand.SetBackAlone` back up the cab steps; `ForAHoldout` finds nothing lit and it drives on (or waits for the freed crewmate to board, T96). Its 180 s give-up (`HoldoutGiveUp`) still counts while it's out, and sends it back.
    - **The gunner in its seat.** In the crew-of-two test the gunner took the breach from the guard gun and sat there "getting down": `Heed.Holdouts` replaced its intent, and with it the Jump that gets it out of the seat (T112). It now adds Jump while seated.
    - **`dt harness` reports `holdouts`**: `lit` (times one lit for someone), `assigned` (D.5's assign events), `breached` (lit ones a breach was begun on), `freed`, `released`, `secondsToFree` (lighting to out: it includes the approach, a facility's lighting 2 km out), `freedBy` (by bot). The trace says `conductor[breaching]`.
    - **Measured** (`dt harness --route frontier:7 --bots 2 --enemies --seconds 1500`; the gunner is dragged at 743 s both times):

      | | before | after |
      |---|---|---|
      | Holdouts lit / breached / freed / released | 2 / 0 / 0 / 1 | 2 / 2 / 2 / 0 |
      | seconds to free | | 110, 93.3 (by the driver) |
      | crew at the end | driver alive, gunner dead | both alive |
      | distance at 1500 s | 13.49 km | 14.37 km |
      | crew-loss fees | 350 | 700 (freed, the gunner was dragged again, and freed again) |

      Out of the cab 15–70 s a breach. Before, the train stood 180 s at each and went on (the second was 13.49 km, where the night ended standing).
    - **Still weak:** the fire's 30 s time constant empties even a full box over a 70 s breach at a facility (0.68 to 0.18, the low fire at 0.2 just missed): a far door means a cold engine. The driver doesn't fire again on its way back. A gunner freed by the driver walks the train on the ground while it's moving and has died of it (`JumpedAtSpeed`, an earlier run): the walkers' boarding, not this.
    - Bot behaviour and the harness report only. No tuning, enemy rule or wire change (protocol stays 28). The new constants are how the driver reads tuning it already has, as note 252's are.
    - **Verified:** `HoldoutTests.ALoneDriverStopsAtALitLockupBreachesItAndDrivesOnWithThemBack` (host, a bot driver and a client killed back up the line, over a loopback at a Halt's Lockup: the driver stops, leaves with the brake on, fired up and vented, the train stands while it's out, it frees them, they're alive inside the Lockup, it's back in the cab and 50 m on) and `WithAWalkerOrGunnerAliveTheDriverLeavesTheBreachToThem` (a crew of two: the gunner frees them and the driver never leaves the cab).
260. **The line's own kills are telegraphed: a roof warning before a tunnel's mouth and a bend taken too fast (T115 playtest, "random death walking outside"; GDD App. A.1's fairness contract).** `Lineside.Hazards` struck anyone stood on a roof at a tunnel's mouth (`Struck`, 200) and threw roof riders off a posted stretch taken `throwOver` too fast (`Thrown`). The only warning was a board the headlamp had to find (lamps down, the mouth itself 10 m short), and the HUD said what happened only on the death screen. App. A.1: "TELEGRAPH always precedes COMMIT", and the line's kills aren't whole-train events.
    - **The warning** (`Lineside.Warns`, `Warning`; tuned in `sight.json` `roofWarning`). A board's stretch is warned of once it's within `leadSeconds` (10) of the leading car at the speed now, plus `leadMargin` (30 m, a train gathering speed), with speeds under `minSpeed` (3 m/s) counted as that, until the last car's through. Every tunnel is warned of. A posted stretch (a sharp bend or a weak bridge) is warned of only with the train within `curveHeadroom` (1.5 m/s) of throwing riders off it (`ThrowsAbove`: `throwOver` over the board, more with handrails). It comes from the route and the train alone, **lamp or no lamp**: running dark costs the driver the boards (note 71), not the roof riders their warning. Every machine works it out the same, so nothing new is sent. **No wire change; protocol stays 28.** Prediction is untouched: the hazards are still host-side, and the warning's counter only gates them.
    - **No commit without it.** `See` counts each board's warning ticks on every machine. A hazard takes nobody until its warning has been up a full `leadSeconds` (`WarnedInTime`; counted before the step, so one tick more than the HUD shows). Otherwise the rider is spared (`Lineside.Spared`, which should be zero). The fairness contract is held by construction, not just by tuning: a train surging over a board, or reversing into a mouth, can't kill without the warning.
    - **Why 10 s:** App. A.1's reaction window at its longest (4 s), plus about 6 s to walk from mid-roof to an end ladder (14 m car, `walk` 2.4 m/s) and climb down it (`ladderClimb` 1.6 m/s).
    - **Who gets it** (`Lineside.For`, `roofOnly`, on): whoever is up top, on a roof or on an end ladder, and not a gun's crew behind its shield (clear of both hazards). Off: everyone aboard, the cab included.
    - **HUD** (`Hud.RoofWarningLines`, in `Alerts`): "LOW CLEARANCE / TUNNEL MOUTH IN 6S: GET OFF THE ROOF" ("IN THE TUNNEL: STAY OFF THE ROOF" once in it), and "TOO FAST FOR THE BEND" (or "BRIDGE", `Sign.Bridge`) / "50 KM/H BOARD IN 6S: GET OFF THE ROOF", flashing red and amber.
    - **By ear** (`GameAudio.Lineside.cs`): two new tier-1 sounds, each a synth definition as every tell started. They play as the warning goes up and every `repeatSeconds` (4) while it's up, to whoever it's for. **`warn-low-clearance`**, the telltales: a run of cord slaps over the roof just ahead, 4–7 kHz (the railways hung cords before low bridges and tunnels so a brakeman felt them before the portal). **`warn-curve`**, the roof irons chattering, rocking in and out, at your feet, 2.5–4.5 kHz. Both are in spec A.4's table, told from the tells sharing their bands by rhythm, and in `AudioBench.TellBands` (every roof must hear them, the cab needn't). `dt audio render --listener all` (chaos): warn-low-clearance is 28.3 dB over the bed at its worst listener and warn-curve 22.0 dB (the floor is 6). `AudioTests` are green.
    - **Not the whistle, not a shout.** No whistle code exists in the GDD or spec (App. C has the whistle only as a source of loudness). A whistle nobody pulled is the Whistler's tell (App. A.4) and feeds the Choir's meter (C.7). A fireman's shout needs a voice, and a fireman. So the cue is the line's own, needing nobody in the cab.
    - **Ambiguity: does a crouch clear a mouth?** Neither the GDD nor the spec says, and the keyboard's body has no crouch at all. The kept reading is note 71's "anyone standing on a roof": it doesn't. That's a flag, `crouchClearsMouth` (off). On, a headset rider whose reported head (`PlayerState.Head`, T82) is down under `crouchHead` (1.0 m) is clear.
    - **Bots get down on the same warning.** `RoofWalkerBot.RoofWarned` (was `TunnelNear`: a tunnel whose board had been read, within 40 s) is now `Lineside.Warning` from its client world, through intent as before. So walkers shelter for a bend taken too fast too, and for a tunnel in the dark. They now go down 10 s out, not 40.
    - **`dt`:** `dt screenshot --hud --roof-warning tunnel|bend [--route r]` stages it (`Staging.RoofWarning`: 6 s short of the first mouth or posted bend past the yard, up on car 2's roof). With no route it's deepTerritory:2, because frontier:7 has neither on its main line. `dt playthrough` reports `roofWarning`: warnings, warnings with riders up, hazards that took someone (`Lineside.Commits`, host), the shortest lead any had, any taken without a full warning, `spared`, and roof deaths by cause.
    - **Verified:** `LinesideTests`:
      - `EveryRoofDeathOnTheLineIsWarnedAFullLeadAhead`: each commit is measured against the warning as the HUD saw it. Runs: a tunnel at 12 m/s; a tunnel at 22 m/s, dark; a tunnel gathering speed; a bend well over its board, lit and dark; and a surge over a board 10 m short of it (the throw waits for the warning).
      - `ABendTakenAtItsBoardIsNoWarningAndATunnelAlwaysIs`.
      - `TheWarningIsForWhoeverIsUpTop`.
      - `ACrouchClearsAMouthOnlyIfTunedTo`.
      - `WalkersGetOffTheRoofsOnTheRoofWarning`: a tunnel lit, dark and at 20 m/s, and a bend too fast. Both walkers live, and both are off the roof within the lead.
      - `AWalkerDownOnAPlate…` starts 120 m from the mouth now, inside the lead.
      - The other Lineside tests are unchanged.
    - **Playthroughs (20 min each).**
      - **frontier:7 solo:** 0 warnings, 0 roof hazards. frontier:7 has no tunnel and no posted bend on its main line. The solo night also covers only 0.25 km, the same as on the base commit: the lone driver never gets out of the yard, which this change doesn't touch.
      - **frontier:7, 4 bots:** 12.2 km, 0 warnings, 0 taken, 0 roof deaths.
      - **deepTerritory:2, 4 bots:** 11.3 km. 1 warning, with riders up; 0 taken, 0 without a full warning, 0 spared, 0 roof deaths. The walkers got down.
    - **Not yet:**
      - Nothing on the line shows the telltales. The cue is diegetic in sound only.
      - The two cues are synth definitions with no audio-checklist line (cues.py) for recorded candidates.
      - The cab isn't told that riders are up (roofOnly). With it off, everyone gets the warning.
261. **Cargo stops for the crew that's there (T114 "cargo stops"; GDD §17–18, spec D.2).** `StopDriver` made only the stops its bots could work alone, and only as an eight would (`CrewCalls.CanWorkWinch`: a shunter and both winch hands; crates wanted a hand besides the shunter), and people playing weren't counted at all. A crew of two (the driver and the gunner, who is the shunter) or one bot and someone playing, the default way to play, loaded nothing all night.
    - **The reading (ambiguous, so `facilities.json` "crew").** Spec D.2's "Crew" column counts people at a module, not their jobs. Read so its own table stays true for a small crew: the shunter is free once the train's in and is one of a module's crew; the driver is too, with too few others (`driverWorks`, on); people playing are hands (`peopleAreHands`, on). The "2 mandatory" of the winch and the crane, the ramp's 2 and the crates' "heavy items need two" are unchanged: the pair is still two at two handles.
    - **Who covers what** (`CrewCalls`): `PairBots` is the winch pair, then the shunter, then the driver, up to two. A pair module is planned for when the bots and the people between them make two (`PairWithPeople`); the bots take their places, the first missing handle the shunter's, the next the driver's (`PairPart`, `Lend`). Crates: the shunter carries where fewer than two bots do (one or two carry), the driver only where nobody else can (`Carries`). The hose wants one, its own hand or a spare. The spout's lever stays the shunter's (the driver's walking the cars under it).
    - **The shunter** (`StopHand`) takes its lent part at the end of the spur (the pair parts by `Pair`, not its job), puts anything in its arms down when there's none left, and goes back to the cab (`Done`, not `Aboard`): the switch is still its to set back.
    - **The driver** gets down as it does for a Holdout (note 259's pattern, now `ConductorBot.ReadyToLeave`): brake held, the firebox filled (to 0.95 for this: a crate run is minutes, and at a stand a full box lasts it a minute), the door shut, vented to its standing pressure. `StopDriver.DriverPart` says what for; `ConductorBot.WorkOut` works it through a `StopHand` (`Lend`) and climbs back up when it's done, the loading's over, or the fire's down to 1.25× the low fire (crates in its arms put down first; App. A.5's Stoker comes for a low fire). With no shunter it throws the spur's switch itself (`StopDriver.ThrowAlone`, after `AloneAfter`) and sets it back as before; it can't get between the cars to cut, so it plans only stops the train goes into whole, no pick-ups, and if the shunter dies before the cut it goes on at once instead of standing out the 240 s.
    - **People who don't come.** The driver watches the loading (`StopDriver.Watch`: what's in the cars, the sled along its haul, the castings set down). With nothing moving for `peopleWait` (45 s) it calls it idle (`CrewCalls.PeopleIdle`), and what only people could have done (the other handle, crates nobody bot carries) is left: `PairNow`, `Loaded`. The people playing are told nothing new; the bots just stop waiting.
    - **A cut that runs away is lost, not chased.** The traces of these nights showed the Passenger taking a hand in a car at deepTerritory:2's mine head and letting the cut's brakes off: note 188's backing-out closes on a rolling cut over its own speed, without a ceiling, so a crew of three's engine went after it at 22 m/s for 7 km and a crew of eight's (on main's code as well) to km 0.16. Past `SetBackTop` away, or still rolling with the engine 400 m back past its points (`ChaseFor`), the cut's gone: back to the points without it, and on.
    - **frontier:7 says nothing about it.** Its dawn leaves 2.4 min over the ideal run (note 188), so `StopDriver`'s reckoning skips every facility whatever the crew: crews of 2, 3 and 8 make no facility stop before or after (`dt harness --route frontier:7 --enemies --seconds 1500`: 14.37 km, net −1,313; 16.62 km, −1,005; 17.46 km, −498; identical). The T114 night of a crew of two had no stop for the time as much as the crew. Every frontier route checked (1–11) has under 4 min of slack; deepTerritory has the time for its first stop.
    - **Measured** (`dt harness --route R --bots N --enemies --seconds 1500`; gross is 0 throughout, as nothing's delivered by 1,500 s):

      | night | before | after |
      |---|---|---|
      | deepTerritory:2, crew 2 | no stop; 18.32 km; 2 deaths, 1 lost; net −2,669 | mine head made; the gunner (shunter) dragged on the way to the cut, the driver goes on 46 s later; 17.42 km; 1 death, 0 lost; net −1,658 |
      | deepTerritory:2, crew 3 | mine head, crates only (0 sleds), 389 s; 13.77 km; cargo 2.18; 1 death; net −1,567 | mine head, both sleds (the shunter on the second handle) and the crates, 548 s; the Passenger takes the shunter carrying in car 4 and the cut rolls away; 12.36 km; cargo 0.29; 2 deaths; net −2,475 |
      | deepTerritory:2, crew 8 | mine head never finished: the cut chased to km 0.16; 4 deaths; net −3,795 | mine head, 1 sled, 688 s, the cut let go at 400 m; 10.58 km; 3 deaths; net −2,926 |
      | deepTerritory:1, crew 2 | no stop; 15.68 km; cargo 2.78; 2 deaths; net −2,354 | mine head made, but the gunner (shunter) is dragged off the guard van at 617 s on the way in, as before: given up at once; 16.62 km; cargo 2.33; 2 deaths; net −2,466 |
      | deepTerritory:1, crew 3 | mine head, crates only (0 sleds), 390 s; 14.25 km; cargo 1.21; 0 deaths; net −845 | mine head, both sleds and the crates, 545 s; 12.35 km; cargo 2.02; a hand burned in a car fire there (850 fee); net −1,660 |

      One night each, and enemy nights vary a lot. What's sure: the crews of three now haul the winch (2 sleds where they hauled none), and the crews of two make the stop; on all four crew-of-two nights the gunner, who is their shunter, was dragged off the guard van at or near the stop, before the cut, so they loaded nothing there (the tests show them loading). The crew of eight's night is better for the runaway cut, not for the crew rules (an eight has every part).
    - **Still weak:** a shunter or a driver on the ground and in the cars at a stop is one more for the Passenger and the Dragger (the crew-of-3 night above lost its shunter so). The lone driver's fire wants it back every minute or so, and a lone driver's crate stop takes most of the 300 s loading give-up (the test's three cars: 300 s, the last door shut at the wire). The driver doesn't coal alone (the chute's lever is the shunter's). A train left with a lost cut on the line plans no more stops (`StopDriver` Cruise wants one rake). People playing aren't told which handle is theirs.
    - Bot behaviour, the harness trace (`conductor[Loading: cranking]`) and one tuning block (`facilities.json` "crew", `StopCrewTuning`). No payment, loading rule or wire change (protocol stays 28).
    - **Verified:** `StopCrewTests.ACrewOfTwoStopsAndTheShunterCarriesTheCratesIn`, `ACrewOfTwoWorksTheWinchTheShunterAndTheDriverOnItsHandles` (both sleds; the driver down and back in the cab), `OneBotAndSomeonePlayingStillStopAndTheBotWorksIt` (a driver and a person standing on a roof: the driver throws the switch, carries every crate in, shuts the doors, sets the switch back; the person never moves); `ACrewWithoutTheWinchPairRunsStraightPast` now with `driverWorks` off (with it on, the shunter and the driver are the pair).
262. **The demo's creatures, higher poly and textured denser, and their clips checked for parts through parts (the Look Review: "some things are coming out looking weird... continue working on better models"; "go ahead with the creatures animation pass and updated higher poly model & texture pass").** The Track Doll, Car Hugger, Whistler, Tippy Toesie, Ribbits and the Choir.
    - **More triangles alone didn't show.** `rig.densify` (a Catmull-Clark level with the hard edges creased, collapsed back to the part's share of `kit.target_tris`) took the Ribbit from 3.8k to 7.4k triangles, and close up its before and after were the same picture. What read as low quality was how the kit builds a body: limbs are tubes pushed into a trunk, with a seam and often a gap at every hip and shoulder, and a head of stacked discs.
    - **`rig.fuse`: one skin from several parts.** This is the crew body's method (`crewbody.py`, note 145), now for any creature:
      - `kit.fuse(name, parts, voxel, faces, cut)` welds the parts and fills their holes (a tube's UV seam is a column of doubled vertices and its ends are open, so without this the remesh wraps both sides of every surface). It voxel-remeshes them into one surface, keeps only the outward shells, relaxes it, and retopologises it to the game mesh.
      - Each new face takes the material of the nearest part face. Each vertex takes the bone weights of the nearest point on the parts, interpolated over that face, then relaxed over the skin's edges, at most four bones a vertex.
      - `cut(point)` opens the skin again where it was meant to be open (the Car Hugger's mouth). It cuts the dense union and then the game mesh, and QuadriFlow is told to keep the edge.
      - Fine parts stay out of the fused skin: hands, fingers, eyes, teeth, lips, insect legs. A voxel union melts them, and QuadriFlow drops them.
    - **QuadriFlow, checked.** It drops limbs thinner than its quads (Tippy's shin went), refuses a mesh with any edge under 0.1 mm a side, and isn't the same run to run (one Whistler union gave 1,576 good quads one run and 683 missing a third of the body the next). So `fuse` tries it with seeds 0 to 3, symmetric across x. It measures each result against the union and keeps the first that leaves at most 1.5% of the union further than 4 voxels (8 mm at least) from it. Otherwise it collapses the union to the same count, which never loses a form but spends its triangles less evenly. The build log says which it took. **Not deterministic:** a rebuild can give a slightly different mesh. The budgets, the clearance check and the renders are what's pinned.
    - **What changed, per creature (triangles; GDD §27):**
      - **Ribbit 3.8k → 7.6k.** Body and legs are one skin, so the haunches and the legs grow out of the flanks. The head is remodelled as a toad's flat wedge (`SKULL` sections) with a rolled lip over the gums. The eye mounds, the paratoid glands, the nostrils and the lids are one fused skull skin. The eyes are cloudy grey-green with a toad's flat-bar pupil. The ears are pinched at the root and cupped along their length. The jaw is a deeper scoop, and the paws stay a separate piece.
      - **Tippy Toesie 4.4k → 8.5k.** The neck, torso, arms and legs are one skin, with knees, calves and thighs under it. The hands are a separate piece, given knuckles and fingers that curl and spread.
      - **Car Hugger 13.5k (unchanged).** The hide and arms are one skin with the mouth cut open again, and the hands are a separate piece. The gap between the lip ring and the body's rim is gone.
      - **Whistler 8.5k.** The head's wedge and the siphon are fused with the segmented body. Its legs have a coxa knuckle, a swollen femur with a ridge and a spine (two on the forelegs), and banded tibias.
      - **Track Doll 9.2k and Choir 2.9k** are densified only. The doll is jointed porcelain, so its seams are meant.
    - **Textures.**
      - `overbake.Atlas.unwrap` now uses smart-project at 75 degrees with tight margins, which fills 54–56% of each atlas instead of 38–55%.
      - The Car Hugger bakes to 2048. It wraps most of a car (38 m² of skin), and at 1024 it came out about 120 px/m.
      - **The renderer has a 2048 hero tier** for layers authored above 1024 (`look.json` `bigHeroLayerSize`). The scene set gains bindings 11–13, and a hero slot from 64 up samples the big arrays (`scene.frag`).
      - Texel density, against GDD §27's hero target of 256–512 px/m: Ribbit 434, Tippy 476, Choir 499, Track Doll 312, Whistler 282, Car Hugger 247. The Car Hugger is about 4% short; its belly and the inside of its funnel take a share of the atlas they don't need.
    - **A distance copy for each, so the frame stays in budget.** At their full budgets the six creatures put the headset's trackside view at 1.57M triangles a frame, over `perf.json`'s 1.5M, because each is drawn for two eyes and the shadows (`PerfBudgetTests`). The precedent (note 102) was to trim the models to fit. Instead:
      - `overbake.finish(lod=0.4)` also exports `<name>.lod1.glb` (`rig.export_lod`): every part of 400 triangles or more collapsed to two fifths, symmetric, on the same rig, clips, atlas and materials.
      - `CreatureArt` loads it with its joints renumbered by name to the full model's. Past `look.json`'s `creatureLodMetres` (14 m) from the eye it draws the copy, posed with the full model's palette. The scene is built about the eye, so a creature's distance is its matrix's origin.
      - Up close, as near as one comes to anyone, it's the whole model. `dt art clip` and the reel draw at the origin, so they always show the full model.
      - `CreatureArtTests.TheDemoCreaturesHaveADistanceCopy` checks each copy: a quarter to three fifths of the triangles, the same materials, and every joint one of the full model's.
    - **`Clearance.Mesh`: any model's clips checked for parts through parts, from its own mesh.**
      - Each bone with 24 or more vertices carried mostly by it becomes a capsule fitted to them: along their longest spread, from the 10th to the 90th percentile of it, its radius their median distance off that line, so the capsule sits inside the skin.
      - Every pair of bones three or more joints apart is measured at 30 fps. The finding is how much deeper the pair overlaps than it does at rest.
      - Bones a clip scales are left out (the throat sac, the Car Hugger's rings): they're meant to swell into their neighbours.
      - `dt art clearance --only <model>` uses it for anything but the crew's figures (`--mesh` forces it). `CreatureArtTests.TheDemoCreaturesStayOutOfThemselves` fails any of the six deeper than `Clearance.Touching` (6 cm).
    - **What it found, all fixed:**
      - The Car Hugger's left upper arm, folded in lurk, went 0.29 m into its body and across the mouth. The mouth sits over to that side, so that hand's fold target was inside the lip; it now folds down beside it.
      - The Track Doll's hand "at its chest" in admire was at its cheek, and went 0.11 m into the head as it snapped round. The hand is now at the chest.
      - The Whistler's coil went through itself (0.14 m) in fold. It's wound a little looser and its front rides up over the tail's turn, still under the plate (`WhistlerTests`, 1.05 m).
      - The Choir's rim curled through its curtain on a hit.
      - The Ribbit's jaw went into its chest in devour, and an ear swung into its flank on a hit.
    - **Verified:** CreatureArtTests (budgets, loops, the crew's and the creatures' clearance), WhistlerTests, RibbitTests and TippyToesieTests. Each creature was looked at before and after, close up and in its clips (`dt art clip`), and in the Look Review's reel.
263. **Build 1121's playtest: the safe yard, the runaway nobody drove, the kit "the train has none", the Stoker redesigned, car fires (the director's decisions of 6 Oct 2026).** The director hosted frontier:7 alone (4 cars, no bots) and reported six bugs. Each was reproduced headless and root-caused before it was fixed.
    - **The train left on its own.** The cause was not the throttle, the gun seat's controls, the run start or a bot driver. The host spawns in the cab with the brake held (`HostSession`: with steam driving, T97, a standing engine off its brake pulls away). The director went up to the roof gun and sat there. Nobody fired the boiler, and in the yard the pressure fell under 40. After 45 s at that, App. B.5's Stoker came down the stack, and its FEED `Tamper` ramped the applied brake to 0. The train moved off with nobody in the cab, through the gate (so the run began), and up to 22 m/s. The Stoker held the safety valve shut, so the pressure reached 100 and 20 s later the boiler ruptured. Reproduced by `SafeYardTests.ALoneHostAtTheGunAndTheTrainNeverLeavesWithoutThem`: the app's own `NetPlaySession.HostGame`, the host put at the roof gun and seated, ten minutes. Before the fix, the Stoker arrived at 335 s and the train moved at 365 s. It never slowed because the Stoker overrode the brake each tick. With nobody in the cab, `HostSession` never clears or re-applies the held brake, so nothing put it back. Two fixes:
      - `enemies.json` `tamperReleasesStandingBrake` (false): whatever is at the controls (the Stoker's runaway, the Track Doll's tampering), a train standing on its held brake stays on it (`World.Step`; host and clients alike). Under way, they still take the brake off.
      - The safe yard (below): nothing spawns there at all.
    - **The fortress is a safe space** (the director's decision; GDD §9 "The fortress is a safe space"; `run.json` `yardIsSafe`). `World.SafeYard` holds while the run is in `RunPhase.Yard`, and is read from the replicated run phase, so clients predict the same. While it holds:
      - the director doesn't think: no spawns, no Stoker, no marsh;
      - the Choir's meter doesn't step;
      - `TrainOnLine.HeldInYard` skips `Boiler.Step`, so there is no pressure gain or loss, no coal burned and no rupture;
      - `PlayerMotor.StepCold` returns early.
      The director's grace counts from the run's start, not from the world's first tick, so half an hour at the gate doesn't spend it. The train moves only when someone in the cab lets the brake off (`CabControls.ReleasesBrake`) and drives through the gate. Verified by `SafeYardTests.HalfAnHourAfkInTheYardAndNothingHappens`: host and joiner AFK (one in the cab, one on a roof) for 30 minutes. Nothing moves, the gauge, fire and tender are unchanged, there are no spawns, health and cold are unchanged, and nothing is logged.
    - **"Boiler ruptured: the repair kit mends it, and the train has none."** The check itself was right. Its input was wrong: `Hud.RepairKitWhere` read the kit from this machine's bodies, and the host sends only bodies within `interestRadius` (520 m). The director was left behind by a runaway train, so the kit was out of range and the HUD said the train had none. The same applied to a kit in any locker, in a crewmate's hands or on a roof. The host's `Run.Kit` (`EngineeringKit.Where`: place, car, loss) was never on the wire, so a client's `Kit.Lost` was always false and "STRANDED WHEN SHE STOPS" never showed on any machine but the host's. Now the run record carries it (`RunHead` 6 → 9; **protocol 29**; the pinned version in `RejoinTests` and `CrewCapTests` updated), and the HUD falls back to it when no kit body is in sight. `KitCarry` (the bots' fetch from car 2 and back) and the run report already read the host's bodies: they were right, and are unchanged. Verified by `SafeYardTests.LeftBehindTheTrainYouAreStillToldWhereTheKitIs`: the run under way, the host a kilometre back, ruptured. No kit body is on the client, `Run.Kit` says Lying, and the HUD says "IT'S IN CAR 1".
    - **The Stoker clubbed through a shut door.** `World.Swing` struck anything in reach. App. A.5's KILL is "open the firebox and bludgeon it". New `Enemy.Reachable(World)`: a Stoker in the fire is reachable only with `Boiler.FireDoorOpen` (a shovelful opens it). A blow at the shut door gets the metal ring (`GameAudio.Crew`). The bots' `FightStoker` shovels to open the door, then swings. Verified by `Build1121Tests.TheStokerIsClubbedOnlyThroughTheOpenFireboxDoor`.
    - **The Stoker, redesigned** (the director's decision, GDD App. A.5 and B.5 rewritten; `enemies.json` `stoker`). This replaces App. B.5's low fire and open door. "It came straight back" had the same root as the yard bug: the low-pressure clock kept running while one was in, so killing it under a low gauge brought the next one within a second.
      - **Drawn by heat.** A firebox at `heatFirebox` (4.5 of 6) or more for `heatSeconds` (20) draws it. A low fire never does.
      - **Boards at the tender.** `Stoker.AtTender`: on the coal, then it crosses the footplate to the fire door over `boardSeconds` (8, its telegraph). `Extra2` = boarding, replicated. It is drawn on the coal with its sick glow (`CreatureArt`) and the hiss loops from where it is.
      - **Caught on the way in.** A blow while it's boarding counts `tenderBlowScale` (4) times over and doesn't burn: one blow.
      - **Worse once in.** It eats `eatPerSecond` (0.2) of the fire. Its heat swings, `feedRate` 4 ± `swing` 3 over 3 s, with the valve held shut, so the gauge climbs in surges. The swing stays under the feed, so the gauge never falls back and resets the rupture clock. The brake ramps off over `runawayRampSeconds` (20). From 75 the boiler goes in about 26 s.
      - **A break.** None comes for `breakSeconds` (150) after one's gone (`World.StokerBreakSeconds`; the heat clock doesn't run meanwhile). However it went, the boiler's external heat and the jammed valve are cleared, including when the director's linger rule dismissed it and skipped its `Leave`.
      - `Stoker.InFirebox` stays, for tests, audits and the director's insist.
      Verified by `Build1121Tests`:
      - `ALowOrWarmFireNeverDrawsTheStokerAndAHotOneDoes`;
      - `ItBoardsAtTheTenderAndTelegraphsBeforeItGetsIn` (fair: Telegraph ≥ `boardSeconds` before Commit);
      - `TheFiremanCatchesItAtTheTenderAndItStaysGoneForTheBreak` (no burn; the next one comes at break + heat ±2 s);
      - `OnceInItEatsTheFireAndSwingsTheGaugeToARupture` (20–45 s);
      - `AStokerFeedingAStandingTrainDoesNotTakeItOffItsBrake`.
      `RosterTests` uses a hot fire now.
    - **"One extinguisher douses the whole train."** Spraying was already per car. The cause was T114's linger rule (`World.Unmet`): any engaged threat with nobody within 12 m for 120 s was dismissed, car fires included. While the crew fought one fire, the others went out by themselves. Car fires are now exempt. Verified by `Build1121Tests.AFireWithNobodyNearKeepsBurning`. **Time to put one out:** neither App. C.5 nor the spec gives a number, so there's nothing to be over. With one extinguisher it takes about 7 s caught as it takes hold (0.35), about 12 s at 0.6, and at full blaze one charge (15 s) isn't enough, by design ("a big one takes several extinguishers"). **For the director:** spraying ignores aim. It counts within `sprayReach` (2.5 m) of the fire's anchor on the cargo face, but the flames are drawn further along the car than that. Aiming at the flames 3 m away does nothing, which may be "nothing is really going out".
    - **"Touching fire kills instantly."** A fire burned `10 × intensity` every 2 s, on a timer per fire rather than per player. Walking in just before a pulse cost 10 at once, and that was all the fire could do. The likelier cause is that the director's health was already gone. A clubbed Stoker burns 12 a blow, 48 for one with health 4, and they fought two. There is no regeneration, so they had about 4 health left, and any burn killed them. The new burn is by the second (the director's decision; `enemies.json` `carFire.burnPerSecond` 10, `burnBrushShare` 0.5, `burnRampSeconds` 3; `CarFire.Burn`, a dose per player). A brush costs a few points. A second in a full blaze costs about 6, and standing in it kills in about 11 s. Verified by `Build1121Tests.AFireBurnsByTheSecondABrushHurtsAndStandingInItKills`.
    - **Needs the director's call:**
      - Should the Track Doll's tampering also leave a standing train on its brake? It does now (`tamperReleasesStandingBrake` covers both).
      - Spray aim against reach (above).
      - Whether a clubbed Stoker should cost less health. Two fights take nearly all of it.
      - The heat threshold against what the bots fire to. The driver bot keeps a full box at a stand, so bot crews now meet the Stoker more (not swept).

264. **The director's notes on build 1121 (6 Oct 2026): the menus take the mouse, fields don't type until entered, a lighter cab, the whistle cord, the lockers, found toys, no manifest at the gate, and one supplies view.** (Notes 262–266 are left for work in flight.)
    - **Mouse in the menus.** `FrontEnd.Draw` records where each row (and each value's `<` `>` arrows) was drawn; `HitTest`, `Hover`, `Click` and `Scroll` work against that. `MenuInput` (Game) is the one place keys, typing and the pointer meet the front end, so `FrontEndTests` click it headless. The window gained `CaptureOnClick` (off in the menus: a click is a click, not the window taking the mouse), a free pointer position (`InputState.MouseX/Y`, 0..1) and the wheel uncaptured; the app maps them to the 480x270 overlay. Lists scroll by the wheel, or follow the selection when the keys move it. VR keeps `VrMenuInput`.
    - **Text fields.** `TextField` (lobby name, join address, and a new PLAYER NAME in the settings): choosing one doesn't take typing; Enter or a click starts it, Enter, Esc, a click elsewhere or moving off ends it. `WantsText` is only while editing, so WASD and Space navigate everywhere else.
    - **Cab HUD.** In the cab the engine panel is the speed and the levers (`BRAKE ON  [X] FWD`); the steam speed, pressure bar, fire and coal are the backhead's gauges' (GDD §32). The route's name and the next place left the bottom-left block (the top strip names the next place now). The driving prompt is the brake, the vent and the lamp. Before/after: `out/before/hud-cab.png`, `out/after/hud-cab.png`.
    - **Vent.** One key, held anywhere in the cab (`Control.Vent`, default Left Ctrl). The wire's `PlayerActions` byte is full, so it shares `Uncouple`'s bit (that's only on a coupler plate, never in the cab), as Bookmark and Skip share one; `CrewActions.VentHeld`, predicted like the rest. Use at the valve still works. While it's open the prompt says `VENTING STEAM: PRESSURE n, FALLING`; the red-line alerts name the key.
    - **Picking by look** (train.json `pick`). `CrewActions.NearestInteractable` takes the candidate in reach nearest the view's centre within `lookDegrees` (cosines, no Acos: it's deterministic), from `eyeHeight`; none looked at, the nearest as before. `Interactable.Aim` is each thing's height over its footing. Neighbouring lockers, and the firebox, vent, rack and cord, no longer fight over one press; two players work neighbouring lockers at once.
    - **Whistle cord.** `InteractableKind.Whistle` in the driver's front corner over the brake valve (1.26 m from the firebox, outside its 1.1 m reach), a signal-red T-handle where the driver's view has it; Use held on it, looked at (never by proximity), blows the whistle as H does, in the puller's name. `WhistleBy` now rides the World record (appended; old readers ignore it), and the HUD says "THE WHISTLE: <NAME> ON THE CORD". The Whistler's whistle has no name, which keeps App. A.4's tell ("no hand on the cord") readable.
    - **"The whistle went off by itself"** (item 8). Only two things blow it: a player's whistle action (H, or now the cord) and the Whistler (`Flank.cs`: dormant in a gap, it blows the train's whistle `whistleAfterStop` 4 s into any stop, then waits). Bots never whistle, nothing else is bound to it, and the cord had no use volume before this note. A whistle at a stop with nobody on H was the Whistler's tell working as designed; nothing said so. Now a crew whistle names its puller, so an unnamed one is the Whistler.
    - **Lockers.** A tap at a shut locker opens it, and what's in your hands goes in on the same tap (it used to do nothing); empty-handed the tap only opens it. `kit.lockers.stock` (not in the GDD, which names only the fitter's kit; the director's call): DRIVER a lamp, FIREMAN an extinguisher, GUARD a lamp, BRAKESMAN an extinguisher, LAMPMAN two lamps; the rest empty for finds. A shut locker with something in it has a stores tag on its handle (`LockerKit.Tag`), and the prompt at its door names what's in it ("THE FITTER'S LOCKER: THE REPAIR KIT   [E] OPEN"). What's carried in both hands shows lit at the hotbar's left ("IN HANDS: THE REPAIR KIT").
    - **Toys** (item 6). train.json `kit.toys` is 0 and `toyNoises` empty; loot.json `toys` puts them in village cupboards, cellars, haylofts and under floors (chance 0.4 each, about two a village), each noise drawn from two quiet, a squeaker, a music box and a drummer, on their own seed stream (`StopSeed.Toy`), so the finds and kits dealt are unchanged. The Track Doll's appeasement now wants a toy found first.
    - **Manifest** (item 7). It said "Yard to consist. Manifest follows. Crew: <name>... Coal: n. Powder and shot: n. Cars: n. Freight: ... Gates open. Yard out." run.json `radio.manifest` false drops it, voice and card; everything it read is on the roster, the gauges, the supplies view and the contract. `Radio.Manifest` stays for `dt` and tests; the clerk's tally home is unchanged.
    - **Supplies view** (the director's decision of 2026-10-06, recorded in GDD §10). `Hud.Supplies`, toggled with I (`Control.Supplies`), never always on: coal and fire, the kit and where it is (and spares), extinguishers, cargo cars loaded and crates, stores (lamps, radios, toys, finds), powder and shot. Water isn't modelled by the sim (the boiler has pressure, fire and the tender's coal), so it has no row.
    - **Heads-up taken** (item 10): the director will redesign the front of the train for a lone driver's view. The cab layout is only moved as far as these fixes needed (the cord); every function stays.
    - **Verified:** `CabPickTests` (cord placement, look-picking, the cord in the puller's name over the wire, the Whistler's with none, the vent key), `LockerTests` (tap-opens, stock, neighbours by look, two at once), `ChoirShareTests.ToysAreFoundAtTheStops...`, `FrontEndTests` (mouse hover, click, arrows, wheel, fields), `HudTests` (cab prompts, lighter panel, locker tag, supplies), `RadioCardTests.TheManifestAtTheGateIsDropped...`, `NetcodeTests`. Screenshots: `dt screenshot --hud --route frontier:7 [--cord | --lockers NAME [--carrying kit] | --supplies]`, and `--menu title|host|join`.
265. **A derailment is the driver's mistake, told in time: boards on the bends that derail, a train you can hear, the stress before it comes off, and no Sleepers (build 1121, solo frontier:7; the director's decision of 6 Oct 2026).** The director's notes: "20" boards on near-straight track; "the train doesn't appear to be making any noise while it's on the rail"; "we'll want to telegraph using sound design that the train is going under stress"; "Consist derailed … ran onto the Sleepers at 43 km/h … I didn't see the threat"; "I really don't get why we derailed before it said we hit sleepers"; "there's no lanterns, the lights are completely off". Then: "the Sleepers go … a derailment needs to be clearly a mistake by the driver, someone not paying attention to the map."
    - **The boards on straight track were brass, and the cab map put them on the nearest bend.** frontier:7's sharpest bend is 707 m (it derails at √(1.0 × 707) = 26.6 m/s, over the train's 22), so plan §8.5 boards none of them, rightly. Its two "20" boards are its brass fields' cutting speed (6 m/s, LimitSource.Brass), on straight track, and the board said only "20". The run map (`GreyboxScene.PostedBends`, T121) took every speed board and inked its figure on "the sharpest curve in the next 600 m", however gentle (an 1,186 m and a 6,144 m curve here). The "45" resume boards were the Sleepers' restricted zones (13 m/s). The radius-to-limit mapping itself (`CurveLimits`, the T111 boards) was right.
        - A non-bend limit now says what it is: a red **BRASS** plate on its own post 5 m past the speed board (signage.json `limitReason`; a weak bridge already had MAX N CARS).
        - The map inks a brass limit over its own stretch in the map's ink, "20 BRASS"; a weak bridge's in red, "N BRIDGE"; a bend's figure only on a bend whose derailing speed is within 1.5× + 1 m/s of the board's figure. Everything that can derail the train is red, with a key on the title line: "IN RED: KM/H OR OFF THE RAILS".
        - **Two board systems disagreed.** `Lineside` (the hand-laid routes' boards, the roof warning, note 260) posted every generated bend at √(0.4 R) and derailed the train at 1.55 × that (√(0.96 R)), instantly, unwarned, with its own figure: deepTerritory:2's "35 km/h" bend was a "25 km/h" one there, and it came off there first. On a generated line its boards now carry the plan's figure (floor √(aPost R)) on every bend that derails under sight.json `planPostBelow` (22), and the derailment is TrackRules' alone (`world.TrackPlan is null` gates Lineside's). A hand-laid route is unchanged.
    - **The train was quiet, not silent: the drone sat on top of it.** Heard as the app hears it (a NetPlaySession, the client world, default streaming decode), the cab at 18.7 m/s was mixing at −24 dBFS with `ui-music.drone` (−29) louder than the roll (−29…−31), the joints (−31) and the chuff (−36…−38): the recordings had been cut 17–24 dB (tools/audio/install.py `CUE_GAIN_DB`) to keep AudioTests' tells over them. Now: the roll +4 dB, the joints and the chuff +6, the rod clank +6 (install.py and the installed sounds both); the roll grows to full at 18 m/s, not 12 (`RollFullSpeed`), so cruise is heard as faster than a crawl; and the music tier's fader −14 dB, not −6 (mix.json). The same cab, same speed: roll −25, joints −25, chuff −30, drone −37, the mix −21. The joints are per axle every 12 m rail (unchanged): 6 cars at 17 m/s is a click-clack every 0.18 s or so, rising with speed.
        - `dt audio render --listener all` (chaos, 20 cars, 22 m/s), worst listener over the bed, before → after: train-whistle 6.3 → 6.2 (roof 10), hugger-grind 8.6 → 8.3, ribbit-swell 8.5 → 7.1, dragger-scrape 7.9 → 7.5, hound-howl 11.2 → 9.4, sleepers-writhe 9.8 → 8.1; the rest within 1.5 dB. All over the 6 dB floor; `AudioTests` green. Spectrograms: `out/audio/bed17-cab.png` (6 cars, 17 m/s, the cab: the joints are the vertical ticks), `out/audio/chaos-cab.png`, `out/audio/sound-warn-overspeed.png`.
    - **A bend taken too fast is warned, and commits only after a full lead** (train.json `overspeed`; `LineGen.TrackRules.Assess`). Plan §8.5's numbers stand: a bend derails above √(aDerail R) (1.0) and is boarded at floor √(aPost R) (0.7). No spec test pins either; the spec's derail numbers are unchanged.
