        - **The warning** (`BendStress.Warning`): a bend under the train, or ahead the way it's going within `WarnDistance` = v × `leadSeconds` (4) + (v² − v_derail²) / (2 × `brakeShare` (0.6) × the consist's rated brake, spec B.5) + `marginM` (30), that the speed now would derail it on. From the train and the line alone, every machine alike: nothing is sent, no wire change, prediction untouched (the derail is the host's).
        - **The stress** (`BendStress.Stress`): the bend under the train, 0 at its board's speed, 1 at its derailing speed. The bed's flange squeal from 0.35 of the derailing pull up to the board, then the creaks (`bed-groan.creak` up to three a second), the lurch (the slack running in down the train as it passes `lurchAt`, 0.5), and `state-derail.flange-scream` over its last half (it was the last 8% of the speed before).
        - **The cab's cue:** the communication bell over the driver (`warn-overspeed`, a new synth definition: a struck bell twice, 1.2 kHz with partials at 2.6 and 4.1 kHz, tier 3, so any tell ducks it) every `repeatSeconds` (1.5) while the warning's up; and the HUD (`Hud.BendWarningLines`, to whoever's in the cab): "TOO FAST FOR THE BEND AHEAD / 55 KM/H BEND IN 85 M, DERAILS OVER 71. YOU'RE AT 79: BRAKE" flashing red and amber; on the bend, "FLANGES SCREAMING: YOU'RE COMING OFF"; stress past half with no warning, "THE BEND IS PULLING HARD" in amber.
        - **No commit without it:** `TrackRules.Step` counts the warning's seconds on the host (`World.BendWarnSeconds`); a bend derails the train only once it has been up `leadSeconds`. Short of that the tick is spared and counted (`BendsSpared`, should be 0 at a held speed); each bend derailment records its lead (`BendCommits`). By construction a train surging over the speed on the bend gets its 4 s too.
        - **The director's risk:** the warning comes exactly as late as a driver can still brake through: run up to the bend, and on the bell, shut off and brake. `ADriverWhoBrakesOnTheWarningMakesTheBend` brakes 3.5 s after it goes up, 6 and 20 cars, at 1.4× each bend's derailing speed (capped at 22), and makes every bend.
        - `dt screenshot --hud --bend-warning [s]` stages it (`Staging.BendWarning`: s seconds short of the first bend that can derail the train, a quarter over its speed, in the cab; deepTerritory:2 unless `--route`). Read: `out/shots/hud-bend-ahead.png`; the map, `out/shots/cab-map-deep2-crop.png`.
    - **The Sleepers go** (the director, 6 Oct 2026: "it feels like the game is trying to force a tactical point of derailment on the game's behalf"). enemies.json `sleepers.enabled` (false): no Sleeper country on a generated line (no restricted zones for them, so no "R" boards and no Form 19 line for them; restricted zones the land makes, "blind", stay), none placed, and none run on any line (`World.EnableEnemies`). The code stays, for a mod. GDD §22 and App. B.2 say so, marked as the director's decision. What they did, step by step, before this:
        1. **Generation** (`LineBuilder.RestrictedZones`): "Sleeper country" restricted zones on the main line, `restrictedPerKm` (0.06) × the tier's `sleeperDensity` (frontier 2.0) per km, 700–1,800 m long, 30% empty, past the first 2 km and clear of facilities and junctions. Each its "R" board (yellow, the figure on the paper only), "END R", and a Form 19 line "Restricted speed 45 km/h … (Sleeper country)". The restricted speed is what a train can brake from to under 40 km/h within its sight (fog, the lamp's 120 m, the land) over the tier's margin: 13 m/s on frontier:7.
        2. **Placement** (`LayDirector`): 20 m patches inside those zones (frontier:7: 8 patches in 3 zones), a feature on the route (`PlanRoute`), a hazard enemy on the host (`World.EnableEnemies`).
        3. **Dormant**, until the engine is within 120 m with the forward lamp lit, or 60 m (braced) whatever the lamp. Then **TELEGRAPH**: the `sleepers-writhe` tell (tier 1, 400 Hz–2 kHz, from where they lie, every 1.6–3.8 s), the debris in the lamp's light (`DebrisKit`, inert), and the bots brake. In the real game no words: the only text cue was PrototypeSession's ("the lamp catches debris on the line ahead: brake"), not the app's.
        4. **COMMIT** when the engine reaches them, if the telegraph had run 1.5 s (App. A.1). Over 11.1 m/s (40 km/h): **derailed**, "ran onto the Sleepers at N km/h"; over 5.6 m/s: the engine loses 0.4 of its integrity; else 0.05. Gone.
        5. Build 1121's night: lamps out (a Climber, below), so 60 m at 12 m/s, 5 s of a writhe under the train's bed, then the cause card: the first time the game said "Sleepers" in words. That's the order the director saw: the derail, then the name.
        - For a mod that brings them back, fairer: they brace as far out as the train needs to brake under them (`braceLeadSeconds` 4 at its speed plus the same service stop, never under 60 m: 274 m for 6 cars at 20 m/s), and derail only after `derailLeadSeconds` (4) of telegraph, the heavy damage short of it. Measured (`AtCruiseWithTheLampDownADriverWhoBrakesOnTheirTelegraphGetsUnderTheirSpeed`, lamps down, 6 and 20 cars at 17 and 22 m/s, braking 4 s after the writhe): under 40 km/h at them every time.
    - **Why the lights were off** (`dt playthrough --route frontier:7 --bots 4`, 20 min, now reporting `lamps`): the spawn state is lit (the forward lamp and every car's), and no bot puts them out. The Climbers do (App. A.4 ENTER, note 183): one into each car nobody's in, its lamp out as it comes in, and at the engine it smashes the forward lamp for `lampOutSeconds` (45). Alone, every car is empty, so every Climber darkens one; and the forward lamp stayed dark after the 45 s until someone pressed [L], which nothing said to do. The GDD's §23 "lights fail" stands (the Climbers keep doing it); now the smashed forward lamp comes back lit once its glass is in, as the driver had it (train.json `kit.relightSmashedLamp`, on; one switched off stays off), and a player in a car whose lamp is out is told "THE CAR'S LAMP IS OUT: [K] LIGHT IT". Car lamps still start lit: whether they should start off (the Fire Flies' proposal) is the director's call.
    - **Playthroughs** (20 min each, frontier:7):
        - **Solo:** 0.24 km, 0 bend warnings, 0 derails, lamps untouched: the lone ride still never leaves the yard (as note 260 found; not this change).
        - **4 bots:** 17.1 km, 0 bend warnings, 0 derails (frontier:7 has no bend that can derail the train, and no Sleepers now). The forward lamp smashed by a Climber at 237 s and back lit at 282 s (45 s out); car lamps put out 8 times by Climbers, 4 of 6 lit at the end.
        - **deepTerritory:2, 4 bots** (it has bends that can derail a train): 11.0 km, 0 bend warnings, 0 derails: the bot drivers hold the boards, so the warning never had to go up. Car lamps put out 8 times (Climbers, car fires), none lit at the end: the bots don't relight them. The forward lamp stayed lit.
        - **Derails after the change: 0 in all three nights.** None came from anything but a bend, and none could have come unwarned: a bend derailment needs `leadSeconds` of the warning (`underLead` 0, `spared` 0 in each report).
    - **Not yet / the director's call:**
        - **frontier:7 has no bend to brake for.** frontier's `minRadius` is 300 m but this route's sharpest is 707 m; with the Sleepers gone a frontier night may have no derailment point at all. Whether the generator should lay some tighter bends on every frontier line (the "run up to it and brake" choice) is a design call.
        - **The Switchman still derails a train** (points thrown under it over 6.9 m/s): a creature acting on the train without boarding it, which the director is questioning. Untouched here.
        - The Stoker's runaway still derails on a bend through the same warning (its cause line unchanged).
        - The bell is a synth definition with no audio-checklist line for recorded candidates.
    - **Verified:** `BendWarningTests` (`EveryBendDerailOnAGeneratedLineIsWarnedAFullLeadAhead`: every bend that can derail a train on deepTerritory:2, deepTerritory:5, deadLines:3 and frontier:2, into it held at 1.2× and surging over it on the bend from 0.95×: each derails, once, after ≥ 4 s of warning, none spared at a held speed; `ADriverWhoBrakesOnTheWarningMakesTheBend` 6 and 20 cars; `ABendTakenAtItsBoardIsNoWarningAndItsStressRisesTowardItsDerailingSpeed`). `EnemyTests`: the Sleepers' brace distance and lead (`WithTheLampDownTheyStillBraceAsFarOutAsTheTrainNeedsToStop`, the cruise theory above). `SandTests`' Sleepers case brings them back as a mod would. All Sim tests and Game tests green; `AudioTests` green.
266. **The director's pacing by pressure, and a grace picked per night (GDD App. B.1 "Pressure" and "Pacing rules", spec B.10; design decision, 2026-10).** The tuning had a 20 s grace where the GDD said 90 s, and a forced spawn whenever the line had been quiet `paceSeconds` (18 s), cooldown or not: the harness's nights got a send every 40 s or so from the gate to the end, flat (frontier:7's director spawns per five minutes: 8, 9, 7, 9, 3, 6), and the director's own per-second tally was 80% "cooldown". The user's decision: the grace is a range, and spawns are algorithmic, driven by escalating pressures.
    - **The grace:** `graceMinSeconds` 20 to `graceMaxSeconds` 90, drawn from the night's seed on its own PCG stream (the director's draws are untouched), the draw above the minimum × `pressure.graceTierScale` (1 Local, 0.85 Frontier, 0.7 Dead Lines, 0.55 Deep Territory). `Director.Grace`. A generated line's grace stretch now gives way at the night's grace (`lineGraceSeconds` 0: no extra). "Later in a campaign" isn't a separate input: the campaign's tier steps with the consist (`campaign.json` `tiers`), so the tier scale is it; the director isn't told the campaign night.
    - **The pressure** (`Director.Build`, once a second after the grace and outside a ban, the terrain's ceiling or the final approach; starts at 4):
      `rate = tier × conditions × relief × busy × escalation × (base + quiet + loud + cargo)`, banked to at most 18.
      escalation = 1 + 3 × progress (along the line or toward dawn, whichever is further); base 0.03 /s; quiet + 0.1 /s × min(1, seconds since a threat was confronting the crew or sent ÷ 90); loud + 0.1 /s × min(1.5, Choir meter ÷ its threshold); cargo + 0.01 /s per car-load (load × cargo integrity × value: comet 2, livestock 1.5, food and medicine 1.3, ammunition 1.2); tier 0.8/1/1.2/1.4; conditions 1 + 0.15 lamp out + 0.1 × weather cold + 0.05 per deep-cold step + 0.05 rain + 0.05 × wind; relief (alive ÷ crew)² × (1 − 0.25 × share of the living under 35 health); busy 1 ÷ (1 + 0.5 × confronting threats × (1 − progress)).
      At the threshold 10 the director spends on what its weights pick (budget curve, caps, gates, saving up, pairs, variety, wants, the dead's votes within want tags: all unchanged); each spawn takes 3 × its cost off and restarts the quiet. At 16 (`pressAt`) the 25–45 s cooldown gives way and the curve may be overdrawn by `pacedCost` (3), as the paced spawn did; `DirectorSpawn.Paced` now means that. Condition-triggered charges (the Stoker) relieve it too.
    - **Ambiguities, read:** "since the last threat engaged" is `Director.Confronting` (telegraph, commit, grab, punish), not the caps' `Engaged`: a Climber pacing a car nobody walked into held the quiet at zero and the night's pressure flat (the first measured round). The dead's votes stay out of the pressure: D.11 says the vote never changes pacing. The cooldown was 30–60 s; with the relief making the trough it is 25–45 s, and the GDD's text says so.
    - **Observable:** the harness report's `threats.pressure` (beside the per-second `threats.director` tally, which keeps its shape and gains "building"): the night's grace and threshold, the director's own spawns per five minutes out on the line, and a sample every 30 s (time, km, pressure, rate, escalation, quiet, loud, cargo, relief, conditions, what it held for). `dt harness` prints it with the rest of the JSON.
    - **Measured** (`dt harness --route R --bots 8 --enemies --seconds 1800`, main e36be64 against this; one night each, deterministic per build):

      | | frontier:7 before | after | frontier:3 before | after |
      |---|---|---|---|---|
      | director spawns per 5 min | 8, 9, 7, 9, 3, 6 (42) | 4, 5, 4, 6, 3, 11 (33) | 7, 11, 6, 7, 8, 8 (47) | 4, 7, 4, 8, 9, 12 (44) |
      | beats per minute | 6.1 | 5.3 | 6.9 | 6.6 |
      | longest / mean quiet (s) | 19.9 / 10.0 | 27.4 / 9.4 | 19.5 / 8.6 | 30.1 / 11.1 |
      | quiet share | 0.24 | 0.22 | 0.25 | 0.37 |
      | deaths (run) | 1 | 0 | 0 | 1 |
      | cargo aboard at 1,800 s (of 4.5) | 4.50 at 21.2 km | 3.36 at 21.9 km | 4.48 at 23.0 km | 4.50 at 22.1 km |
      | car fires alight | 1 | 5 | 1 | 0 |

      frontier:7's 1,200–1,500 s window is the terrain at its ceiling (the line's own hardest stretch, 195 s held), pressure banked at 15 and spent after. The rounds before this tuning: with the busy damping on everything engaged, the night fell instead of rising (frontier:7 4, 6, 4, 4, 1, 1, cargo 2.96); with 2× escalation and a constant busy, flat (4, 7, 3, 8, 3, 7; frontier:3 lost all its cargo to 11 car fires from 5 Fire Fly swarms). frontier:7's cargo here went to fires from 15 Fire Fly swarms late in the night (the Fire Flies come on most where the night's busiest); single nights vary a lot on fires (note 188), and frontier:3 kept all of it.
    - **Not yet:** `content/tuning/balance.json`'s `meanQuietSeconds` 20 was left as it was; its `maxQuietSeconds` went 30 → 90 (the longest quiet spell), and `LinesideTests`' pace check with it (local:3, 3 bots, 600 s: longest quiet 39.3 s, two or more beats a minute). `dt balance --pairs` (insisted spawns, which bypass `Decide`) wasn't rerun. A campaign-night input to the grace, if the tier step isn't enough. Protocol unchanged (the director is the host's alone).
    - Tests: `PacingTests` (the grace's range, per seed and per tier; nothing before it; quiet, loudness, escalation and cargo raise it; a spawn relieves it; the crew down or hurt eases it; the same night the same trace; a director-only night rises toward its end with no gap over 3 min; the line's grace ban gives way at the night's grace); `EnemyTests.TheDirectorKeepsItsPacingRules` now checks every director spawn against the night's grace.

267. **Fire is a grid (the director's decision of 6 Oct 2026, GDD App. F.1: "Each car's surfaces (floor, walls, roof; never mid-air) are cut into large cells of 1–2 m. Fire spreads cell to cell, the extinguisher puts out the cell you aim at, and burnt cells char the textures").** A car fire was one number (`Extra`, 0–1) at one spot on the floor: it grew, it was knocked back by anyone holding Fire with an extinguisher within 2.5 m of that spot whichever way they faced, and it burned whoever was within 4 m of it along the car.
    - **The grid** (`Enemies/FireGrid.cs`). The cells are laid out from the car's interior box alone: floor and roof along × across, side walls along × up, at enemies.json `carFire.cellSize` 1.5 m. That's 72 cells in a cargo car. The host and every client have the same cells in the same order, so only heat and char go over the wire. Each cell's neighbours are those sharing an edge with it, on its own surface or round a corner onto the next. The end walls aren't cells: a fire leaves a car through its ends. Floor cells sit on the boards, `FloorPad` (0.1 m) above the interior box's floor. CarFrame pads the box below the boards, and TrainKit draws them there. `Hit` is the spray's ray against the room's floor, roof and side walls.
    - **The fire** (`CarFire`, Incidents.cs). Each cell has heat and fuel, and the car's `Vehicle.Char` is the fuel that's gone. A lit cell grows. Floor cells grow at the cargo's rate, since it's the load burning there; walls and roof are the car's boards. A cell burns `charPerSecond` × its heat of its own fuel and dies down once that's gone. A cell at `catchFrom` heats its neighbours, `climb` × as fast upward, so the walls and roof catch before the far end of the floor. Embers below `outBelow` don't grow on their own, and go out unless a neighbour is heating them: a sprayed cell doesn't creep back from a spark. This was found in a probe: one bot sprayed a 40 s fire down to a single 0.01 cell it wasn't aiming at, which regrew and jumped the coupling. Every tick reads the cells as they were at its start, in cell order, so it's deterministic. `Extra` is now the mean heat. It still drives the explosion (`explodeAt` 1 → 0.6: more than half the powder car well alight), cargo and car damage, the sound, the bots' "too far gone" and the HUD. It's alight (the commit) once any cell reaches `burnFrom`. `Local.Z` follows the heat-weighted middle, for the sound, the smoke and anything that still wants one spot. A fire that burns a car out goes out: the car is charred through.
    - **The extinguisher puts out the cell you aim at.** Each sprayer's look (`Bookmarks.Forward`, from `pick.eyeHeight`) is cast against the cells within `sprayReach` (2.5 → 3.5 m, since the old 2.5 m from the eye didn't reach the floor at a natural downward look). The cell hit cools by `sprayPerSecond` (0.06 on the whole fire → 0.35 on the cell), the cells round it by `sprayShare` of that, and the cell is wet for `dampSeconds`, catching from nothing round it. Aimed at no cell, the charge is wasted. `CarFire.Sprayed` lists each sprayer's cell this tick.
    - **Burns** come from the cells: the hottest within `burnReach` (4 → 1.6 m) of a burning cell's patch, measured from feet to `bodyHeight`, falling off with distance; the roof's at `ceilingBurnShare`. The by-the-second brush/ramp of note 263 is unchanged. Standing in it still kills in 8–15 s (`Build1121Tests`), and you're clear of it outside the burning cells, not 4 m from one spot.
    - **Jumping the coupling** is from a cell against an end wall at `spreadFrom` for `spreadSeconds` (30 → 15, now the fire has to walk to the end first). The cargo multipliers are unchanged. The fire takes the next car at its near end, once from each end.
    - **The wire** (protocol 30). A fire's record carries its cells' heat, and a car's record its cells' char, four bits a cell (`CarFire.Pack`, rounded up so a cell alight shows alight): 5 fields for 72 cells. Neither touches player or train motion, so prediction is unaffected. Char rides in `VehicleState` so a rejoin sees it.
    - **The bots** (`Fight` in Bots.cs) stand in the aisle 1.2 m short of the nearest burning cell, turn to look at it (yaw and pitch from the eye), and spray. They used to stand by the fire's one spot facing along the car.
    - **The art.** `GreyboxScene.FireGrids` draws each burning cell where it is (`Effects.FireCell`): tongues standing off the floor, licking up the walls from the lower part of each patch, rolling flat under the roof. A cell short of alight is a red glow in its boards. `Effects.CarFire` keeps the smoke, glow, cinders and light at the heart of it (`CellFlames`). Char is solid geometry (`Effects.Char`): a ragged patch per burnt cell, scorched brown to black, a little bigger than the cell so burnt cells run together. It isn't drawn as an effect because the blended effects only ever add light; a black effect quad drew nothing (checked against a red one). `Staging.Cells` gives `dt screenshot --threats --view fire` a fire that's been burning a while, with its char; `--smoulder` shows the char under a fire knocked back to one cell.
    - **Measured** (headless probe, not kept; frontier test train, 5 cars):
        - **Goods car, left alone:** alight at 25 s, the roof caught by 56 s, half the car by 70 s, the next car taken at 86 s, burnt out (72/72 cells charred) inside 400 s.
        - **Coal:** 18 / 45 / 55 / 71 s. **Chemicals:** 19 / 47 / 58 / 76 s.
        - **One walker with the car's extinguisher:** a fire found at 20 s is out in 4 s, using 6% of the charge; at 40 s, out in 7 s (19%). At 60 s (about 60% of the car alight) one extinguisher can't put it out: it takes more hands, or cutting the car loose.
        - `dt audit cascades --only car-fire` passes on seeds 1–3: out in 12–13 s, the car whole.
    - **Not yet / the director's call:**
        - The char is a flat-coloured patch, not a burnt version of the planking texture.
        - Cargo crates aren't cells: flames on the floor under the stack show through it.
        - The bots fight one cell at a time, nearest first. Two bots don't split a big fire between them.
        - `--spread f` (the staged fire's spread) no longer draws a creep. The grid shows the spread as it is.
    - **Verified:** `CarFireTests`, all green:
        - New: `TheSprayPutsOutTheCellItsAimedAtAndNotTheFireBehindYou`, `LeftAloneAFireClimbsTheWallsAndRunsAlongTheRoof` (every burning cell on a surface), `WhatBurnsChars` (char kept, replicated with the heat to a client), `ItJumpsTheCouplingFromAnEndWallIntoTheNextCarsNearEnd`, `CellsPackForTheWire`.
        - Changed: the hand-sprayed test now aims at the fire's cell.
        - Unchanged and passing: every bot fire-fighting test (from the next roof, the lockers, the aisle, the guard van), the powder explosion, `CargoTests`' coal and timber escalation, and `Build1121Tests`' burn by the second. That test's fire is now a 3 m blaze, and the brush steps out past its cells.
        - The Sim and Game suites; `dt screenshot --threats --view fire` (with and without `--smoulder`) looked at.

268. **The Track Doll escalates if ignored (the director's decision of 6 Oct 2026, later the same day; GDD App. F.1).** "She's no problem at first: she haunts, plays with and admires things in the car. Left alone, she moves on to the controls, and in the end she can let a standing train off its brake. It's a consequence of the crew's inattention and of not getting her off the train, never sudden." Before, a struck doll took the cab and worked the regulator and brake (full throttle, then off, then the brake on) the first time the cab had been empty 8 s, from her first second aboard; note 263's `tamperReleasesStandingBrake` (false) was all that kept a standing train on its brake.
    - **Neglect.** `TrackDoll` keeps a host-side neglect clock from the strike: a second a second while no living crewmate is in or on her car or within `attendRadius` (8 m) of her, and while someone is, it winds back at `attendedEase` (0.5 a second). Being minded is being near her: she still vanishes from anyone within 3 m, so minding her is keeping her company a few paces off, or following her round the cars. `TrackDoll.Level` turns the clock into her stage:
        1. **Stage 1** (from the strike): she haunts the cars, admires the cargo (its small cargo cost, as before) and giggles. She never goes to the cab, however long it's empty.
        2. **Stage 2** at `controlsAfter` (120 s) of neglect: the cab empty `tamperAfterEmpty` (8 s), she's at the controls, but only nudging the regulator up to `nudgeThrottle` (0.25) on one beat in three (`TrackDoll.Hands`). She never touches the brake, so a standing train on its brake stays put and one under way only gains a little.
        3. **Stage 3** at `releaseAfter` (300 s): the old tampering (the regulator open, shut, the brake on, in 3 s beats); and once she's been at the controls `releaseAfterAtControls` (12 s) at that stage on the visit, a standing train's held brake goes too (`Enemy.ReleasesStandingBrake`, which `World.Step`'s note-263 guard now asks). `finalStageReleasesBrake` (true) switches the exception off; `tamperReleasesStandingBrake` stays false and still keeps every other creature (the Stoker) off a standing brake.
    - **Telegraphs.** `warnSeconds` (30) before each stage she's restless (`TrackDoll.Restless`). Clients hear her giggle come twice as often (`GameAudio`), and restless at stage 2 she rattles the brake handle on the beat she'll take it, without moving it (`DollSounds`, the crew's brake-handle sound until she has her own); the prototype's threat line says "the doll's restless: she's eyeing the cab" / "get her off the train". Stage 2 itself (the regulator nudged, heard as the notch) is stage 3's telegraph too. So a standing train goes off its brake at the earliest 300 + 12 s into her being left alone, after two restless spells and at least 3 minutes of her at the regulator if the cab was empty for them.
    - **Replicated** in the existing fields, no wire change: `Extra2` is her escalation (1, 2 or 3, plus 0.5 restless; `TrackDoll.Escalation`, `Stage`), negative in a car and positive at the controls, so `Extra2 > 0.5` still means tampering to `CreatureArt` and the audio. `Extra` is cornered (in a car) as before, and in the cab her seconds at the controls at stage 3, so a predicting client knows the brake's going exactly when the host does (`ReleasesStandingBrake` reads only replicated state). `Cornered` now ignores `Extra` in the cab, and `CreatureArt` checks the cab before cowering.
    - **Getting her off the train ends it.** A toy, or cornered and clubbed, and she's gone for the run (unchanged); she's once a run, and a doll struck later would start at stage 1. **A haunting doll is never dismissed for want of company** (`World.Unmet`, T114's `lingerSeconds` 120 within 12 m): left alone she'd have gone at 120 s, which is exactly when she should start getting worse. She does hold a forward-zone cap slot while she haunts (she's engaged), as she did whenever someone was near her.
    - **Not yet / the director's call:** the numbers are first-pass guesses (2 and 5 minutes of neglect); "looking at her" doesn't count as minding, only being near; the doll has no recorded "restless" sound of her own (the faster giggle and the crew's brake handle stand in).
    - **Verified:** `TrackDollEscalationTests`: `AtFirstSheOnlyHauntsTheCarsEvenWithTheCabEmpty`, `MindedSheNeverEscalates` (company kept 360 s: stage 1 throughout), `IgnoredSheTakesTheCabAfterHerTelegraphAndOnlyNudgesTheRegulator`, `AStandingTrainLosesItsBrakeOnlyAtHerLastStageAfterTheTelegraphs` (restless ≥ 30 s before each stage, at the regulator at stage 2, and the train moves only ≥ 12 s into stage 3 at the controls), `WithTheDirectorsExceptionOffNotEvenHerLastStageTakesAStandingBrake`, `GettingHerOffTheTrainEndsItAndTheNextDollStartsOver`, `AClientSeesHerStageAndLetsTheBrakeOffAsTheHostDoes`. `DemoRosterTests.HitTheDollAndItHauntsTheTrainAndTakesAnEmptyCab` now waits out stage 1; `CreatureSoundTests.TheTrackDollWorksTheCabLeversWithTheirOwnSoundsUntilItHasOne` stages her last stage, and `RestlessAtTheRegulatorTheTrackDollRattlesTheBrakeHandleItHasntTakenYet` hears the stage-2 telegraph. No spec table lists the doll's numbers. All 881 Sim tests green (`NetcodeTests` among them).

269. **Per-creature boarding rules: Cinder Hounds that board stay aboard; Fire Flies come only to a stopped train (GDD App. F, the director's decisions of 6 Oct 2026).** The GDD's words: "Cinder Hounds that board stay aboard. They keep setting the car alight while they eat the supplies, which forces the crew to confront them." and "Car lamps start lit. Their pull on Fire Flies is rare, and only while the car is stopped." (Car lamps already started lit.) Notes 266–268 are left for work in flight.
    - **Cinder Hounds** (enemies.json `cinderHounds`, `CinderHound.Feed`): `stayAboard` (true) replaces v1.1's drop-off after `boredSeconds` with nobody within 12 m. Left alone aboard, each eats `cargoPerSecond` (0.002) of its car's cargo, and every `igniteEverySeconds` (20) it's been left alone it sets the car alight (App. C.5's fire, one to a car; put out, it starts another 20 s on). `Enemy.StaysAboard` exempts a boarded hound from the director's linger and stuck dismissals (`World.Unmet`, T114), so it holds its cap slot until it's dealt with: killed (the pack fight's blows) or its car cut loose. A hound still running behind is unchanged (outrun, the cannon). No C.9 record for a fire the hounds set: the table has no row for one, and the spec's tables stay as they are. `stayAboard: false` restores v1.1.
    - **Fire Flies** (enemies.json `fireFlies`, `Spawns`): the director sends them only while the train's under `stoppedBelow` (0.3 m/s, the "stopped" the Track Doll and Switchman use), at `stoppedWeight` (0.5) of their B.5 weight for "rare". **Spec reading:** App. A.5's break-off "the train pulls away at speed" now means getting under way, `pullAwaySpeed` 15 → 2 m/s (`pullAwaySeconds` 8 unchanged), so a swarm that hasn't ignited goes once the train's moving; B.5's gates and weights (dark sections, a lit lamp, ×2 at depth, ×0 lamps out) all stand. No spec test pins either number.
    - **Bots:** the walkers went *away* from boarded hounds to wait for the drop-off. With them staying, the first harness night had a pack sit on the rear car all night (`CinderHound:Commit` at the cap 1,000 samples, 58 car-fire beats). Now the fit (`Heed.PackFightHealth`, 55) go along the roofs at them and swing; the hurt keep clear. With `stayAboard` off they keep the old way.
    - **Harness** (`dt harness --route frontier:7 --bots 8 --enemies --seconds 600`), the old tuning vs this: sends 17 → 10, Fire Flies 9 → 2, car-fire beats 2 → 19, deaths 0 → 1 (mauled in the pack fight), fairness violations 0 → 0, no derail, 7.2 → 6.5 km, beats/min 5.8 → 6.4, longest quiet 19.4 → 14.1 s, quiet share 0.35 → 0.04; the director at the cap 0 → 148 of its decisions (the hounds aboard hold their slot until they're beaten). Max correction 0.41 → 0.24 m.
    - **Not yet / the director's call:** the rest of App. F's boarding rules (shut doors stopping some boarders, the Stoker's heat, the Track Doll's haunt) are other items. Whether a pack aboard should hold a cap slot all night, as it does now, is a pacing call.
    - **Verified:** `BoardingRuleTests` (hounds stay past every dismissal, eating and setting the car alight, start the fire only after 20 s alone, go with a cut car, v1.1's drop-off with the flag off; the director's Fire Flies weight null at speed and `stoppedWeight` stopped; a swarm goes once under way; left stopped, the car catches). `CarFireTests` restaged stopped (a walker's lamp, a driver getting under way); `EnemyTests.AGunnerWhoWaitsForRange…` aims at any running hound (the director's own pack came in its 90 s). All 881 Sim tests green, `NetcodeTests` included; Game tests 234/235 with `CrewAudioTests.ABotCrewIsHeardOverTheNetworkAsTheAppHearsIt` failing under load and passing alone.

271. **Stoker v3, the firebox half (the director's decision of 6 Oct 2026, GDD App. F.1).** The GDD reads: "In the firebox it's territorial: opening the door while it's in gets you a heavy burn, and a second kills. That's the mistake you learn from. The counter is to vent and starve the fire below a set heat; it then leaves the way it came, and a break of two to three minutes follows … A water hose through the open door kills it, at the cost of much of the fire. No chip damage." Note 263 already had the rest of v3: drawn by heat, boarding at the tender with a telegraph, driven off on the way in, and the 150 s break. In the firebox it still worked to App. A.5's old rule (open the door with a shovelful and club it, 12 burn a blow), and it ate the fire.
    - **Territorial** (`Stoker.Tick`, Interior.cs). The door opening on it (a shovelful, `Boiler.FireDoorOpen` rising) hurts whoever's nearest the door within `stoker.doorReach` (1.8 m): `doorBurn` 60 the first time, `doorKill` the second. Both are lethal-class hurts, kept per Stoker, so a fresh one starts over. It can't be clubbed in the firebox: `Reachable` and `MeleeRadius` hold only while it's boarding, so a crowbar does nothing and `burnPerBlow` is gone. That is the "no chip damage".
    - **Vent and starve.** While it's in, it still lurches heat in (`feedRate` ± `swing`), holds the safety valve shut and runs the regulator open (`Tamper`), so the boiler goes in about half a minute if left (`OnceInItEatsTheFireAndSwingsTheGaugeToARupture` unchanged). It no longer eats the fire (`eatPerSecond` 0.2 → 0), so the crew have to starve it. Under `starveFirebox` (2 of 6) it lets the valve go, takes its heat out (`BreakOff`) and goes back over the footplate to the coal over `boardSeconds` (`Extra2` 1 again, drawn there). It's then gone, and `World`'s break begins. The vent (12/s) outruns its heat and the fire's, so with the blow-off held and no coal it's gone before the boiler: 10–90 s in the test, 15 s for the bots.
    - **The hose** is an extinguisher held into the firebox (Fire, within `doorReach`) for `hoseSeconds` (2). The spray opens the door without the burn, kills it, and takes `hoseFireCost` (60%) of the fire. **Director's call:** the GDD says a water hose, and the cab has none, so the extinguisher stands in (one fetched from car 1: the engine has no room to mount one). A cab hose fitting (a slacking pipe off the injector) would be the real thing.
    - **Bots** (`FightStoker`). They club it while it's boarding, as before. Once it's in they never shovel or open the door: the vent held, the brake on, throttle shut, until it's starved out. The old "leave it if hurt" health gate went with the burns.
    - **Words:** the event hints are "it's in the firebox: don't open the door. Vent and starve it (no coal), or hose it" and "starved out: it's going back the way it came". The death line is "BURNED OPENING THE FIREBOX ON THE STOKER".
    - **Verified:**
        - `Build1121Tests.TheDoorOpenedOnItBurnsHeavilyAndTheSecondTimeKills`: swings do nothing; the first shovelful costs 60; the second kills.
        - `VentedAndStarvedItLeavesTheWayItCameAndNobodysHurt`: it goes back over the footplate, no rupture, nobody hurt, and the break holds.
        - `AHoseThroughTheDoorKillsItAndTakesMostOfTheFire`.
        - `CarFireTests.TheDriverVentsAndStarvesTheStokerOutBeforeTheBoilerGoes`: the conductor bot, at full health.
        - The rest of `Build1121Tests` and `CarFireTests`, and the Game `StokerTests`, `SafeYardTests` and `CreatureArtTests`, are green.
        - `dt audit cascades --only stoker` seeds 1–3: gone at 15 s, no rupture, no derailment.

273. **T128, the director's notes on build 1121: falls, where a grab takes you, a player left behind, and the forts (GDD App. F; queue #7).** Four notes, each reproduced or measured headless before it was changed. Built on #189's pressure director (note 266).
    - **"Far too easy to fall off the train."**
      - *Cause.* Nothing held you on a roof. A step with your centre past the roof's 1.5 m half-width put you in the air, and at speed the ground killed you (spec B.3). Walking at roof-run pace while you looked 30–50° aside for under a second, with the key held, was enough. The wind (note 201) pushed you sideways on top of that. Thrown-on-a-bend (note 260) and the Draggers were already warned or telegraphed, so they weren't the cause.
      - *Measured.* `FallTests`' careless walker: a wandering aim, a glance aside every 5–10 s with the key held, a correction only once the edge has been in view for half a second, half the cars at a run, the gaps jumped, wind 1, 16 m/s on R 500 m bends. Over 4 × 300 s it fell **42 times (14 deaths) before and 0 after**. The before figure is the same walker with `edge.lip` 0, measured in the same test. With glances allowed during gap jumps too, it was 54 before and 13 after, all 13 off short jumps.
      - *Fix* (`PlayerMotor.HoldAtEdge`, `player.json` `edge`). On a roof or a coupler plate, a walking step that would bring your feet within `lip` (0.3 m) of where the footing ends is held. Across the car and along it are held separately, so you slide along the edge.
        - You go over a side only if you mean to: walking at it within `stepOffDegrees` (30°) of straight out. The careless walker's deliberate steps went over 7 out of 7.
        - Off a roof's end only by a jump, a ladder, or a step down onto more of the train within `catchDrop` (3.5 m): a coupler plate, a platform, the tender. A car's door steps, a running board and the chassis don't count as a catch. That keeps the bots' step off the end onto the plate (note 231) working.
        - The wind brings you to the lip and no further (`windOverLip` false; true restores the old wind).
        - Jumps, the Draggers' `PullOff`, thrown-on-a-bend and every creature's knock put you in the air, so the hold never sees them.
      - *Spec reading.* Spec B.2's "roof run: wind and balance penalty" is kept as the wind's stronger push while running, which now ends at the lip. The flag records the other reading.
      - Stateless, from the intent and the car shapes alone, so `PlayerState` and the wire are unchanged and prediction re-simulates it exactly.
    - **"A creature carried the director up a mountainside."**
      - *Cause.* The Whistler is the only creature that carries a player away from the train. The Corrupted drag theirs along the cars, and the Gaunt carries bodies away over the ground already. The Whistler ran `nestDistance` (60 m) straight out from its gap at the gap's height. That is past Line Plan §12.6's 40 m walkable corridor (`train.json` `recovery.corridorM`), and the run went through the land. Where the land rose, the carried player was held underground, and when let go `UpdateSupport`'s "the earth, however far up it is" stood them on the hillside.
      - *Fix* (`Whistler.NestSite`). The nest is straight out across the line from the gap. It tries the side the player was taken from first, at `nestDistance` (now 30 m, inside the corridor), shortening by `nestStep` (4 m) to `nestMinDistance` (8 m). The way there must be runnable:
        - no 2 m of it steeper than `nestMaxSlope` (0.4, about 22°);
        - never more than `nestMaxRise` (6 m) above or below the rail;
        - no water (a generated line's `TerrainField.WaterNear`).
        If neither side has such a spot, the nest is at the formation's edge. The carry runs over the ground (`GroundAt` each tick).
      - The run's length goes in the replicated `Extra2`, so `CreatureArt` puts the nest down when the Whistler arrives.
      - `GrabDestinationTests`: on flat land the nest is 30 m out, on the ground. With a 1.5 rise-over-run mountainside on the taken side it goes to the level side, within ±6 m of the rail's height, and the player stands on the ground there. A gentle bank is fine; in a cutting with walls on both sides the nest is at the formation's edge.
    - **"A player left behind should feel the world close in … They needn't die at once."**
      - *Rule* (`Director.Abandoned`, `enemies.json` `director.abandoned`). Once a second, alongside the night's pressure and whatever holds that (the grace, a ban, the caps), each crewmate gets a pressure of their own when they are:
        - alive and on the ground;
        - more than `behindM` (150 m) along the line from the train's nearest end;
        - outside the forts.
      - *Rate.* It builds by tier × the night's conditions term (note 266's `Terms.Conditions`) × (`perSecond` 0.05 + `rampPerSecond` 0.15 × min(1, seconds left / 60) + `perKm` 0.3 × km behind).
      - *Hunts.* Past `threshold` (6) a Ribbit pack comes for them alone. Ribbits fit because App. A.6 has them hop in on whoever they outnumber on the ground, and they can be outrun. The pack is:
        - `pack` 2 the first time, one more each time to 5, and always larger than the group the player is in;
        - put down `spawnOut` 35 m away the first time, closer each time to 18 m, on the line's side the player is on, on the ground and out of the forts.
        Each hunt takes `relief` (6) off. While a pack is still after them, no other comes.
      - Hunts don't come from the budget or count against the caps, and they use their own dice stream (`0xAB4D0`), so the night's own draws are unchanged. Back aboard, back near the train, or in a fort, the pressure is gone. The roster gate holds: an edition without Ribbits sends nothing.
      - `LeftBehindPressureTests`: 400 m behind a train at 12 m/s, the first hunt comes 20–90 s in. A player who gets away from each pack 8 s after it arrives sees at least 4 hunts in 4 minutes, never smaller and never slower to come, and is still alive. The Ribbits sent target only them. Beside the train or aboard, there are no hunts and no pressure.
    - **"Forts must be safe spaces that monsters never enter (§9)."**
      - *Found.* Note 263's `World.SafeYard` holds only while the run is in `RunPhase.Yard`: the departure yard before the gate. It didn't cover:
        - the departure fortress once the train is through the gate, for a crewmate left behind there or a creature chasing them in;
        - the terminus. Its last 500 m is spawn-free (`noSpawnFinal`, plan `terminus_safe`), but anything already about, or riding in aboard the train, could follow the crew in.
        No other stop is a fort. Facilities are freight stops (§16–18), and the holdouts aren't forts.
      - *Fix* (`run.json` `forts`, `World.InFort` / `TrainInFort`). The forts are safe all night: the main line up to the outer gate (the run's yard), and from the terminus gate on (the plan's `Terminus.GateM`, or the run's terminus zone on a hand-laid line; a silent settlement only if its plan keeps the gate safe), out to `halfWidthM` (80 m) either side of the line. While any of the train is in one:
        - the director holds ("in a fort");
        - the Stoker isn't drawn.
        Any creature found inside a fort after the enemies step is dismissed: it lets go and is gone. This covers spawns put down inside one, creatures riding the train in, and creatures chasing a crewmate in. Hazards (the marsh, the Sleepers) and in-car incidents (car fires) aren't creatures and aren't touched.
      - `LeftBehindPressureTests`: the forts end at the gate, start again at the terminus, and stop 80 m out. A crewmate left behind 100 m inside the gate gets no hunts. A pack hopping in after a player at the gate never has a member inside and never grabs them. A train standing in the yard holds the director.
      - *Not done.* The Choir's meter isn't stilled at the terminus. The fort buildings still have no collision (T124).
    - Tuning changes:
      - `player.json`: new `edge` block.
      - `enemies.json`: `whistler.nestDistance` 60 → 30, plus `nestMinDistance`, `nestStep`, `nestMaxSlope` and `nestMaxRise`; new `director.abandoned`.
      - `run.json`: new `forts`.
      No spec table pins any of these, so `systems-spec.md` and `SpecTableTests` are unchanged.

276. **The engine, cab forward (the director's sketch of 6 Oct 2026: "the current front of train design that I want to change to the desired design where controls are at the front with full vis of the rail"; then "put a coal bunker in the cab").** GDD's open item ("a redesign of the front of the train … for a lone driver's view of track hazards. Every function stays") is done. Before, the boiler and stack led, the cab sat behind them with the cannon on its roof, and the coal tender followed. Now the order is reversed, as on the Southern Pacific's cab-forwards.
    - **The layout** (train.json `geometry.engine`, built by `CarShape.Engine`; `EnginePlan` is where everything reads it from, so nothing recomputes it).
        - Front to back: the pilot (`pilotLength` 0.6: buffer beam, plough, the headlamp on the cab's nose), then the cab (`cabLength` 5.0), then the boiler with its smokebox and stack at the rear, where car 1 couples on.
        - The unit is still 20 m (spec B.4), and every function stays.
    - **The cab.**
        - **Front, the driver's end:** the front windows either side of a narrow post; the driver's console under the right-hand one, with the regulator, brake and reverser; the whistle cord in that corner, hung against the side so it's out of the window. The levers still come back (+Z) towards the driver as they're worked, so `CabLevers`, `VrLevers` and the bots' driving are unchanged.
        - **Back, the fireman's end:** the firebox door in the back wall, where the boiler comes through. The coal bunker (`bunkerLength` 1.8, `bunkerDepth` 0.85, `bunkerHeight` 1.0, the director's "coal bunker in the cab") stands against the left wall ahead of the left doorway. Its gate is low in its inner side by the fire door, so the fireman's turn from coal to fire is the quarter turn it was.
        - **Roof:** the coaling hatch over the bunker, which a tower's spout pours through (`EnginePlan.CoalFromFront` replaces the tender's offset in `Run`, `StopCrew` and `dt`). The cannon stands at the roof's front, and the gun rail is the cab roof's.
        - **Ways in and up:** the doorways are at the back of each side. The running boards run from them back along the boiler to the rear deck, and a footplate off car 1's plate meets them (T90). A ladder climbs the cab's back wall from the boiler's top to its roof, and the hatch ladder goes up from the floor in the front left corner.
        - **Sandboxes:** on the boards over the drivers (`sandboxBehind` 2.5), since nothing is ahead of the cab now.
        - The tuning fields `tenderLength`, `tenderTop` and `tenderGangway` are gone. The coal store is still the boiler's `Tender` in the sim, and its capacity (spec B.5's 400 units) is unchanged.
    - **The driver's sight (T101, re-stated):** the line is in sight through the right-hand window from 8 m past the plough; with the boiler ahead it was 30 m. Spec B.7's "the stack masks the rail closer than ~60 m" is gone from it.
        - **Driver's gauges.** The backhead is now behind the driver. As on the real cab-forwards, a second set of the four gauges stands in front of them, two rows of two over the right-hand window beside the run map (`TrainKit.DriverGauge`). The scene turns both sets' needles.
        - **Backhead.** It's drawn in its own frame (`TrainKit.BackheadFrame`: the engine's turned round, +Z out into the cab), and so are everything on it, the shut fire door, the fire seen through the hole (`InFirebox`), the furnace's light and the firebox camera. A Stoker in the fire looks out of it at the cab ahead.
    - **Art** (`TrainKit.Engine`).
        - The drivers move back under the boiler with the cylinders ahead of them under the cab's back; the pilot truck is under the cab and a trailing truck under the smokebox. `Drivers` and `CrossheadRestZ` read the cab's back, so `SceneArt.Gear` turns them where they are.
        - The cab's sides have window posts. The smokebox door looks back down the train, with the tail lamp on its corner.
        - The headlamp is on the cab's nose under the windows: `World.LampHeight` is 2.0, where it was 2.8 high on the smokebox door.
        - The whistle and safety valves stand on the boiler just behind the cab (`WhistleZ`, `SafetyValveZ`; `Effects` plumes there).
        - The Stoker perches on the stack at the rear, facing forward along the boiler at the cab.
    - **Knock-ons in the sim.**
        - `PlayerMotor.SpawnInCab` used to put a crewmate at the cab's middle, which is now the bunker. A second crewmate spawned to the left (the harness's, the tests') was shoved out through the wall onto the ballast. `CabFloorZ` puts them on the open floor between the driver's console and the bunker, clear across the cab.
        - A ground switch stand's lever no longer counts from inside the cab (`SwitchStands.AtStand`). Cab forward, the cab can stand right over a toe, and getting down to the lever is the excursion the powered thrower saves (spec F.3).
        - The bots' loading give-up is 360 s; it was 300 (`StopDriver.LoadingGiveUp`, a bot's patience, in no spec table). The warm a cold hand goes back to is now 10 m further from the cars. A lone bot's crate stop already ran to the give-up on main (311 s) and now left a door open at it.
    - **Bots.** `ConductorBot.FiringSpot` is in front of the fire door, facing it (`FacingFire`). On the bunker's side, the way out of the cab goes down the middle of the cab past the bunker before turning out (`Route`, `StopDriver.GetDown`). The way out to car 1 (`Route`) runs through the left doorway, along the left board, over the rear deck and onto the plate. `SandWay`, `CabDoorZ` and the kit run read `EnginePlan`.
    - **Views.**
        - `fireman` looks back across the footplate at the fire door and the bunker.
        - `coaling` looks up at the bunker's hatch.
        - `gangway` looks along the boiler from the plate.
        - New `engine`: the whole engine three-quarters on.

277. **The art checklist's open rows to L1 (C1, the art session; the director: "keep getting L1s in there").** Eight rows had nothing of their own drawn, or only part. Each is drawn now, with a `dt` path to look at it and a test.
    - **The boiler rupture** (spec B.6, GDD §23 "loud, spectacular, run-changing"). The sim had it (the drag down to coasting, the fire and pressure to nothing) and the audio, but nothing was drawn.
        - **The burst** (`Effects.Rupture`): a flash, then a white cloud blown out of a seam torn in the boiler's left flank (`TrainKit.RuptureSeam`, between the first two straps behind the cab, seen from the left doorway and the line). A hard core of steam stands out of the tear. The jet is checked by the air and laid back down the train by its going, full for 4 s, then a hiss that thins over half a minute. Scale and grit fly from the tear. In the cab, the fire is blown back out of the firehole in steam, ash and embers.
        - **The tear** (`TrainKit.RuptureTear`, drawn while ruptured): a ragged black hole in a scorch of soot, a lip of raw bright iron, the plate peeled back in rusted petals, and burst copper tubes curling out.
        - **The seized engine:** the stack dead (a last grey wisp, no cinders), no whistle, valve or cylinder-cock steam. While she's still dragging down to `ruptureCoastBelow` the drivers are locked (`GreyboxScene.DriversLocked`: the rods stop turning and pick up where they stopped once she's coasting). Each tyre slides in a stream of sparks over a glow on the rail. The cylinder cocks' steam now comes from the cylinders (it was at the old front).
        - `dt screenshot --view rupture --ruptured s` (0.8: the blast; 30: the hiss; the train dragged down from `--speed` as the sim does), and `--view fireman` for the cab.
    - **Noisy toys** (App. C.4, C.7). Three models in `tools/models/recipes/train_stores.py`: `toy_squeaker` (a papier-mâché pig on a pleated bellows), `toy_musicbox` (walnut, lid up, the comb and cylinder under glass, a dancer on her spindle, the key) and `toy_drummer` (a tin drummer boy, sticks up mid-beat, the key in his back). `SceneArt.ToyModel` draws a toy by its `Body.Noise`; quiet ones keep the bear, horse and doll by id.
    - **The headlamp out** (train-lights: "lit and dark must both read"). Switched off or smashed (`World.LampShining` false), the beam and the halo go (`Effects.Train` `lamp`), and dark glass with a crack star covers the lit lens (`SceneArt.HeadlampOut`). The greybox lamp box is on the cab's nose at `World.LampHeight`. `dt screenshot --headlamp-out`.
    - **Lockers that don't block each other** (App. F.1). The open door swings 105°, standing out into the aisle; at 150° it lay back across the next locker's face. `LockerArtTests` pins it standing out more than three times as far as it leans back.
    - **Bend limits on the route card** (the line plan: "speed ticks on derailment bends that show the max speed"). `LineGen.PostedSpeeds` is the cab map's list of posted stretches, now shared. The route card's profile rules each stretch red along its foot with its km/h over it: B for brass, W for a weak bridge, the slower winning where two would overprint. Its key reads "IN RED: KM/H OR OFF". `dt screenshot --hud --route deepTerritory:2 --card`.
    - **Smaller prompts** (App. F.1: "they're good but they are too big"). The prompt is fine print a hand's breadth under the crosshair (`Hud.PromptDrop`), on a thin iron strip rather than a riveted plate, its hold bar along its foot. The canvas is 480×270 and its 5×7 font is already its smallest, so the overlay now draws text and keycaps at fractional scale (`Overlay.Text(..., float scale)`, `UiStyle.Keyed(..., scale)`). The scale is half the HUD's where that still gives a font pixel two screen pixels (1080p up) and whole steps bigger below (`Hud.PromptScaleAt`: the app passes the renderer's height over the canvas's, the VR panel 2). `dt screenshot --hud` now renders at `--scale` with the canvas laid over it, as the app does; it used to render at 480×270 and enlarge, so fine print showed as mush.
    - **A lighter HUD out of the cab** (hud-look: "too much UI ... not enough in world or embodied"). Off the engine, a steam-drive train's panel is gone: the driver's gauges are in the cab, the supplies view is on I, and a rupture or a bend too fast still takes the screen's middle. `HudTests` pins it.
    - **The overspeed telegraph, seen** (App. F.1: "cars straining and leaning, flanges grinding, sparks"). `BendStrain.PerCar` works each car's strain on the bend under it as `TrackRules.Assess` works the train's (0 at the board's speed, 1 at derailing), and which rail it's crowding. `Effects.Flanges` grinds sparks off each truck's leading wheel on the outer rail, more and hotter toward 1; a glow on the rail and an orange light on the ballast from 0.35; hot-iron haze past halfway. A rider on a straining car is juddered (`BendStrain.Offset`, the eye only). Presentation only, from the train and the line. `dt screenshot --view flanges --strain 0.9`.
    - **The crew in the wreck, caught mid-task** (App. F.2 take 4: "arms-up and awkward, not people mid-task"). `FilmPlayer.Task` (the host reads it from what they were at: the gun's seat, a body in their hands, the cab's controls as the driver, else the fire) starts each body in its work's pose (`WreckFilm.TaskPose`: on the throttle, bent into the shovel, sat with the knees either side of the breech, leaned back under a load). Each pose is laid bone by bone at the skeleton's lengths, and its muscles hold it for `film.taskHold` (0.4 s of the recording) before they go over to the brace and the hands go out. The gunner's hands and knees are clear of the gun, so they still go up and over it. `dt screenshot --view poses --wreck-poses --dawn 0.8`.
    - **Not yet / the director's call:**
        - A carried crate isn't in the ragdoll's hands in the film; the film has no loose bodies.
        - The cars don't lean on a bend: the frames are the sim's, and a visual roll that moved the riders' eyes but not the riders was left out.
        - No in-world toy staging for `dt screenshot`; the toys are reviewed on `dt art show prop-toy_*`.
        - The tear isn't left as a patch after a mend.
    - **Verified:** `EffectsTests` (`TheBoilerTearsInItsFlankAndTheBurstBlowsOutOfItThenHisses`, `ABendTakenTooFastGrindsSparksOffTheOuterRailHarderTheNearerItIsToOff`), `WreckDeathTests.EachGoesIntoTheWreckFromWhatTheyWereDoing` (and the gunner's test unchanged), `LockerArtTests`, `HudTests`; every view above looked at.
